# Website redesign: status

Last updated: 2026-10-21 (WS01, step 25: gold text off light grounds, with a guard; step 24: status colours, greys and raw hex on the tokens; step 23: the ink and grounds on the tokens; step 22: ThemeScope and the route-to-look map; step 21: tokens, dark mode, focus ring and motion; step 20 opened checkpoint D; checkpoint C, after step 16, step 17 and step 18, is merged)

The brief is `docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md`. This file records, for each workstream, where it stands, what was decided, where the work differs from the brief, what is left to do, and every `verify:` item that has been checked. It is updated in every redesign PR.

## Ordering rules

1. **The branch is pushed only after the preview database slice is committed.** Every pushed branch gets a Cloudflare Pages preview. Before `[env.preview]` existed in `wrangler.toml`, that preview was bound to the production database `lca-db`, so anyone clicking around it would have written real registrations and accounts. `redesign/ws01-design-system` stays local until the commit that adds the preview database. Before that first push, K also checks the Preview environment's secrets in the Pages dashboard and removes any live Stripe or Resend key: previews existed before this slice and may already hold the live ones.
2. **K migrates the preview database from the branch before reviewing each checkpoint's preview.** He runs `npm run db:migrate:preview` from the branch, with his own Cloudflare credentials, before he looks at the preview, not after the merge. Merging to `main` migrates production only (`.github/workflows/migrate-db.yml`); nothing migrates `lca-db-preview` automatically. Once the branch adds tables the code reads (the sections and schedules tables), a preview bound to an unmigrated database shows errors.
3. **No other branch touches `functions/utils` while checkpoint C is open.** Steps 16 to 19 move files; step 16 moved twenty server helpers into area folders under `functions/utils`. A change to `functions/utils` made on another branch in the meantime would conflict on every moved file, and an import it adds by an old path would break the build after the merge. Step 17 moved nine site library files from `src/lib` into `src/areas`, and the same holds for them: an import another branch adds by an old `src/lib` path breaks the build after the merge. Step 19 moves the domain components the same way: `src/components/tournaments`, `family`, `admin`, `governance`, `maps` and `uscf`, and the two top-level files `src/components/AdminAnnouncementPanel.tsx` and `src/components/RegistrationReminderButton.tsx`, now sit under `src/areas/<area>/components`. A component import another branch adds by one of those old paths breaks the build after the merge too. Until checkpoint C merges, changes to server helpers, the site library and those component folders and files wait or go on `redesign/ws01-design-system`.

## WS01: Design system, themes and accessibility foundation

**Status:** in progress. Built in 32 small steps on one branch and merged in four checkpoints (A: steps 1 to 8, B: 9 to 15, C: 16 to 19, D: 20 to 32).
**Branch:** `redesign/ws01-design-system`, pushed once the preview database step was committed (ordering rule 1).
**PR:** checkpoint A was pull request 105, B was 104 and C was 107, all merged into `main`; checkpoint D (steps 20 to 32) is draft pull request 108.

### What each acceptance criterion and decision is proved by

AC1 to AC14 are the brief's WS01 acceptance criteria. K1a to K6 are the parts of K's October 8 structural decisions. T1 is a step of the plan that neither names: moving the existing pages onto the tokens so they follow dark mode and the looks. T2 is its second half. Each item is proved in one step. "Planned" means that step has not been built yet; the entry is replaced with the passing test, or the recorded measurement, when it lands. Step 32 checks that every row ends with a passing test or a recorded result.

### Step 1: preview database

- `wrangler.toml` gains `[env.preview]`: `DB` bound to `lca-db-preview` (`28e13174-3a06-47a7-ab0f-b4ac7f24af6f`, region ENAM, created by K on October 8), all ten `[vars]` keys repeated with production's values, and the `CLUB_LOGOS` bucket repeated. The production binding is unchanged.
- `scripts/db/preview-guard.ts` reads `wrangler.toml` (with `smol-toml`, pinned at 1.8.0) and stops a preview command with a plain message when the preview id is missing or a placeholder, when it is production's id, when the preview block names `lca-db`, or when the command is aimed at `lca-db` or at a database the preview block does not bind.
- New npm scripts, each running the guard first and stopping if it fails: `db:migrate:preview`, `db:migrate:status:preview`, `db:seed:preview`. The seed script refused until `scripts/seed/fixtures.sql` existed; step 20 wrote it.
- `README.md` has a new "Safe previews" section.
- Nothing in a development container or CI targets the preview database. Only K runs the preview scripts.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 406, up from 326 with 80 new tests in `test/unit/wrangler-config.test.ts` and `test/unit/preview-database.test.ts`; integration 267). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches.

### Step 2: switches and component test setup

- `src/lib/features.ts` gains the 15 Phase 0 switches from brief 0.2 (`newLook`, `themeHeritage`, `themeScholastic`, `newNav`, `siteSearch`, `homeSearch`, `eventStrip`, `eventStripLive`, `liveMarker`, `newHome`, `homeResults`, `homeChampions`, `homeLive`, `homeWeek`, `homeRecap`), every one off, each with a short comment saying what it turns on. `clubTournaments`, `tournamentQuickFilters` and `externalTags` are unchanged. Nothing reads the new switches yet, so nothing visible changes.
- `vitest.config.ts` gains the `@` alias for `src/` (as in `vite.config.ts`), picks up `test/unit/**/*.test.tsx`, and compiles test JSX with `@vitejs/plugin-react`, the same as the site build. Tests still run in node by default; a component test asks for a browser-like DOM with a `// @vitest-environment jsdom` comment at the top of its own file, so the pairing engine, scanner and other node tests are unaffected.
- New development packages, each pinned to an exact version at least two weeks old: `jsdom` 29.1.1 (April 30), `@testing-library/react` 16.3.3 (August 27), and `@testing-library/dom` 10.4.2 (September 13), which `@testing-library/react` needs alongside it. jsdom 30 is not used because it needs Node 22.22.2 or later; this container runs 22.22.0 and CI asks only for Node 22. No runtime dependency changes.
- New tests: `test/unit/features.test.ts` (every Phase 0 switch exists, is a boolean and is off, which is the starting state, so turning one on means taking its name out of the test's list (the comment in `src/lib/features.ts` says so); the three earlier switches still exist as booleans; the object holds at least these 18, so later switches can be added without editing the test; the file runs in node with no DOM), `test/unit/render-smoke.test.tsx` (runs in jsdom, renders today's `StatusBadge` through `@/components/StatusBadge` without importing React, and checks its words and tone classes) and `test/unit/test-setup.test.ts` (the switch names match brief 0.2 and each switch has a comment; the test configuration keeps node as the default and has the `@` alias, the `.tsx` pattern and the React plugin; only `.tsx` files ask for jsdom; the test packages are exact-pinned development packages; and this record names the switches, counts, verify items and decisions in plain words). Later component tests follow the smoke test's pattern, including `afterEach(cleanup)`, because the test globals are off and Testing Library does not clean up on its own without them.
- This step proves no acceptance criterion or decision by itself; it sets up the switches and the component tests that later steps use.
- Brief version 1.3 (merged from `main` into this branch before checkpoint A merged) added five Phase 1 switches to the table in section 0.2: `homeFeatured`, `newTournaments`, `newEventPage`, `festivals` and `householdCheckout`. They are in `src/lib/features.ts` too, every one off, so the switch list in the code and the table in the brief stay the same list; `test-setup.test.ts` now expects 20 switches and reads a workstream column that carries a note ("WS05 festivals").
- `test/unit/preview-database.test.ts` checked that only AC9 was filled in the table above, which was true after step 1. It now checks that every row names its step (1 to 32) and what proves it, that K1d says "partial: ratchet", and that AC9 keeps its step 1 entry.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 477, up from 406 with 71 new tests: 20 in `features.test.ts`, 3 in `render-smoke.test.tsx` and 48 in `test-setup.test.ts`; integration 267, unchanged). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches.

### Step 3: shared `domain/` folder and formats

- New folder `domain/` at the repo root for pure code that the site, the server functions and the workers all use: no database access, no DOM, no Workers types and no React. This step creates `domain/format` only; the other areas (events, registration, households, membership, clubs, live, contracts) are created by the steps that fill them.
- `domain/format` holds `formatScore` (3.5 is "3½", 0.5 is "½", 0 is "0", 10 is "10"), `formatDate` ("Sat, Oct 24", or "Sat, Oct 24, 2026" with the year option), `formatTime` ("7:00 PM", with "12:00 PM" at noon and "12:00 AM" at midnight) and `formatTimeControl` ("G/90+30" becomes "G/90+30 · 90 min each + 30 sec per move"; "G/30;d5" adds "30 min each + 5 sec delay"; "G/60" adds "60 min each"; text it does not recognise comes back unchanged). `describeTimeControl` gives the plain words on their own.
- How values are read: a bare stored date or datetime-local value ("2026-10-24", "2026-10-24T19:00") is Louisiana wall-clock time and is shown as written; a Date, epoch milliseconds or an ISO string with a zone is an instant and is shown in Central time. Columns filled by SQLite's `datetime('now')` (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `sent_at`, `recorded_at` and the like) look like wall-clock text ("2026-10-24 02:30:00") but hold UTC, as the admin audit log, board seats and board inbox already assume by appending `Z`; `formatDate` and `formatTime` read them as UTC when passed `{ stored: 'utc' }` (that example is "Fri, Oct 23" at "9:30 PM"), and the `date.ts` header says those columns must be passed that way. The default stays wall-clock because announcement start and end times are stored as Louisiana time in the same text form. Weekday and month names, and the "7:00 PM" text, are built from fixed tables rather than by `Intl`, so the text is identical in browsers, Node and Workers (newer locale data puts a narrow no-break space before "PM"). A string a helper cannot read is returned as given, so a bad value shows up rather than vanishing.
- `src/lib/format.ts` is one line, `export * from '@domain/format'`, so the brief's path works. Nothing in the site uses the helpers yet, so nothing visible changes and no switch is needed; pages adopt them behind `newLook` in later steps.
- Wiring: `tsconfig.app.json` gains the `@domain/*` path and includes `domain`; `vite.config.ts` and `vitest.config.ts` gain the matching `@domain` alias; `tsconfig.functions.json` now includes `functions`, `domain` and `workers/daily-emails/src`; `workers/daily-emails/tsconfig.json` includes `../../domain`. The server functions and the workers import `domain/` by relative path, as the worker already does with `functions/utils`.
- Two checks keep `domain/` pure. ESLint has a block for `domain/**` that forbids importing `@/`, any path into `src/` or `functions/`, React and `cloudflare:` modules, and forbids `window`, `document`, `localStorage` and `navigator`. The type checks are the second guard: the site's check compiles `domain/` with the DOM library and without the Workers types, and `npm run typecheck:functions` compiles it with the Workers types and without the DOM, so code that leans on either one fails one of them. Each guard was tried with a planted file: `window` fails `typecheck:functions`, `D1Database` fails the site's check, and an import from `src/` fails the boundary test and lint.
- The lint fixture `test/unit/fixtures/lint/domain-violations.ts` breaks every rule on purpose. `eslint.config.js` ignores its folder, so `npm run lint` never counts it; the test lints its text as if it were a file in `domain/`, with the real config.
- Bundling: `npx wrangler pages functions build` succeeds before and after the change. With a temporary handler under `functions/api/` importing `../../domain/format`, the Pages Functions build bundled all five format files, and `wrangler deploy --dry-run` of a temporary daily-emails entry importing `../../../domain/format` did the same. Both temporary files were removed.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 578, up from 477 with 101 new tests: 52 in `test/unit/format.test.ts`, 30 in `test/unit/format-edges.test.ts` and 19 in `test/unit/domain-boundaries.test.ts`; integration 274, up from 267 with 7 in `test/integration/domain-format.test.ts`, which runs the helpers inside the Workers runtime). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches.

### Step 4: one definition for shared rules

- The rules the site and the server each kept a copy of now live once in `domain/`: section eligibility in `domain/events/sectionRules.ts`, the Central wall-clock conversion in `domain/format/centralTime.ts`, entry pricing in `domain/registration/pricing.ts`, club regions in `domain/clubs/regions.ts` and the family size (3 children) in `domain/households/family.ts`. Each old file (`functions/utils/sectionRules.ts`, `time.ts`, `pricing.ts`, `regions.ts`; `src/lib/sectionRules.ts`, `lcaTime.ts`, `pricing.ts`, `regions.ts`, `family.ts`) is now one `export *` line, by relative path on the server and through `@domain/` in the site, so no importer changed. The daily-emails worker still imports `functions/utils/time`. `functions/utils/family.ts` keeps `listChildren`, `canActFor` and `syncFamilyCoverage`, which use the database, and imports and re-exports the family size. `src/lib/family.ts` stays as a re-export for good.
- Behaviour is unchanged. The rule bodies moved word for word; only `centralTime.ts` changed inside. It takes the zone from `domain/format/date.ts` (`LCA_TIME_ZONE` is now `CENTRAL_TIME_ZONE`, so `'America/Chicago'` is written once in `domain/`) and reads a moment's Central clock through the same `zonedParts` the date formats use, so there is one place that asks `Intl` for Central time. Its results were compared with the old file's for about 80,000 inputs (wall-clock, date-only and zoned values every 97 minutes from 2025 to 2027, across every clock change) and for unreadable values, and every one matched.
- New `domain/membership/tiers.ts` holds the membership prices (adult $15, scholastic $5, family $25, senior $10) that were written into `functions/api/membership/checkout.ts`. Checkout now imports them, and `isMembershipTier` checks the tier, so names inherited from every object (such as `toString`) are refused with the same 400 as any other unknown tier. `src/pages/MembershipPage.tsx` shows the same constants and writes "up to 3 children" from the family size, so the page reads exactly as before.
- New `domain/households/publicName.ts`: `publicName({ fullName, hasActiveGuardianLink, entryMarkedMinor }, context)`. On the entrants list (`'entrants'`) a player with an active guardian link, or whose entry is marked under 18, shows as first name and last initial ("Leo R."); in `'results'` (pairings, standings, results, winners and recaps) the full name is always shown (D3). Being in a household is not the trigger by itself. A single name is left as it is; a hyphenated surname gives its first letter ("Maya F."); a surname that starts with a particle such as "de la", "Le", "Van" or "St." is kept together ("Ana D.", "Remy L."); a middle name or initial and Jr., Sr. or a numeral are skipped ("Leo James Robichaux" is "Leo R."); "Robichaux, Leo" reads as "Leo R.". Nothing calls it yet: the inputs it needs (guardian links and the under-18 mark on an entry) arrive with the household work in WS06.
- `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` also runs on changes to `domain/**`, since the worker now reaches `domain/format/centralTime.ts` through `functions/utils/time.ts`.
- Bundling: `npx wrangler deploy --dry-run --outdir <scratch>/w --config workers/daily-emails/wrangler.toml` bundles (31.96 KiB, with `domain/format/centralTime.ts` and `date.ts` inside), and `npx wrangler pages functions build --outdir <scratch>/fn2` compiles, with the seven `domain/` files the functions reach.
- Nothing visible changes, so no switch is needed.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 662, up from 578; integration 311, up from 274). Builder tests: `mirrors.test.ts` and its 5 tests are gone, `test/unit/domain-shims.test.ts` adds 42 and `test/unit/public-name.test.ts` 14, `domain-boundaries.test.ts` checks the 7 new `domain/` files and `format-edges.test.ts` the new format file. Tester tests: `test/unit/central-time-legacy.test.ts` compares `lcaTimeToMs` with a copy of the old implementation across the 2026 and 2027 clock changes; `test/unit/domain-wiring.test.ts` checks the workflow triggers, the worker's import of the time shim, the removal of `mirrors.test.ts` and this section; `test/unit/public-name-edges.test.ts` covers the name rule's edge shapes; `test/integration/domain-shared-rules.test.ts` runs the time, pricing, section, region and family rules through the old server paths in the Workers runtime; `test/integration/membership-tiers.test.ts` checks that checkout charges each tier's price and refuses inherited names such as `toString` with a 400. The pricing, section rules, Central time and renewal expiry tests are unchanged and pass. `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches.

### Step 5: event-mode phases

- New `domain/events/eventMode.ts`, pure code with no database, DOM, host clock or host time zone. It exports `EventPhase` and `EVENT_PHASES` (the nine phases the brief names: `week`, `dayBefore`, `checkin`, `eventDay`, `roundPosted`, `roundInProgress`, `between`, `final`, `after`), `eventPhase(input, now)`, `phaseKey(eventId, phase, round)`, and three helpers for the WS02 endpoint and the client's window gate: `eventEndMs`, `eventModeWindow` and `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule`.
- The input is a plain object, `{ date, endDate, status, firstRoundTimes, lastRoundResultsComplete }`. `firstRoundTimes` is keyed by the event day ("YYYY-MM-DD") with that day's first `round_schedule` time ("HH:MM") or null. A null, blank or unreadable value means the day has a round whose time is not set yet. A day with no key has no round (an off day), provided at least one day of the event has a key; with no key inside the event at all, every day counts as not set. `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(round_schedule, date, end_date)` builds it from the stored schedule (earliest time per day; a day with no round has no key only when every round is dated inside the event, otherwise each such day is null).
- Every boundary is a Louisiana wall-clock time turned into an instant with `lcaTimeToMs` (`domain/format/centralTime.ts`); the Central calendar day of `now` comes from `zonedParts` (`domain/format/date.ts`). Calendar days are counted with `Date.UTC`, so no local-time method is used. `eventEnd` is 11:59 PM on `end_date ?? date`, and the 48 hours of `after` are real elapsed time: across the November change the window closes at 10:59 PM, and across the March change at 12:59 AM.
- `phaseKey` is `${eventId}:${phase}:${round ?? 0}`, the brief's `key`. `week` and `dayBefore` come back on off days between playing days, and `checkin` and `eventDay` on each playing day, so the caller passes a round for them: the first round of the next playing day for `week` and `dayBefore` (1 before day 1), the first round of that day for `checkin` and `eventDay`. Without it, a strip hidden on the day before day 1 would stay hidden on the day before round 2. The doc comment says so and a test shows the two keys differ.
- `roundPosted`, `roundInProgress` and `between` are in the type but never returned; the doc comment says they wait for WS07's round publications and live state, when `checkin` will end at the round 1 publication and `eventDay` will stop being produced.
- Where the brief leaves a moment without a phase, the rule is under Decisions ("Event-mode phase gaps"). Where the module goes against the brief's text (`eventDay` past 11:59 PM, and `after` from 6:00 AM for an event that is not final), the rule is under Deviations ("Event-mode phase times"). The module's doc comment lists both as going beyond the brief.
- Nothing reads the module yet, so nothing visible changes and no switch is needed. WS02's endpoint, WS03's hero and WS13's event-day view import it.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 841 in 37 files, up from 662: 179 new, 104 in `test/unit/eventMode.test.ts`, 74 in `test/unit/eventMode-edges.test.ts` and 1 more in `domain-boundaries.test.ts` for the new file; integration 311, unchanged). Builder tests: `test/unit/eventMode.test.ts` covers every Phase 0 phase at its boundaries, midnight, both 2026 clock changes, multi-day events, days with no round, schedules with undated rounds or rounds dated outside the event, `final`, `after`, the host time zone, the key format, and that the key for the day before day 1 differs from the key for an off day before round 2 once the round is passed. Tester tests: `test/unit/eventMode-edges.test.ts` covers events that start on the clock-change Sundays, weeks that cross a clock change, the year end, the leap day, a three-day event, round times at 6:00 AM and midnight, empty and stale data, the key's three parts, and that the module imports only from `domain/format`. `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches.

### Step 6: Drizzle schema and migration generator

- Dependencies, pinned exactly: `drizzle-orm` 0.45.3 (runtime), `drizzle-kit` 0.31.11 and `@libsql/client` 0.17.4 (development only; drizzle-kit needs it to read a local SQLite file). `@libsql/client` installed through the proxy with its native Linux package, so the `better-sqlite3` fallback was not needed. The lockfile gained 79 packages and removed none; four existing entries (`@cloudflare/workers-types`, `@types/node`, `undici-types`, `ws`) lost their `dev` flag, and `@libsql/client`, `libsql` and their dependencies are marked `devOptional` rather than `dev`, because drizzle-orm lists them as optional peers. CI uses plain `npm ci`, so nothing installs differently.
- `splitSql` moved from `test/integration/setup.ts` to `test/shared/splitSql.ts` (no `cloudflare:test`, no `import.meta.glob`); `setup.ts` imports it and still re-exports it for `members-rebuild.test.ts`. It now tracks depth: `CASE` opens a block anywhere, `BEGIN` opens one inside `CREATE TRIGGER`, `END` closes one, and a statement ends only at a `;` at depth 0. It also copies double-quoted and backtick identifiers whole, as it already did strings. All 52 migrations give the same statements as before.
- `test/unit/helpers/sqlite.ts` opens `node:sqlite`, runs `PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON` before the first migration, and applies `migrations/*.sql` in filename order through `splitSql`. `scripts/db/build-local-sqlite.ts` (`npm run db:local-sqlite`) uses it to write `.drizzle/introspect.sqlite` (gitignored): 52 migrations, 29 tables.
- `drizzle.config.ts`: dialect `sqlite`, schema `functions/db/schema.ts`, output in the staging folder `drizzle/` (never `migrations/`), and `tablesFilter` leaving out `d1_migrations`, `_cf_*` and `sqlite_*`. `drizzle-kit pull` read the local file; its schema and relations became `functions/db/schema.ts` and `functions/db/relations.ts`, reviewed by hand (see Deviations, "Drizzle baseline"). `drizzle/meta` (the journal and the baseline snapshot) is kept; the pulled baseline SQL was deleted and nothing was copied into `migrations/`.
- `functions/db/client.ts` exports `getDb(d1)`, which is `drizzle(d1, { schema })` with the tables and relations together. No runtime file imported `functions/db` at the end of step 6; from step 9 the admin tournament endpoints do.
- `drizzle-kit generate` right after the pull, with the reviewed schema:

  ```

  Reading config file '/home/user/lca-website/drizzle.config.ts'
  ```

- `scripts/db/generate.ts` (`npm run db:generate -- <name>`, with `--custom` for hand-written migrations) runs drizzle-kit, numbers its file after the highest prefix in `migrations/` (`0052` next), adds a header comment and writes it to `migrations/`, keeping the new snapshot in `drizzle/meta`. It refuses a file containing `DROP TABLE`, `__new_` or `PRAGMA foreign_keys`, and a column added with `REFERENCES` whose `ON DELETE` or `ON UPDATE` action drizzle-kit left out; it then writes nothing, restores `drizzle/` and points at `migrations/README.md`. Tried by hand on real schema changes (a new table, a rebuild of `tournaments`, a column with a cascading foreign key, a custom data migration) and every file and snapshot removed afterwards.
- `test/unit/migration-safety.test.ts` reads drizzle-kit's style: names bare, in backticks or double quotes everywhere (`CREATE TABLE`, `REFERENCES`, `INSERT INTO`, `UPDATE`, `DROP TABLE`), `ON UPDATE` before `ON DELETE`, and `ALTER TABLE ... ADD` with or without `COLUMN`. It reports exactly what it reported before (0019, 0033 and 0040 on `members`, 14 delete actions). Fixtures from drizzle-kit 0.31.11 are in `test/unit/fixtures/drizzle-sql/`: a new table with a cascading foreign key, a column added with `REFERENCES ... ON DELETE cascade`, and the rebuild of `tournaments` (``DROP TABLE `tournaments` ``).
- `migrations/README.md` documents the generate flow and says triggers, CHECK constraints and refused changes stay hand-written.
- Nothing visible changes and no runtime query changes, so no switch is needed.

Tester tests: `test/unit/db-generate-real-kit.test.ts` (runs the real drizzle-kit on throwaway copies), `drizzle-baseline.test.ts`, `split-sql-edges.test.ts`, more `migration-safety.test.ts` cases and `test/integration/drizzle-d1-schema.test.ts` (relations, foreign keys, defaults and the members role trigger through Drizzle on Miniflare D1). The tester found no defect. `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 7: request and response contracts

- `zod` 4.6.5 (published September 13, 2026) is a runtime dependency, pinned exactly. The root `node_modules/zod` moves from 3.25.76 to 4.6.5; `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` and `shadcn`, which need zod 3, each get their own nested 3.25.76 copy in the lockfile. Nothing else in the lockfile changed.
- New `domain/contracts/`: `common.ts` (ids, calendar days, stored timestamps, money in dollars, the 0-or-1 flags, the `{ error }` body and the `{ error, fields }` body), `events.ts` (the tournament list row and its parts: status, registration status, pairing system, keep-apart, sections and prizes), `registry.ts` and `index.ts`. zod is imported only here.
- `registry.ts` maps a route named as file routing names it under `functions/api` (`'tournaments'`, `'tournaments/[id]'`, `'admin/tournaments'`) to `{ request?, query?, response }` per method, for the methods the file exports. OPTIONS is never listed. The first entry is `tournaments` GET.
- Response schemas are strict objects: a column added to `tournaments` fails the list contract test until `events.ts` names it, which is the point (step 15's removal of `member_discount` updates it). Request schemas are to be loose objects with optional fields where the handler treats them as optional, so nothing the site sends today is rejected. Responses are not checked at runtime.
- `functions/utils/response.ts` gains `parseBody(request, schema)`: the parsed body, or a 400 with the existing `{ error }` plus `fields` (one plain message per field, keyed by its path, `body` for the whole body). A body that is not JSON keeps the message the handlers already use, "Invalid JSON body". The file imports only zod's types and calls the schema's own `safeParse`, so zod enters the functions bundle only with a handler that imports a contract. No handler uses `parseBody` yet; each moves over when a step touches it.
- `test/integration/harness.ts` gains `expectContract(result, schema)`: it parses the answer, prints zod's issues when it fails, also fails when the parsed copy differs from what was sent (a field the schema does not name), and returns the typed body.
- `collectRoutes` moved from `test/unit/route-audit.test.ts` to `test/unit/routes.ts` unchanged (it still walks all of `functions/`), next to `collectRouteFiles` and `exportedMethods`. The route audit runs the same 78 tests as before.
- `test/unit/contract-coverage.test.ts` scans `functions/api` and the handler files beside it (not `utils/` or `db/`), and reads each file's `onRequest<Method>` exports (declared or listed in an `export { }`; a bare `onRequest` is refused). 88 handler files under `functions/api` export 124 methods besides OPTIONS: GET 47, POST 40, DELETE 18, PATCH 16, PUT 3. One has a contract; the other 123 are in `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts`, whose count is pinned in the file. Its synthetic cases write a new handler file to a temporary folder. The three page routes are excluded because they serve HTML: `functions/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/clubs/[id].ts` and `functions/news/[slug].ts` (see Deviations). Five pending lines do not answer with JSON (three images, the CSV member export and the Stripe webhook) and are marked in the file (see Follow-ups).
- `test/unit/domain-boundaries.test.ts`: the per-file scan now also fails when a `domain/` file outside `domain/contracts` imports zod (`zod` or `zod/...`, by import, re-export or `import()`) or imports `domain/contracts` other than with `import type`, and a new check fails when any file in `src/` imports zod or imports `domain/contracts` other than with `import type`. Each rule has a case showing it fires.
- The site build has no zod in it (no `safeParse` or `ZodError` in `dist/assets`), and `npx wrangler pages functions build` compiles with no zod in the bundle. `workers/daily-emails` typechecks with `domain/contracts` included.
- Nothing visible changes, so no switch is needed.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1131 in 47 files, up from 1071 in 43; integration 377 in 38 files, up from 325 in 32; after review and fixes). The tester added `routes.test.ts`, `contracts-layer.test.ts`, `contracts-status.test.ts`, `handler-exports.test.ts`, `parse-body-edges.test.ts` and two more `tournaments-list` files, and fixed `exportedMethods` so a commented-out export no longer counts as a handler. `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 8: sections and schedules tables

- `functions/db/schema.ts` gains three tables and two columns, nothing else changes:
  - `tournament_sections`: `id` (text, default `lower(hex(randomblob(8)))`), `tournament_id` (`ON DELETE CASCADE`), `position` (default 0), `name`, `fee_regular`, `fee_early`, `fee_late` (real, null), `cap` (integer, null), `prize_fund` (text), `rating_min`, `rating_max`, `grade_min`, `grade_max` (integer), `unrated_ok` (integer, null when the JSON does not say), `rules_set` (default 0), `prizes_json`, `extra_json`, `archived_at`, `created_at`. A null `fee_regular` means the tournament's `entry_fee`; a null `fee_early` or `fee_late` means that tier is worked out when read from the tournament's own `early_deadline`, `early_discount`, `late_after` and `late_fee`. No tier price is stored as a copy of a tournament column.
  - `tournament_schedules`: `id`, `tournament_id` (cascade), `position`, `label` (default 'Main schedule'), `time_control` (null means the tournament's own), `is_primary` (default 0), `merge_round`, `archived_at`, `created_at`.
  - `tournament_schedule_rounds`: `schedule_id` (cascade), `round`, `date`, `time`, primary key (`schedule_id`, `round`). Date and time are kept as the JSON wrote them, blank strings included.
  - `registrations.section_id` and `registrations.schedule_id`: nullable, `REFERENCES` with no `ON DELETE`, added with `ALTER TABLE ... ADD`; `registrations` is not rebuilt.
  - Indexes: unique `idx_tournament_sections_live_name` on (`tournament_id`, `name`) `WHERE archived_at IS NULL`; unique `idx_tournament_schedules_primary` on `tournament_id` `WHERE is_primary = 1 AND archived_at IS NULL`; plain indexes on both tables' `tournament_id` and on `registrations.section_id` and `schedule_id`, so a cascade or a foreign key check does not scan the table.
- `npm run db:generate -- sections schedules` wrote `migrations/0052_sections_schedules.sql` and `drizzle/meta/0001_snapshot.json`. drizzle-kit 0.31.11 wrote everything itself, the two partial unique indexes and the expression defaults included, so nothing in 0052 is hand-written and its two `ALTER TABLE ... ADD ... REFERENCES` lines need no delete action. `npm run db:generate -- sections schedules backfill --custom` then wrote the header of `0053_sections_schedules_backfill.sql`, plus `drizzle/meta/0002_snapshot.json` (the same schema as 0001) and journal entry 2. Afterwards `drizzle-kit generate` says:

  ```
  32 tables
  registrations 14 columns 4 indexes 4 fks
  tournament_schedule_rounds 4 columns 0 indexes 1 fks
  tournament_schedules 9 columns 2 indexes 1 fks
  tournament_sections 19 columns 2 indexes 1 fks
  No schema changes, nothing to migrate 😴
  ```
- `0053_sections_schedules_backfill.sql` is hand-written: four triggers (`CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS`), created first, then the backfill.
  - `tournaments_sections_sync_update`, `AFTER UPDATE OF sections, round_schedule ON tournaments`: archives live rows whose names left a valid array; brings back the latest archived row of a name that returns; adds a row for each new name (`INSERT OR IGNORE` against the partial unique index, the first of a repeated name winning); copies the JSON-shaped columns of each live row by name (`position`, `fee_regular` from a numeric `entryFee`, `prize_fund`, the rating and grade limits, `unrated_ok`, `rules_set`, `prizes_json`, and `extra_json` for any other key or a known key of an unexpected type); makes sure there is one live primary schedule; when `round_schedule` changed and is null or a valid array, replaces the primary rounds (a repeated round keeps its first entry), and fills a primary schedule that has no rounds; a `round_schedule` of JSON `'null'` (what the admin PATCH stores for `roundSchedule: null`) clears the rounds like SQL NULL and `[]`, matching the readers, which see no rounds; then fills any null `section_id` and `schedule_id` of the tournament's entries. It never writes `cap`, `fee_early`, `fee_late` or an id.
  - `tournaments_sections_sync_insert`, `AFTER INSERT ON tournaments`: sets `sections` to the value just inserted, which runs the update trigger for the new row, so the sync is written once.
  - `registrations_fill_section_insert`: a new entry without `section_id` gets the section of its name, and one without `schedule_id` the primary schedule.
  - `registrations_fill_section_update`, `AFTER UPDATE OF section ... WHEN NEW.section IS NOT OLD.section AND NEW.section_id IS OLD.section_id`: `section_id` follows the new name; a writer that sets `section_id` in the same update wins.
  - "The section of its name" is the live row of that name, else the most recently archived one; when there is none, an archived row is created, so `section_id` is never left null.
  - Every `json_each` reads its column through `json_valid` and `json_type = 'array'`; malformed JSON yields no rows, archives nothing and replaces no rounds. A plain string element is a name; an element without a text name, or with an empty one, is skipped. Names are matched exactly as stored, with no trimming, as the code matches them with `===`.
  - The backfill: `UPDATE tournaments SET sections = sections` runs the sync once for every tournament (no value in the row changes), then an archived row is added for each name a game uses that has none. Running the file again changes nothing.
- The JSON columns stay and stay authoritative. No reader changed: no runtime file reads the new tables, and none imports `functions/db`.
- `functions/db/relations.ts` gains the relations for the new foreign keys (`tournaments.tournamentSections` and `tournamentSchedules`, `tournamentSchedules.rounds`, and `registrations.sectionRow` and `scheduleRow`, named so because `registrations.section` is the name column), which `test/integration/drizzle-d1-schema.test.ts` requires for every foreign key. The schema header lists the new triggers among what it does not model. `migrations/README.md` names the 0053 triggers and the next number.
- Tests: `test/unit/migration-0053-backfill.test.ts` (23 cases) and `test/integration/sections-triggers.test.ts` (10 cases, including a refused POST and PATCH by a plain member that change no rows); edge cases in `test/unit/migration-0052-schema.test.ts` (15), `test/unit/migration-0053-edges.test.ts` (28), `test/integration/sections-triggers-edges.test.ts` (20) and `test/unit/redesign-status-k2a.test.ts` (7); `migration-safety.test.ts` gains two cases (the three new cascade children, and a later rebuild of `tournaments` would be flagged for them). Counts pinned by earlier steps moved with the new files: `schema-drift.test.ts` (32 tables, 313 columns), `split-sql.test.ts` (the step 6 comparison stays on the 52 files it was written for; 0052 and 0053 split into 11 and 6 statements with each trigger whole), `split-sql-edges.test.ts` (six triggers), `drizzle-baseline.test.ts` (54 files, 32 tables, the six triggers and no view), `db-generate-guard.test.ts` and `db-generate-real-kit.test.ts` (0054 is next; three journal entries).
- `npx wrangler d1 migrations apply lca-db --local` applied 0001 to 0053 to the local database. Nothing was run against a remote database.
- Nothing visible changes, so no switch is needed.
- **What K runs on production, read-only, before the checkpoint A merge** (`npx wrangler d1 execute lca-db --remote --command "<query>"`). Each answer is recorded here; none blocks the merge, but each row they return is a section the backfill will archive, skip or keep as found:

  ```sql
  -- 1. sections or round_schedule that is not a JSON array (each is read as no sections, or no rounds)
  SELECT id, name, substr(sections, 1, 80) AS sections FROM tournaments WHERE CASE WHEN json_valid(sections) THEN json_type(sections) <> 'array' ELSE 1 END;
  SELECT id, name, substr(round_schedule, 1, 80) AS round_schedule FROM tournaments WHERE round_schedule IS NOT NULL AND CASE WHEN json_valid(round_schedule) THEN json_type(round_schedule) <> 'array' ELSE 1 END;
  -- 2. section names given twice, missing, empty or with spaces at either end (the first of a repeated name wins; nameless ones are skipped)
  SELECT t.id, CASE e.type WHEN 'text' THEN e.value WHEN 'object' THEN json_extract(e.value, '$.name') END AS section, COUNT(*) AS times FROM tournaments t, json_each(CASE WHEN json_valid(t.sections) THEN CASE WHEN json_type(t.sections) = 'array' THEN t.sections END END) e GROUP BY t.id, section HAVING COUNT(*) > 1 OR section IS NULL OR section = '' OR section <> trim(section);
  -- 3. entries and games naming a section the JSON does not have (each name gets an archived row)
  SELECT u.tournament_id, u.section, u.source, COUNT(*) AS rows FROM (SELECT tournament_id, section, 'entry' AS source FROM registrations UNION ALL SELECT tournament_id, section, 'game' FROM tournament_games) u JOIN tournaments t ON t.id = u.tournament_id WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM json_each(CASE WHEN json_valid(t.sections) THEN CASE WHEN json_type(t.sections) = 'array' THEN t.sections END END) e WHERE CASE e.type WHEN 'text' THEN e.value WHEN 'object' THEN json_extract(e.value, '$.name') END = u.section) GROUP BY u.tournament_id, u.section, u.source;
  -- 4. entries whose tournament is gone (they cannot get a section row; expected 0)
  SELECT COUNT(*) AS entries_without_tournament FROM registrations r WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM tournaments t WHERE t.id = r.tournament_id);
  -- 5. an entryFee that is not a number (kept in extra_json; the section then charges the tournament's entry_fee in the table)
  SELECT t.id, json_extract(e.value, '$.name') AS section, json_extract(e.value, '$.entryFee') AS entry_fee FROM tournaments t, json_each(CASE WHEN json_valid(t.sections) THEN CASE WHEN json_type(t.sections) = 'array' THEN t.sections END END) e WHERE e.type = 'object' AND json_type(e.value, '$.entryFee') NOT IN ('integer', 'real', 'null');
  ```
- **On merge:** `.github/workflows/migrate-db.yml` applies 0052 and 0053 to production (`lca-db --remote`) when checkpoint A merges to `main`. Both only add: three tables, two nullable columns, indexes and triggers, and the backfill writes only the new tables and columns (its `UPDATE tournaments SET sections = sections` rewrites each row with its own value), so the code already deployed keeps working. K migrates the preview database separately (`npm run db:migrate:preview`, ordering rule 2).
- **What K runs on production after the merge, and again before checkpoint B merges** (all expected 0):

  ```sql
  SELECT COUNT(*) FROM registrations WHERE section_id IS NULL;
  SELECT COUNT(*) FROM registrations WHERE schedule_id IS NULL;
  SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tournaments t WHERE (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM tournament_schedules p WHERE p.tournament_id = t.id AND p.is_primary = 1 AND p.archived_at IS NULL) <> 1;
  SELECT COUNT(*) FROM registrations r JOIN tournament_sections s ON s.id = r.section_id WHERE s.name <> r.section OR s.tournament_id <> r.tournament_id;
  ```
  The queries were checked on a local database built from the migrations with rows of every kind above. Results on production, Monday, October 12, 2026 (read-only, after 0052 and 0053 were applied): all four returned 0. Production then held 1 tournament (a test event, status active), 15 entries, 3 live section rows and no archived ones, and the six triggers `members_role_insert`, `members_role_update`, `registrations_fill_section_insert`, `registrations_fill_section_update`, `tournaments_sections_sync_insert` and `tournaments_sections_sync_update`.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1207 in 51 files, up from 1131 in 47; integration 407 in 40 files, up from 377 in 38). `drizzle-kit generate` reports no changes. `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 9: one writer for sections

- `domain/events/sections.ts` (no zod): `LegacySection` (an element of the JSON), `SectionInput` (what a save takes: the legacy keys plus an optional `id`, `cap` and `fees { early, late }`), `SectionRecord` (a row read back); `normalizeLegacySections` (column text or a parsed value; a plain string is a name, an element without a text name is skipped, the first of a repeated name wins, nothing trimmed, as the 0053 trigger reads it); `toLegacySection` (the JSON element: the keys the admin sent (known keys first, then any others, as zod returns them, so the stored text can reorder keys; contents are unchanged), without `id`, `cap`, `fees` or undefined values; a bare name stays a bare name); `SectionListItem` and `asSectionInput` (a list element a request sends is a section or a bare name, which is the section `{ name }`); the known keys of `LegacySection` are typed `unknown`, because events saved earlier can hold one of another type; `repeatedSectionName`, `sectionListProblem` and the decision 7 messages; `tierFees(section, tournament)` (regular is `fee_regular` else the tournament's `entry_fee`; early and late are the section's own price, else regular less `early_discount` when `early_deadline` is set and regular plus `late_fee` when `late_after` is set, as checkout prices them; null when the event has no such price).
- `domain/contracts/events.ts`: `sectionSchema` (a section in a request: name 1 to 80 characters and not blank, `cap` a positive whole number or null, the rule and prize fields, optional `id` and `fees`, other keys kept; a known key whose value is of its column's type must be valid for the column, and a value of another type is taken as it is and kept in `extra_json`, as the 0053 trigger keeps it), `sectionListItemSchema` (a list element: a section, or a bare name of 1 to 80 characters, so the manage page can send back a stored list of names with the sections it adds), `savedSectionSchema` (a section in an answer), `adminTournamentSchema` (every `tournaments` column, sections from the table) and its response, and the loose request schemas for the admin create and edit. `registry.ts` adds `'admin/tournaments'` POST and `'admin/tournaments/[id]'` PATCH; `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` drops both lines and `PENDING_COUNT` goes from 123 to 121.
- `functions/utils/events/sectionsRepo.ts`, the first runtime use of `getDb`:
  - `loadSections(db, tournamentId, { includeArchived })` (live rows by position, then archived ones, latest first) and `loadSectionsFor(db, ids)` (90 ids per `IN` list, under D1's 100 bound parameters).
  - `buildSaveSections(db, tournamentId, input, { reportSettings })` returns the queries for one `db.batch`, or the reason in words. `input` can mix sections and bare names; a bare name is the section `{ name }` and stays a bare name in the JSON. It matches by id (an id that is not this event's is refused), else the live row of that name, else the latest archived row of that name (which comes back), else a new row with an id made in code (16 hex characters from `crypto.randomUUID`). Order: archive the sections left out; give every live row being renamed a temporary name (longer than any allowed name and holding its id); give each section its final name, position and fields, or insert it; move entries of renamed rows by `section_id`, then games and the `report_settings.sections` key by old name (games in two steps, so a swap does not mix them); write `tournaments.sections` last. Leaving `cap` or a fee out keeps the row's value; null clears it.
  - `columnsFromLegacy` works out the row's columns from the JSON element exactly as the 0053 trigger does (a known key only when its type is the expected one, the rest in `extra_json`), so the trigger finds nothing to change.
  - Decision 7: a section left out that has entries that are not withdrawn (waitlisted ones count), or games, refuses the save with its message; nothing is written.
  - Guards: the plan is made from rows read before the batch, so the batch opens with guard statements that fail it as a whole when the plan has gone stale. One compares the rows as read (id, name and `archived_at` of each, by id) with the table as it is when the batch runs; the others check, in lists cut like the reads, that no section being archived has gained an entry that is not withdrawn or a game. A guard raises an error only when its condition holds (`json_extract` with a malformed path, selected from the tournament row), and the error rolls the whole batch back. `isSectionsConflict` recognises that error and the live-name index refusing a name another save just took. A guard is a select builder, because Drizzle's D1 batch cannot bind the parameters of a raw `db.run(sql)` query.
  - `runBatch`, `saveSections` (plan and run alone) and `sectionResponse` (a row priced with `tierFees` for an answer).
- `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id].ts` PATCH: reads the body with `parseBody` and the new contract, loads the tournament and writes its columns through Drizzle, and puts that `UPDATE` (which no longer writes `sections`) and the writer's queries in one `db.batch`. Role checks are unchanged and come before any write: `requireTournamentManager`, then only an LCA admin may change the organizing club. `sections: null` or no `sections` leaves the sections alone. When the batch fails because the sections changed under it (a guard, or another save taking a name), nothing is written, the tournament's own columns included, and it answers 409 with `{ error: "These sections changed while you were saving. Reload the page and try again." }`; any other failure is rethrown. It answers every column with the live sections from the table.
- `functions/api/admin/tournaments.ts` POST: `parseBody` with the new contract; the `INSERT` (sections left to the column default, `'[]'`) and the writer's queries in one `db.batch`, with the same 409 for a conflict; it answers every column with the sections from the table, ids included. The wizard reads only `id`. The handlers keep their own messages for missing fields and an unknown status.
- `test/integration/factories.ts`: `seedTournament` inserts the row without sections and then saves them through the writer (plain string sections are names).
- The 0053 triggers stay. The update trigger fires on the writer's JSON write and changes nothing (proved by running each plan in two batches); the entry trigger fires when a rename moves `registrations.section` and resolves to the same row. When old code writes only the JSON (the deploy window), the trigger still makes the rows follow.
- The site is unchanged: today's setup form sends no ids, so its sections match by name.
- Nothing visible changes apart from the refusals (repeated names, removing a section with entries or games), which are data rules and come with no switch.
- Tests: `test/unit/sections-writer-audit.test.ts` (5), `test/unit/sections-domain.test.ts` (27), `test/integration/sections-writer.test.ts` (42, among them a plan gone stale under another save, a late entry and a late game on a section being removed, and a PATCH whose rows change just before its batch, answered 409 with nothing written) and `test/integration/contracts/admin-tournaments.test.ts` (16, among them a PATCH that sends back a stored list of bare names and an old element with its price as text, with a section added). `schema-drift.test.ts` loses its import check (one case), and `sections-triggers.test.ts` and `sections-triggers-edges.test.ts` follow decision 7 and the new POST answer (see Deviations). `tournament-create.test.ts` and `tournament-edit.test.ts` pass unchanged, including the `toEqual` on the parsed JSON at `tournament-edit.test.ts:122`.
- The Pages Functions bundle builds (`npx wrangler pages functions build`) and now carries zod, through the two handlers' contracts; the site build has none. Nothing was run against a remote database.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1239 in 53 files, up from 1207 in 51; integration 465 in 42 files, up from 407 in 40). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Step 10: player registration reads the section rows

- `domain/registration/pricing.ts` gains `priceEntry(section, tournament, nowMs, { isLcaMember })`, with no storage in it. `section` is a section as `loadSections` returns it (`feeRegular`, `feeEarly`, `feeLate`), and `tournament` carries `entry_fee`, `early_deadline`, `early_discount`, `late_after`, `late_fee` and, until step 15, `member_discount`, taken from the tournament row the request reads, so changing one of those columns changes the next price at once. It answers the same `{ amount, base, lines }` as before, with the same labels ("Early entry discount", "LCA member discount", "Late entry fee") and the same sum: the base (`fee_regular`, else `entry_fee`), less the early discount while the early deadline has not passed, less the member discount for an active member, plus the late fee after the late date, rounded to the cent and never below zero, with lines only when the base is above zero. A section's own `fee_early` or `fee_late` (none exist until WS08) replaces the worked-out line under the same label, its amount being the difference from the base. `entryPrice(t, sectionName, opts)` stays for the tournament page and the family entry panel until step 13: it reads the legacy JSON through `normalizeLegacySections` and hands the section to `priceEntry`. `PricedTournament` keeps `sections`; the new `PriceTournament` is the same without it.
- `functions/utils/events/sectionsRepo.ts` gains `sectionWithRules(record)`, a section row in the form `eligibilityProblem` takes; a null column reads as a key left out, as the JSON element did.
- `functions/api/registrations.ts` POST reads the body with `parseBody` and the new contract, finds the section among the live rows (`loadSections`), checks the entry rules on the row and prices with `priceEntry`. The three `INSERT INTO registrations` (waitlist, free, paid) are Drizzle inserts that set `section_id`; the free and paid ones share one builder, so the two paths cannot drift, and each shares a `db.batch` with its payment row, also a Drizzle insert. `rating_at_entry` is still read inside the insert. `parseSections` and `parseSectionNames` are gone.
- `functions/api/registrations/batch.ts` POST: `parseBody`; the section rows are read once per request and every player is checked and priced from them; every registration and payment row is a Drizzle insert, run together through `runBatch`, and every registration sets its `section_id`. `parseSections` is gone.
- `functions/api/registrations/[id].ts` PATCH: `parseBody`; the tournament is read through Drizzle without `sections`. A section change looks for a live row of the asked name (the entry's current section, found by `section_id` and else by name, may be archived), sets `section` and `section_id` together, and reprices a pending payment from the two rows' `fee_regular` (else `entry_fee`), as `sectionFee` did. The entry update and the repriced payment are written in one batch. `parseSectionList` and `sectionFee` are gone.
- Archived sections are never offered: a new entry, a family entry or a move into an archived section is 400 in the `{ error }` shape ("Invalid section", or "Choose a valid section for" and the player's name in a family entry), and nothing is written.
- `domain/contracts/registration.ts` holds the request contracts (loose, written from the bodies `createRegistration`, `createBatchRegistration` and `updateRegistration` in `src/lib/api.ts` send) and the response contracts (strict: the entry with every `registrations` column, the waitlist place, the family entry and the edit answer). `registry.ts` adds `registrations` POST, `registrations/batch` POST and `registrations/[id]` PATCH, `domain/contracts/index.ts` exports the file, and `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` drops the three lines: `PENDING_COUNT` goes from 121 to 118.
- The 0053 triggers stay as a safety net. The entry insert trigger leaves a `section_id` the handler sets alone, and the update trigger stays out of a move because `section_id` changes in the same update.
- `registrations/[id]/pay.ts` is unchanged: it charges the amount on the payment row.
- What players see is unchanged apart from the wording listed under Deviations, which comes with no switch.
- Tests: `test/integration/contracts/registrations.test.ts` (18), `test/integration/registration-section-ids.test.ts` (7), `test/integration/registration-section-reads.test.ts` (20), `test/integration/registration-plain-language.test.ts` (4), `test/integration/registration-replays-edges.test.ts` (32: a request sent twice answered 409, Stripe down writing nothing, the waitlist, spots left, byes, and the role checks on a move and on payment status), `test/unit/registration-readers.test.ts` (10) and `test/unit/pricing.test.ts` (25, up from 3); `domain-boundaries.test.ts` scans the new contract file. Every other test passes unchanged, `registration-rules.test.ts` included.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1272 in 54 files, up from 1239 in 53; integration 546 in 47 files, up from 465 in 42). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed. Nothing was run against a remote database.

### Step 11: walk-ins and the waitlist read the section rows

- `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/walk-ins.ts` POST reads the body with `parseBody` and the new contract, and finds the section among the live rows (`loadSections`) by exact name, as before (case-sensitive; a director may still type a name). An archived or unknown section is 400 with `{ error: 'Invalid section' }`, the same words as before. The tournament select no longer reads `sections`, and `parseSectionList` is gone. The guest, the entry and the payment are Drizzle inserts in one `db.batch`, as step 10 did; the entry sets `section_id`. The fee is the row's `fee_regular`, else the event's `entry_fee`, exactly as the JSON's `entryFee` was read: no early, late or member pricing at the door, as before. The capacity check still counts waitlisted entries, as it did.
- `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/waitlist.ts` POST: `parseBody`; the entry select adds `section_id`, the tournament type drops `sections`, and the price is `priceEntry` over the entry's section row (by `section_id`, else by name, among live and archived rows, live first) and the tournament's live pricing columns, with the member discount for an active member until step 15. An entry whose section has no row at all is priced at the event fee, as `entryPrice` priced a section the JSON lacked. The update and the payment insert stay raw SQL; their SQL did not change.
- `domain/contracts/registration.ts` adds `walkInRequestSchema` and `walkInResponseSchema` (`{ registration, guestId }`, the registration being the full row), and `waitlistOfferRequestSchema` and `waitlistOfferResponseSchema` (`{ success: true, amount }`), written from the bodies `adminAddWalkIn` and `offerWaitlistSpot` in `src/lib/api.ts` send as `TournamentManagePage.tsx` fills them. `registry.ts` adds `admin/tournaments/[id]/walk-ins` POST and `admin/tournaments/[id]/waitlist` POST, and `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` drops the two lines: `PENDING_COUNT` goes from 118 to 116.
- No permission logic changed: both handlers still call `requireTournamentManager` before the body is read.
- `entryPrice` stays for the tournament page and the family entry panel until step 13; no server handler calls it now.
- Tests: `test/integration/contracts/walkins-waitlist.test.ts` (15, new), `test/integration/contracts/walkins-waitlist-json.test.ts` (6, new), `test/integration/contracts/walkins-waitlist-edges.test.ts` (35, new), `test/integration/registration-section-ids.test.ts` (9, up from 7) and `test/unit/registration-readers.test.ts` (15, up from 10). Every other test passes unchanged, `lifecycle.test.ts`, `registration-rules.test.ts` and the two `sections-triggers` files included.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1277 in 54 files, up from 1272; integration 604 in 50 files, up from 546 in 47). `npm run lint` is at 36 errors and 4 warnings, the same as before the change, none in the files this step touches. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed. Nothing was run against a remote database.

### Step 12: server display reads the section rows

- `functions/utils/events/sectionsRepo.ts` gains `toTournamentResponse(row, sections, schedules?)`: it drops the raw `sections` and `round_schedule` text from a `tournaments` row and puts in the priced rows and the parsed round list (`round_schedule` stays the parsed array until step 14). Every handler that answers with a `tournaments` row uses it. `sectionResponse` keeps today's JSON element shape (`prizeFund`, `unratedOk` and `prizes` are left out when empty, not null) and adds `id`, `cap` and `fees { regular, early, late }` as effective prices from `tierFees`, with `entryFee` equal to the effective regular fee for old clients. `sectionResponsesFor(db, ids)` is a chunked query joined to the tournament for prices, used by the clearinghouse and the club page, whose SQL does not select the price columns. `loadSectionsFor` (90 ids per `IN` list) loads the sections of every listed event at once.
- Readers moved to the table: `functions/api/tournaments.ts` (list), `tournaments/[id].ts`, `clearinghouse.ts` (LCA rows; partner rows keep `sections: []`), `clubs/[id].ts` (sections went from a JSON string to objects), `admin/tournaments/[id].ts` (the PATCH answer), `admin/tournaments/[id]/registration.ts` (its PATCH answered `SELECT *` raw; it now answers through `toTournamentResponse`), `admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts` and `rating-report.ts` (names from the table, archived rows included, each name once). `admin/tournaments.ts` (POST) also uses `toTournamentResponse`, though it was not on the step's list, because the audit requires it. `functions/utils/tournament-manage.ts` loses `parseTournamentSections` (no callers) and types `tournamentPrizes`; `functions/utils/prizes.ts` needed no change. `functions/types.ts`: `TournamentRow` has every column. The clearinghouse and club queries only lost the `sections` column; they stay plain D1, not Drizzle, since their SQL did not otherwise change.
- Pages that offer entry (detail, manage, list, clearinghouse, club page) show live sections only. The rating report includes archived ones.
- `domain/contracts/events.ts` adds response contracts for the event detail, the manage page, the registration settings PATCH, the rating report and the clearinghouse, and a request contract for the registration PATCH (the handler now reads its body with `parseBody`). The list and admin contracts use the new section shape and a `round_schedule` list. `domain/contracts/clubs.ts` (new, exported from `index.ts`) holds the club detail response. `savedSectionSchema` is strict on its own keys and `fees`, and loose (`catchall`) beyond them so stored extra keys can come back. `registry.ts` adds six routes (`tournaments/[id]` GET, `admin/tournaments/[id]/manage` GET, `admin/tournaments/[id]/registration` PATCH, `admin/tournaments/[id]/rating-report` GET, `clearinghouse` GET, `clubs/[id]` GET); `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` drops six lines and `PENDING_COUNT` goes from 116 to 110.
- Nothing runs against a remote database. The frontend is untouched; every response a page reads keeps its field names and shapes and only gains fields, with the exceptions under Deviations.
- Tests: `test/unit/sections-reader-audit.test.ts` (24, among them the planted `SELECT * FROM tournaments` handler passed to `jsonResponse`), `test/integration/contracts/tournaments.test.ts` (22, new), `test/integration/contracts/tournaments-readers.test.ts` (34, new), and updates to `tournaments-list.test.ts`, `tournaments-list-edges.test.ts`, `admin-tournaments.test.ts` (one case added) and `sections-domain.test.ts`. The bounded-query proof: for 150 events the list and clearinghouse tests assert one section query per 90 listed events with at most 100 bound parameters each.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1303 in 55 files, up from 1277 in 54; integration 661 in 52 files, up from 604 in 50). `npm run lint` goes from 36 errors and 4 warnings to 35 errors and 4 warnings: the one error removed was an unused assignment in `rating-report.ts`; no touched file has an error left. `npx wrangler pages functions build` compiles. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed. Nothing was run against a remote database.

### Step 13: the pages read the section rows

- `src/lib/api.ts` takes its tournament types from the contracts with `import type` (a runtime import would put zod in the site bundle): `ApiTournamentSection` is `SavedSection` (`savedSectionSchema`: `id`, `name`, `entryFee`, the rules, `rulesSet`, `cap`, `fees { regular, early, late }`, plus the optional `prizeFund`, `unratedOk` and `prizes` and any stored extra key), `ApiTournamentListItem` is `TournamentListItem`, and `ApiTournamentDetail` is the detail answer's tournament with `custom_details` and `round_schedule` typed as the setup form writes them and `waitlist_count` optional (only the event page gets it). A new `ApiSectionDraft` is a section while the wizard or the manage page edits it: a loaded one keeps its id, one added in the form has only a name and a fee. `adminCreateTournament` and `adminUpdateTournament` take `ApiSectionDraft[]`; `SectionRulesEditor` and `PrizesEditor` take and return one.
- The manage page keeps every loaded section object while it is edited (fee, prize fund, rules, prizes) and sends the list back whole when it changed, ids included, so the server matches each section by id. It already sent what it loaded; the type now carries the id, so nothing can drop it. A section added on the page has no id until the save. The wizard's "From an existing tournament" copy drops the source's ids (the create ignores them anyway); a wizard section from scratch is `{ name, entryFee }` as before. Neither screen has a box to rename a section today, so the rename path is the body the page sends with a name changed (see the test below).
- The tournament page and the family entry panel price with `priceShownSection(section, tournament, nowMs, { isLcaMember })`, new in `domain/registration/pricing.ts` (zod-free, next to `priceEntry`), with the section found by name, as before. An answer's `fees.early` and `fees.late` are either the section's own price or one worked out from the tournament's columns (`tierFees`), and checkout prices the two differently: an own price replaces the line's amount, a worked-out one keeps the tournament's discount or fee, and a worked-out early price is clamped at $0 where the discount line is not. So the helper works out what the tournament alone would give and passes a tier price on to `priceEntry` as the section's own only when it differs. The page then shows what checkout charges for a section with its own early or late price, and the same lines as before for every other section (no section has its own tier price until WS08). The one case the answer cannot tell apart is an own early price of $0 where the early discount is larger than the fee: it is read as worked out, and differs from checkout only when the late fee is also due. A name with no section (nothing chosen yet) is priced at the event fee, as `entryPrice` priced it. The member discount still applies until step 15. The time is read once when the page or the panel opens (`useState(() => Date.now())`), because reading the clock while rendering is a lint error; checkout prices each entry again on the server.
- `entryPrice`, `sectionBaseFee` and `PricedTournament` are removed from `domain/registration/pricing.ts`, with the header comment that described them. The shims `src/lib/pricing.ts` and `functions/utils/pricing.ts` are each one `export *` line, so they lose the names without an edit. No file under `functions/` called `entryPrice` (steps 10 and 11 moved the server callers).
- Other readers now see objects only: `src/lib/scholastic.ts` drops `sectionName` (a section is always an object with a name) and `isScholasticTournament` takes a list of `{ name }`; `UnifiedTournament.sections` in `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts` is `ApiTournamentSection[]` (partner events answer `[]`); `TournamentsPage.tsx` and `ScholasticPage.tsx` lose their bare-string branches. `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` sends the browser to Stripe with `window.location.assign(url)` instead of assigning `window.location.href`, the same navigation, which clears that file's lint error.
- `scripts/check-bundle.ts` (`npm run check:bundle`, run with `node --experimental-strip-types` like the database scripts) reads every file under `dist/assets` after `npm run build` and fails when one carries `ZodError` or `_zod.def`, two strings zod's runtime keeps after minifying. Dry run: with a runtime import of `savedSectionSchema` planted at the end of `src/lib/api.ts` and the site built to a scratch folder, the check failed with exit 1 naming `index-*.js (contains "ZodError")`; with the line removed it passes (114 files checked).
- Tests: `test/integration/sections-rename-ui-path.test.ts` (6, new): it loads the event through `GET .../manage` as the page does, renames one section in that list and sends it as `adminUpdateTournament` puts it on the wire (`JSON.stringify` of the body); the row keeps its id, both entries keep `section_id` and take the new name, the game moves, the mirror holds the new name and no id, and the answer and the next load show the same id under the new name. It also shows a rename back and forth, a save of the unchanged list moving nothing, a rename with a new section, the refusal without ids, the role cases (assigned director and organizing club rep pass; another club's rep and a plain member 403, no sign-in 401, nothing moved) and the wizard's copy. `test/unit/check-bundle.test.ts` (5, new) builds a planted page and a clean one with vite and runs the check on both. `test/unit/pricing.test.ts` keeps its cases and amounts, now through `priceShownSection` with each answered section (the 432-case comparison with the old function builds the answered sections from the same JSON with `tierFees`), and adds three: `priceShownSection` gives the same amount and lines as `priceEntry` on the stored row for eight rows (own regular, early and late prices, a free section, cents) under four tournaments (one with the early and late windows overlapping and a $30 discount on a $25 fee) at four moments, member or not; the overlap case comes to $5 (25 − 30 + 10), as checkout charges; and the one case it cannot tell apart is pinned. `test/unit/domain-shims.test.ts` checks that `priceEntry` and `priceShownSection` come through the server shim, each defined once in `domain/registration/pricing.ts`, and that `entryPrice` and `sectionBaseFee` are defined nowhere. Four more files cover the same move from other sides: `test/unit/client-sections-audit.test.ts` (13) scans the site's code for `entryPrice`, `sectionBaseFee` and sections JSON handed to pricing, checks that `api.ts` takes its section, list and detail types from the contracts with `import type`, that the two pricing screens call `priceShownSection` and not `priceEntry`, and follows the runtime imports from `src/main.tsx` to show that none reaches zod or `domain/contracts`; `test/unit/api-section-bodies.test.ts` (5) checks the bodies `adminUpdateTournament` and `adminCreateTournament` put on the wire (a loaded section keeps its id, an added one has none); `test/unit/tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx` (15, jsdom) renders the tournament page and the family panel from an answer shaped like the server's and reads the section list, the price lines and the family total, including a section with its own early price and one with its own late price; `test/integration/sections-client-readers.test.ts` (7) shows that list, detail and manage answer plain-name sections as objects that match the contract, that the price the pages work out equals what a waitlist offer charges across early, late and member settings and for sections with their own early or late price, the overlap case among them, and that the manage page echoing worked-out prices back stores no price of its own. `test/integration/domain-shared-rules.test.ts` and `test/integration/contracts/walkins-waitlist.test.ts` call `priceEntry`; the waitlist test keeps a copy of the old JSON reader to compare with.
- Check on the local dev server: the site was built with the sign-in pointed at a local stand-in for Supabase auth (a small server on 127.0.0.1 that answers `/auth/v1/user` from the token's `sub`, as the integration harness does) and served with `wrangler pages dev` on a copy of the local D1 (migrations 0001 to 0053; the repository's own local database was not touched), with an LCA admin, an active member and that member's child (not a member) seeded. Chromium (`/opt/pw-browsers`) drove, as the admin: the wizard from scratch (Bayou Fall Open, Sat, Oct 24, 2026, sections Open $40, U1600 $30 and K-8 $15; the review showed "Open · $40 U1600 · $30 K-8 · $15" and the create body carried names and fees only); then, beside the screens, the event was made visible with registration open, an early discount of $5 to Tue, Oct 20, a member discount of $3 and a $10 late fee from Fri, Oct 23, and one entry was put in U1600; the manage page (Details), where U1600's fee went to $35 and U1000 was added: the PATCH carried the three loaded sections with their ids and U1000 without one, the rows kept their ids, U1000 got a new row and the U1600 entry kept its `section_id`. As the member, the tournament page's section list offered Open at $32, U1600 at $27, K-8 at $7 and U1000 with no price, and the price box read "Entry fee $40, Early entry discount −$5, LCA member discount −$3, You pay $32" for Open (likewise for U1600 and K-8; none for the free U1000). The family panel listed the child's sections at $35, $30, $10 and free (no member discount for the child), and with the member and the child both in Open the button read "Register 2 · pay $67". The same run against the build from before this step, on its own copy of the same database, gave the same text, options, price lines, buttons and request bodies; the screenshots match pixel for pixel except for the hover shade of a button just clicked. Nothing was submitted to Stripe. That run came before the pages moved to `priceShownSection`; no section in it had its own early or late price, and for such sections the helper hands `priceEntry` the same input as before, so the prices seen hold. A section's own tier prices on the two screens are covered by `tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx` and `sections-client-readers.test.ts`.
- Checks: `npm run build`, `npm run check:bundle` (no zod in 114 files), `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run test:all` pass (unit 1352 in 59 files, up from 1303 in 55; integration 674 in 54 files, up from 661 in 52). `npm run lint` goes from 35 errors and 4 warnings to 34 errors and 4 warnings: the `window.location.href` error in `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` is gone, and the price is computed from a time held in state rather than by reading the clock during render; no touched file has an error left. `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed. No route was added, so `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` stays at 110. Nothing was run against a remote database.

### Step 14: schedules have one writer and one reader

- `domain/events/schedules.ts` (no zod) holds `normalizeRoundSchedule` (reads the old JSON the way the 0053 trigger does), `roundScheduleJson` (writes `[{ round, date, time }]`), `validateSchedules(schedules, roundCount)`, `roundsFor(schedules, scheduleId)` and `scheduleForRound`. Rules: exactly one primary; a second schedule's merge round is from 2 to the event's round count and it lists exactly rounds 1 to merge round minus 1; the primary lists the rest; rounds are unique within a schedule; names are unique. `roundsFor` gives a player on the second schedule their own rounds before the merge round and the primary's from the merge round on.
- `domain/contracts/events.ts` adds `scheduleRoundSchema`, `scheduleSchema` and `scheduleInputSchema` (types `ScheduleRound`, `Schedule`, `ScheduleRequest`). Every tournament answer types `round_schedule` with the round schema and gains `schedules`; the admin PATCH body accepts `schedules`. `src/lib/api.ts` takes `ApiRoundScheduleItem` and the new `ApiSchedule` from the contracts with `import type`. No page file changed, and `npm run check:bundle` still finds no zod (114 files).
- `functions/utils/events/schedulesRepo.ts` provides `loadSchedules`, `loadSchedulesFor` (one query per 90 event ids, at most 100 bound parameters), `scheduleResponse`, `roundScheduleResponse` and `buildSaveSchedules`. The 0053 insert trigger already makes a primary schedule for every event, so the writer matches that row (a primary sent without an id matches the existing primary) and does not create another. Batch order: guards, archive the schedules left out, demote the old primary, update or insert rows (new ids made in code), rewrite changed rounds, then the `round_schedule` JSON last, in today's shape. A new primary is only inserted after the old one is archived or demoted, because the partial unique index allows one live primary per event. The save takes either `roundSchedule` (today's body field) or `schedules` (the whole list); sending both is 400.
- The admin PATCH (`functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id].ts`) runs the schedule plan after the sections plan in the same batch, with the same kind of in-batch guard (a fingerprint of the event's schedule rows, a check that a schedule being removed has no entries that are not withdrawn, and a check that every live second schedule's merge round fits `tournaments.rounds` as the batch runs). A stale plan is 409 with a plain message. The main update no longer writes `round_schedule`.
- Readers: `round_schedule` is the live primary's rounds, as a list, and `schedules[]` holds the live rows, in `functions/api/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts`, the admin PATCH and POST answers, the registration PATCH answer and the list in `functions/api/tournaments.ts`. `toTournamentResponse` now requires the schedule records and no longer parses the column.
- Registrations keep `schedule_id` from the 0053 trigger (the primary schedule) until WS06 adds the schedule picker. A second schedule has no screen until WS08's setup, so the new refusals below have no flag.
- Tests: `schedules-audit.test.ts` (11), `schedules-domain.test.ts` (31) and `schedules.test.ts` (46), all new, plus 3 cases in `contracts/tournaments.test.ts`. `tournaments-list-edges.test.ts` and `contracts-layer.test.ts` now count the `schedules` field. `tournament-edit.test.ts`, `sections-writer.test.ts` and the lifecycle tests pass unchanged. `contracts-pending.ts` stays at 110: no new route.
- From review: a save that does not send `rounds` leaves the round count out of its update (it used to write back the count it had read), so it cannot undo a count another save changed. The merge-round fit guard reads `tournaments.rounds` inside the batch and runs whenever a save sends schedules or a round count; a save that sends neither has no schedule statement. Two races are covered by tests: a round-count change landing between a schedule save's read and its batch, and a second schedule added while a round-count change is being checked. Both end in 409 with the data still valid.
