  - StatusBadge is step 26's file: only its light gold tone's words change (line 35, to `text-gold-ink-badge`); its gold tone on the navy surface (line 28) stays `text-lca-gold` on navy, by the allowlist, and its other tones and their keep entries are untouched.
  - The hover forms and the one `/90` (the header's "Association" line) are all on navy and stay. None of the 135 sat on an opaque gold fill.
  - A ground counts as dark only where brand gold reads 4.5:1 on it. A hex ground stays put in every look, so it is navy only where brand gold reads 4.5:1 (a relative luminance of about 0.0526 or less) and light only where the light look's dark gold `#866a1e` does (about 0.871 or more); between them neither gold reads as words, as on the urgent banner's red (brand gold 3.58:1), the info banner's blue (3.84:1) and Facebook's blue (1.86:1), and a person decides. `data-ground="dark"` marks a ground for the focus ring and says nothing of its colour: the home page hero carries it over a darkened photo, where brand gold reads 4.29:1 at the lightest and 3.14:1 on a gold tint, so a gold use there needs an allowlist entry with the ratio it reads, and `contrast.test.ts` measures what the hero holds over the photo rather than the navy. `text-gold-ink` on a ground the reading cannot place is a problem that no entry can take. No `text-lca-gold` or `text-gold-ink` on the tree changes ground: the six on the club heroes are placed by their inline navy, and none sits on the hero, a banner or a hex ground. The hero's pill, `text-gold-on-dark-badge`, is now measured over the photo, at 4.76:1, rather than over the navy.
  - The filter dropdowns draw the chosen filter in light gold in their navy branch and in dark gold in the other, chosen by their `on` prop. The guard and the reader of `contrast.test.ts` take the branch for `on === 'navy'` as on navy and the other as on a light ground, wherever the component is drawn today, and the guard holds every place a component told its ground by `on` is drawn to the `on` its ground needs: `FilterDropdown`'s one place gives `on="navy"` in the `/tournaments` hero, `MultiSelectDropdown`'s three take the light default on the email page's card, and `StatusBadge`'s four give `on="light"` on light grounds.
  - `GOLD_BUTTON` in `src/lib/brand.ts` now names `text-brand-navy` rather than `text-lca-navy`: the same navy, fixed in every look, so the button is unchanged, and no `lca-navy` text class is left in `src`. `semantic-map-cascade.test.ts` and `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts`, which read the string, pass.
- **The guard.** `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` runs the script's own check, so the codemod and the guard never disagree, and adds what the codemod does not read: brand gold as a text class in its other forms (the text utilities of `--gold` and `--gold-on-dark`, the two tokens that are brand gold in every look; the hex or its `rgb()`, with a type hint, as an arbitrary property, in capitals; either token's variable), each placed by the same reader and allowed only on navy, an allowlist entry for one held to its count; a style colour that can be brand gold (the literal or its `rgb()`, `LCA.gold`, a template of one of them, a constant bound to either, a fallback or a `?:` that can be one, a style held in a constant or a property of one or spread from one, placed on each element whose style takes it, or a club's colour that does not go through `clubTextColor`); brand gold in HTML a page builds as a string; `#705718` outside the heritage pages (the six `HERITAGE_ROUTES` draw, read from `src/App.tsx`) and the About menu's "You are here" label; and `#866a1e` as text below 13px (in a class, or as the value of a color property, which is words whatever its string holds), as text anywhere but the phone sheet's "You are here" on its `#fbfaf6` row, or as a line anywhere but the phone tab bar's current tab. Those three are WS02's parts, `WS02_GOLD` in the fixture, named by the files WS02's Frontend list gives (`AboutMenu.tsx`, `MobileMenu.tsx`, `BottomTabs.tsx`); none exists yet, so each matches nothing, which the guard says, and a planted copy of each passes in its context and fails out of it. It holds the gold tokens of one kind of ground to it as well, placed by the same reading: a badge's dark gold (`text-gold-ink-badge` and `text-gold-ink-badge-club`, `#705718` in the light looks and about 2.2:1 on navy, and gold-ink as a variable in a class or a style or through `clubTextColor`) never on navy, and a pill's light gold (`text-gold-on-dark-badge`) only on a dark ground, wherever the reading finds the ground, inside a wrapper component that paints navy around what it is given or in a component drawn on navy under another name. The 12 uses it cannot place are entries of `GOLD_BADGE_ALLOWLIST` in the fixture: the tags of the audit log, the members table, the admin list of tournaments and the email campaigns and StatusBadge's light gold tone, each looked up by a status in an object of strings, the club page's Upcoming badge, and the home hero's pill over its darkened photo. A badge's entry names the code that reads its string (`meta.className` and the like), and the guard places every element whose className holds that code on the entry's ground, so moving one of those rows onto navy fails it. In a component told its ground by an `on` prop the branch for `on === 'navy'` is on navy and the other on a light ground, and every place such a component is drawn (`FilterDropdown`, `MultiSelectDropdown` and `StatusBadge`, eight places) must give the `on` its ground needs, as each does. Brand gold fails as an icon's paint too: a lucide icon's `color` (which lucide draws as the icon's stroke), `fill` or `stroke`, an SVG shape's, a style's `fill` or `stroke`, either token's variable in a style, and a colour a place passes into a component of the site, read where the component uses it. Every failure is planted in a temporary tree, with the class names put together when the test runs.
- **Lint.** 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after. No error is in a file this step touches: the errors are in `functions/` and `scanner/`. Of the 13 warnings, ten sit in eight files the step rewrites and stay as they were.
- **Build.** `dist/assets` before (`3d80a0e`, built again for this record) and after: CSS 121,540 and 122,025 bytes (one file, 485 more), JavaScript 2,567,800 and 2,568,391 bytes (591 more) in 104 and 104 files; 127 and 127 files in all. The CSS adds the four utilities the pages now use (the three tokens' text classes and the add-seat pill's hover form) and the three tokens in the plain `:root` and the four new-look blocks, and drops the old hover rule nothing names any more; the old gold hex classes keep their rules because the gold map and the step 24 records spell them (see Follow-ups, "Classes only docs and tests name reach the CSS"). The tests and the script of this step put the class names they plant together when they run, so they add no rule. The JavaScript grows with the longer token names, the News page's word and `clubTextColor`. `npm run check:bundle` finds no zod.
- **The dark spot run.** `dark-spot.spec.ts` now credits a colour-contrast failure to a later step only for StatusBadge's light tones (step 26); gold text is credited to no step, so a gold text failure in dark mode fails it. Results, quoted from the run: "axe in dark mode on /: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings" (the five slide dots fail `target-size`, as listed for the light look); "axe in dark mode on /tournaments: 27 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 1 other rule(s) with findings": the LCA pill's words, `text-gold-ink-badge` on its gold tints, read 5.08:1 where `text-[#7a5c00]` read 1.84:1, and the one other finding is the gold "State champions" link's `link-in-text-block`, as at steps 23 and 24; "axe in dark mode on /membership: 25 rules passed, 0 colour-contrast failure(s), 0 other rule(s) with findings".
- Checks: `npm run lint` is 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after, no error in a touched file. `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck:functions` and `npm run check:bundle` pass (no zod in 127 files). `npm run test:all` passes: unit 3117 tests in 115 files (2770 in 107 before; the eight new files hold 258 and the changed ones 89 more), integration 790 in 58 (unchanged). `npm run test:a11y`, with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`: 125 passed and 30 skipped (unchanged: no test was added, and `dark-spot.spec.ts` now credits only StatusBadge's light tones to a later step); "Baseline matches: no colour or font changed." and `test/a11y/baseline.json` is unmodified. The last run logged no `SQLITE_BUSY`; earlier runs of the step logged it twice from a local server, about when `builds.spec.ts` started, and no test failed (see Follow-ups, "The a11y servers share one database (step 23)"). `scanner:check` was not run; nothing under `scanner/` or `functions/utils/scan/` changed.

### Decisions

- The real preview id is in `wrangler.toml`, not a placeholder (K confirmed it on October 8). The guard still refuses a placeholder, production's id and production's name.
- Previews keep `SITE_URL` (and `VITE_SITE_URL`) on the production origin. Previews are not meant to send mail.
- Preview secrets are set by K in the Pages dashboard: Stripe test keys only, and no Resend key until K wants test mail. Without a Resend key every send fails, is logged, and the request carries on.
- Previews keep the production `lca-club-logos` bucket for now.
- The binding name stays `DB` in both places, so the code reads `env.DB` everywhere.
- **Contracts are a ratchet** (decided October 8, before step 7). Every endpoint a WS01 step touches gets a zod contract and a contract test; a coverage test blocks any new endpoint without one; the list of endpoints still waiting for a contract can only shrink. There is no step that converts every endpoint at once, so K1d is marked "partial: ratchet" in the table above. The three page routes that serve HTML (`functions/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/clubs/[id].ts` and `functions/news/[slug].ts`) are left out of the contract list, with that reason. The count of endpoints still waiting is 123 after step 7.
- **Archive rule (step 12, decision 7 applied to readers).** Only an empty section can be archived (step 9 refuses otherwise), so an archived section never hides live entries, and a name that returns revives its row (same id). Pages that offer entry therefore show live sections only; the rating report includes archived rows, so players in a section only an archived row names now appear (the old code dropped them), each name listed once with the live row winning. This settles the plan's open question; it is a decision, not an open question.
- **Echo rule (step 12).** The manage page sends its sections back exactly as it loaded them, and the wizard copies them from the list. So `fees` that carry `regular` set nothing on a save (otherwise the save would be 400, and would store the worked-out early and late prices as the section's own fixed prices), and the admin POST ignores section ids (ids copied from another event would be refused). Caps are copied. `sectionFeeOverrides` in `domain/events/sections.ts` holds the rule.
- **Extra keys come back (step 12, from review).** Section answers spread the row's `extra_json` keys first, then their own fields (`SAVED_SECTION_KEYS` and `extraKeys()` drop any extra key that clashes), so a manage-page save keeps them in the row and the JSON mirror. `savedSectionSchema` is loose beyond its strict own keys. Limitation: a known key stored with an unexpected type (for example `prizeFund: 700`, which sits in `extra_json`) is not echoed and is lost on the next manage-page save; echoing it would break the field types pages read today. It affects only rows that already held malformed values.
- **Dark mode** follows the device setting as soon as `newLook` is on, with the Appearance control (System, Light, Dark). There is no separate dark mode switch.
- **Member discount** is retired (applied in step 15). There is one price per section per tier and no member pricing. The member-discount input and the "LCA members save" line are removed without a switch, an exception to the brief's rule that visible changes ship behind one, because keeping them would advertise a price that is not charged (see Deviations). `tournaments.member_discount` stays in the table, unread and no longer written. Before checkpoint B merges, K runs `SELECT id, name, member_discount FROM tournaments WHERE member_discount > 0 AND status != 'completed'` on production; **not run yet**, and the result is recorded here. The step does not wait on it, but the merge does.
- **LCA membership requirement.** `requires_lca_membership` defaults to on for LCA-run events (no `club_id`). Club-run events, existing and new, start with it off, because clubs get tournament creation first, free of charge. Only the owning club's rep or an LCA admin may change it; an assigned tournament director cannot. Applied in step 15 (migration 0054 backfills existing club-run events to off, and a new event with a club starts off unless the request says otherwise). An assigned director gets 403 on any request carrying the field. An LCA-run event can never be set to off (400), and `FORCE_LCA_MEMBERSHIP` (off) is the stand-in for the planned site setting `membership_required_for_all` in WS08. In WS01 the column exists and the setup form shows the switch, behind `membershipRequirementSwitch`, which stays off until WS06; turning a non-member away or adding the membership at checkout is WS06's, because today's registration has no way to add a membership inline, and enforcing it now would leave non-members with no way forward.
- **Removing a section that has entries** (decision 7, which replaces the plan's "archive it and the entries keep `section_id`") is refused with 400 in the usual `{ error }` shape and a plain message of the decided form, "3 entries are in this section. Move them to another section first.", naming the section ("3 entries are in the U1600 section. Move them to another section first."; "1 entry is ... Move it ..." for one). An entry counts unless it is withdrawn, so a waitlisted entry counts. A section with games is refused too ("Games have been paired in the Blitz section, so it cannot be removed."), even when every entry has withdrawn, because games are matched to sections by name. Only an empty section is archived, and withdrawn entries keep their `section_id` on the archived row. Nothing is written when the save is refused, the tournament's other fields included. Built in step 9.
- **Event-mode phase gaps (step 5).** The brief's Phase 0 rules leave some moments without a phase. Decided, and tested in `test/unit/eventMode.test.ts` and `test/unit/eventMode-edges.test.ts` (the two rules that go against the brief's text are under Deviations, "Event-mode phase times"):
  - `week` changes to `dayBefore` at midnight starting the day before, as the brief says. `dayBefore` then runs on through the night until 6:00 AM on day 1, the gap the brief leaves between "the calendar day before" and check-in at 6:00 AM.
  - A day whose round has no time set (a null, blank or unreadable value) stays in `checkin` all day (the strip's "time to be announced" line, and the TD's "Round times missing" fix-it). A first round time before 6:00 AM makes the day `eventDay` from 6:00 AM.
  - A day between `date` and `end_date` with no round in the schedule (no key in `firstRoundTimes`, while other days of the event have one) is not a playing day, so it is never `checkin`. From 6:00 AM it is `dayBefore` when the next round day is the next calendar day, `week` when the next round day is further off (a one-round-a-week event), and `eventDay` when no round day is left before `end_date`. So a Fri to Sun event with no Saturday round is `dayBefore` all Saturday, polls at the slow rate and raises no fix-it. When no key falls inside the event at all, the schedule is not written yet and every day is "time to be announced".
  - A day counts as having no round only when the whole schedule is dated inside the event. `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(schedule, date, endDate)` takes the event's days for this: when any round has a blank or unreadable date, or a date outside the event (a typo, or a schedule left on the old dates after the event moved), every event day with no round of its own gets null, so it stays `checkin` with "time to be announced" and the TD's "Round times missing" fix-it still appears. Rounds dated outside the event are left out. So a Fri to Sun event with only round 1 dated is `checkin` all Saturday and Sunday, not `eventDay`.
  - `final` (status `completed`, or results in for every section's last round) wins over the calendar phases inside the window and lasts until 11:59 PM on the last day; from then until 48 hours later it is `after`. Outside the window the result is null even for a completed event.
  - An `end_date` that is missing, unreadable or before `date` means a one-day event. A `date` that is not a calendar day gives null.
- **Server helper paths (step 16).** A path under `functions/utils` that the brief or the phase plans give for an existing helper dates from before step 16; read it through `scripts/moves/server-areas.json`, which maps each old path to its area folder (`registrationEmails.ts` is now `registration/registrationEmails.ts`, `swiss/` is now `live/swiss/`, and so on). A new server helper that the brief places at the root of `functions/utils` (such as `receipts.ts`, `recipients.ts`, `homeHero.ts` or `clubSchedule.ts`) goes in the area folder that fits it (`events`, `registration`, `membership`, `live` or `admin`), or in a new area folder under `functions/utils` when none does, never in `functions/<area>/`, where it would become a page route. Only a platform file shared by every area (like `auth`, `response`, `email` or `site`) goes at the root. The brief's text is left as written.
- **Site library paths (step 17).** A path under `src/lib` that the brief, the phase plans or the boards give for `tournamentStatus`, `clearinghouse`, `scholastic`, `uschessUpload`, `champions`, `clubColors`, `clubMapData`, `mapLocation` or `roles` dates from before step 17; read it through `scripts/moves/client-lib.json` (for example, the coach role goes in `src/areas/admin/roles.ts`). New library code for one area goes in `src/areas/<area>/` and is re-exported from that area's `index.ts`; an area's `index.ts` re-exports only that area's own modules, plus the `domain/` rules it fronts (`clubs` the regions, `households` the family size), never another area or `src/lib`. `src/lib` keeps the platform files shared by every area and the one-line re-exports of `domain/`, so the brief's paths for those (`src/lib/pricing.ts`, `src/lib/regions.ts`, `src/lib/family.ts` and the rest) keep working. The brief's text is left as written.
- **Checkpoints.** Each checkpoint is a draft PR that K merges; `migrate-db.yml` then applies its migrations to production. K runs `npm run db:migrate:preview` from the branch before reviewing each checkpoint's preview.
- **API client by area (step 18).** The site's API calls live in `src/areas/<area>/api.ts`, the shared request helpers (`authHeaders`, `handleResponse`) and `ApiError` in `src/lib/http.ts`, and `src/lib/api.ts` re-exports them all. `scripts/moves/api-split.json` names the module of every declaration. The rules:
  - Each declaration sits in exactly one module. A type more than one area uses sits in the area that owns the resource, and the other areas import it from that area's `api.ts` with `import type`, never from `@/lib/api`, which re-exports them and would make a cycle. A private helper used by one area moves with it (`exportQuery`, with the member export); one used by several goes to `http.ts`.
  - `http.ts` imports only `@/lib/supabase`, nothing from `src/areas` or `src/lib/api.ts`.
  - The area imports run one way: `events` takes types from `registration` and `live`, `membership` from `registration` and `households`, and `clubs` from `membership`; nothing points back.
  - Scan and scoresheet calls go to `live`, because scanning records a game on the day of the event.
  - Each area's `index.ts` stays as it was and does not re-export `api.ts`: if it did, importing an area's `index.ts` would pull in `http.ts` and, through the type imports, the other areas' modules. Code imports an area's calls from its `api.ts`.
  - Pages and components keep importing from `@/lib/api`; they move to the area path when they are next changed for another reason. New calls go in their area's `api.ts`.
  - The modules hold:
    - `events`: tournaments, their setup and deletion, the contract-derived section, schedule, list and detail types, prizes, directors, the manage page, the US Chess rating report, announcing an event, news posts and the club news feed, and state champions.
    - `registration`: entries (one player, a family, a walk-in), `ApiRegistration` and `ApiMyRegistration`, byes, paying, the waitlist offer, reminders and the registration settings.
    - `households`: the children on a family account (`ApiChild`).
    - `live`: pairings and results, with `ApiTournamentGame`, `ApiTournamentPairing` and `ApiStanding`, and the scoresheet scanner.
    - `membership`: `ApiMember`, `ApiAdminMember`, the signed-in member (`syncMember`, `getMe`, `updateMe`), joining and renewing, donations, the US Chess rating lookup, the admin member list and its edits, deleting a member and the member export.
    - `clubs`: the club directory and club pages, club administration, officers, club news and logos.
    - `admin`: support tickets, the contact form, email campaigns, site announcements, acting as a member and the audit log.
    - `governance`: board seats and officer photos, board members, governance documents, seat assignment and regions, and the board inbox.
  - Calls that fit no area went where the page that uses them belongs: news posts and the club news feed (the News page; the results, champions and news pages are one workstream) to `events`; support tickets, the contact form, campaigns, announcements, acting as a member and the audit log to `admin`; the US Chess rating lookup to `membership`, with the US Chess search component; the reminder calls to `registration`, with the reminder button.
- **Components by area (step 19).** A component that belongs to one area lives in `src/areas/<area>/components`. A path that the brief, the phase plans or the boards give under `src/components/tournaments`, `family`, `admin`, `governance`, `maps` or `uscf`, or for `src/components/AdminAnnouncementPanel.tsx` or `RegistrationReminderButton.tsx`, dates from before step 19; read it through `scripts/moves/client-components.json` (for example, `Crosstable` is now `src/areas/live/components/Crosstable.tsx`). A new component the brief places in one of those old folders, such as `FestivalsPanel.tsx`, `AdminSidebar.tsx`, `AddFamilyMemberForm.tsx` or `NextMeetingCard.tsx`, goes in the `components` folder of the matching area instead. `src/components` keeps only the pieces every area uses: `ui/`, `layout/`, `errors/`, `auth/`, `PageHero`, `StatusBadge`, `FacebookFeed`, `DonateButton`, `AnnouncementBanner`, `ImpersonationBanner`, `FilterDropdown` and `MultiSelectDropdown`. The rules:
  - Components are imported by their own path, never through an area's `index.ts`. The `index.ts` files stay as step 17 left them and do not re-export components, so code that imports an area's library functions never pulls React components in with them, and a component that is loaded lazily keeps its own chunk.
  - A component that a second area needs moves to `src/components` as a shared piece, or into the area that uses it. A value that is not a component (a constant, a helper or a type) moves into a module of its area that the area's `index.ts` re-exports, and the component file keeps only components, as `react-refresh/only-export-components` asks.
  - The brief's text is left as written.
- **Thin pages (step 19).** A new or rebuilt page composes area components and calls area `api.ts` modules. It does not call `fetch`, and it does no data shaping beyond what that page needs: sorting or filtering for its own view is fine, while shaping that a second screen would need goes in the area. The large existing pages are not split in WS01; each is split up when the workstream that rebuilds it does: `TournamentsPage.tsx` (865 lines) in WS04, `TournamentDetailPage.tsx` (1110) in WS05 and WS06, `TournamentManagePage.tsx` (2379) in WS08 and `ScannerPage.tsx` (1031) in WS15.
- **Area boundaries and the lint baseline (step 19).** Three rules in `eslint.config.js` hold the area layout: a file under `src/areas` may not import `src/pages` (an error); a file in one area imports another area only through that area's `index.ts` or `api.ts` (a warning in WS01); and a page does not call `fetch` (a warning in WS01). The two warnings are warnings so that the imports and calls that existed when the rules came in do not fail the lint run; each is a follow-up, and `test/unit/area-boundaries.test.ts` fails when one is added or removed without the list changing. Once both lists are empty, the two rules become errors.
  - The lint baseline from step 19 on is 34 errors and 13 warnings. The errors are the same 34 as before the step (37 when WS01 began). The warnings rose from 4 to 13 by design: 5 imports across areas and 4 page `fetch` calls, listed under Follow-ups as "Imports across areas (step 19)" and "Pages that call fetch (step 19)".
  - The gate rule for errors is unchanged: the count never rises. A warning count above 13 means a new boundary warning, which `area-boundaries.test.ts` also catches, or a new warning from another rule; either is fixed or listed before the step is committed.

- **Browser test wiring (step 20).** `npm run test:a11y` is the one command for the accessibility suite and is outside the gate: it builds `dist-a11y-on` and `dist-a11y-off` with `vite build --mode a11y` and every `VITE_*` value on the command line (no `.env.a11y` is read, and Vite lets a process value win over a file), applies the migrations and the seed to `.wrangler/a11y`, and serves each build with `wrangler pages dev` on its own port. `VITE_FLAGS` counts only when the mode is `a11y`, the fixture route exists only in that build, and a unit test builds production to prove neither reaches it. Nothing in the suite reaches a real service: the sign-in address is a closed local port, the secrets are fake or bound empty, outside requests are blocked and recorded, and the Facebook feed is stubbed.
- **Known axe violations (step 20).** The smoke spec fails on any axe violation that `test/a11y/known-violations.json` does not list, and reports the ones it lists. The list holds what the current site does wrong and is never added to; step 32 (AC1) fixes each entry and empties the file.
- **The colour baseline (step 20).** The computed colours and fonts of the build with every switch off are committed in `test/a11y/baseline.json`, and every `test:a11y` run compares against it, so a later step that changes an old token fails the run until the change is deliberate (`A11Y_BASELINE=record`).
- **Preview end-to-end tests (step 20).** They never run on a push or in the normal gate. The `e2e-preview` project and `e2e-preview.yml` refuse the live site and any single deployment address, and the early stop in `e2e-preview.yml` and the config check are tested against the same list of spellings. The two specs are `test.fixme` until WS06 and WS07 ship; the person who removes `test.fixme` also confirms the five preconditions.
- **Tokens (step 21).** New code colours through the semantic token utilities (`text-ink`, `bg-ground`, `text-gold-ink`, the status utilities), never through a hex value or a shadcn variable, so each part follows dark mode and the look of its scope. Every token has a value on the plain `:root` equal to today's look, and every rule of the new look starts with `:root[data-look="new"]`, so turning `newLook` off restores today's look in full. Scholastic and heritage keep the base status tokens and the base focus ring; live has its own, dark in either mode.
- **The scope switches need `newLook` (step 21).** The live, scholastic and heritage colours apply only under `:root[data-look="new"]`, so `themeHeritage` and `themeScholastic` take effect only when `newLook` is on as well. A page that sets `data-theme="heritage"` with `newLook` off looks as it does today. The comments on both switches in `src/lib/features.ts` say so.
- **The focus ring on a dark ground (step 21).** A dark ground that is not a `bg-lca-navy` box (a tinted photo, a red or blue banner) carries `data-ground="dark"` on the element that holds its controls, and the ring inside turns gold. A part built on the tokens needs no marker: under `.dark` or the live look `--focus` is already gold.
- **Dark mode and the Appearance control (step 21).** Dark follows the device setting from the moment `newLook` is on, with System, Light and Dark in the current footer; the choice is kept in this browser only. The script that applies it before the first paint is in the page only when the build has `newLook` on.
- **One scope mechanism (step 22).** Every themed area goes through `ThemeScope`; no page sets `data-theme` by hand. Base inside heritage is the only nesting allowed, heritage never renders inside live, and a bad nesting is reported with `console.warn` in every mode but the production build. `routeScope` returns base for every admin, workspace, manage, dashboard, account and board inbox address and for the three redirect addresses, and heritage only for the six pages.
- **JetBrains Mono is not added (step 22).** The Looks-7 tile names it for moves, clocks and ratings, but step 21 made Geist Mono the only mono face on the site in every look. The tile gives ligatures off and tabular figures as its reasons, and Geist Mono is monospaced with no ligatures, so both hold. Big Shoulders Display is not added either; it is for round banners only and arrives with WS07.
- **Ink and grounds (step 23).** Navy text is `text-ink`, which turns light in dark mode, except navy text on an opaque gold fill, which is `text-brand-navy` and stays navy in every look, as the brief's gold button rule asks. A white ground for page chrome or a control is `bg-surface`. A navy band, hero, card header, selected control or badge is `bg-brand-navy`, which stays `#1a2744` in every look and in dark mode, so the white and gold on it keep their contrast; `text-white` on it stays, and a Button or Badge that takes the navy fill through its className names its label as well (`dark:text-white`), because the variant's label is the ground's colour, which is dark in dark mode. A faint navy tint keeps the brand navy at its opacity (`bg-brand-navy/5`) until step 24 moves the tints to the ink with a fallback of their own: a token class with an opacity modifier falls back to its token at full strength in a browser that cannot mix colours. White mats behind logos and around the sign-in QR code stay `bg-white`, and so do translucent white veils on navy. New code follows the same rules; `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.map.json` holds each of them with its reason.
- **The brand navy (step 23).** `--color-brand-navy` is defined once, in the brand `@theme` block, and nothing else sets it; `bg-brand-navy` and `text-brand-navy` read it directly. The focus ring turns gold inside a `bg-brand-navy` box as it does inside a `bg-lca-navy` one, except in a light menu (`bg-popover`) drawn inside the box, whose controls keep the look's own ring.
- **Colour classes move by script and reviewed map (step 23).** A change of colour classes across the pages runs through `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` with a map in which every rule, site and keep entry gives its reason. Each class a map writes must paint the colour it replaces on the plain `:root`, which `test/unit/semantic-map.test.ts` proves, and the script's `--check` holds the TSX files to the map. A later map, such as the status colours of step 24, runs through the same script with `--map`.
- **Status colours (step 24).** New code colours status words with `text-ok` or `text-bad` on a page, a card or a note, and a status tag with its pair, `text-status-open` on `bg-status-open-tint` and so on, never a status word without its tint. The shade tokens step 24 adds (`text-ok-700`, `bg-warn-50`, `border-info-200` and the rest, one for each Tailwind shade a page used) exist so the pages keep today's look with `newLook` off; under the new look each is its tone's words or tint, and new code does not reach for one. A Tailwind palette colour, an `lca-navy` class, `bg-white` in any form or a hex colour class in a TSX file needs a line with its reason and count in `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json`, or a keep entry with its reason in one of the two maps that names its one string; `test/unit/raw-colours.test.ts` enforces it, and the list only shrinks. A keep entry for a whole file or for every file keeps the codemod's check quiet but allows nothing in the ratchet, which counts those uses in the allowlist.
- **A printed page takes the light look (step 24).** The dark look, the live look and heritage in dark mode sit inside `@media screen` in `src/index.css`, so a printed page takes the light look of its scope on any device, and dark words land on the white paper. A new dark or live block goes inside `@media screen` too.
- **Browsers that cannot mix colours (step 24).** The `@supports not (color: color-mix(in lab, red, red))` block in `src/index.css` is the one exception to the rule that every rule of the new look starts with `:root[data-look="new"]`: it applies in either look, and gives each token class with an opacity modifier that a map writes the `#rrggbbaa` tint its old class fell back to, so those browsers draw what they drew before. A map that writes another such form adds its line there, and `semantic-map.test.ts` checks each one.
- **Light islands (step 24).** A ground that stays light in every look, such as the club map's paper, keeps the words on it dark: they take `text-muted-on-light`, or another colour that follows no look such as `text-brand-navy`, and a veil laid over it takes `bg-veil-on-light/30`, never a words colour or ground that follows the look, which would be light on light in dark mode. `test/unit/semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` fails on one, reading from `src/index.css` which colours follow the look, and `test/unit/contrast.test.ts` measures the words on every hex ground in every look.
- **Gold text (step 25).** Brand gold is text only on navy or another ground that stays dark in every look, as `text-lca-gold`. On a light ground a gold icon or gold words take `text-gold-ink`, which follows the look (`#866a1e` in the light looks, `#705718` in heritage, brand gold in dark mode and the live look); dark gold words on a gold tint take `text-gold-ink-badge`, since gold-ink words fall below 4.5:1 on a tint of 20 per cent or more; the light gold of a pill on a dark ground takes `text-gold-on-dark-badge`; and a club's colour as words goes through `clubTextColor`, which gives brand gold, every club's colour until it picks one, the gold-ink token. `text-gold-ink` never sits on navy (2.89:1 in the light look), nor does a badge's dark gold, and a pill's light gold sits only on a dark ground; a component told its ground by an `on` prop is drawn with the `on` its ground names. A ground counts as dark only where brand gold reads 4.5:1 on it: a hex ground by its colour, and a ground marked `data-ground="dark"`, the focus ring's marker, only with a navy of its own, so gold on a darkened photo or a red or blue banner is a person's decision, with the ratio it reads. `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` enforces it, with `test/unit/fixtures/goldAllowlist.ts` for each use its reading cannot place, each with its reason.
- **Ground-aware colour moves (step 25).** A colour move whose right token depends on the ground runs through a script that reads the ground of each use, as `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` does for `text-lca-gold`: it prints a review table of every use, a person reviews every changed line, and a use it cannot place is an allowlist entry with its reason. A move whose token is the same on every ground runs through `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts` with a map, as step 23 decided, which is how the gold hex text classes moved (`scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.gold.map.json`).

### Deviations from the brief

Every deviation planned for WS01 is listed here now, so a checkpoint review sees them all in one place. A line for a step not yet built names that step; when the step lands it confirms or corrects its line.

Recorded in steps 1 to 8:

- **Remote migrations.** The brief (0.2) says K applies remote migrations. In fact `.github/workflows/migrate-db.yml` applies `migrations/**` to `lca-db --remote` on every push to `main` that touches migrations, so merging a checkpoint applies its migrations to production. The preview database is migrated only by K's `db:migrate:preview`, run from the branch before review.
- **Preview id.** The plan was a placeholder for K to paste over; the real id went in directly on K's confirmation.
- **Base branch.** The branch was created from the docs branch that carries the brief and the decided boards, not from `main`. It is rebased onto `main` once that docs PR merges; the content is the same.
- **Mail on previews.** The brief has the Resend key set separately for previews; the decision is no Resend key on previews until K asks for test mail.
- **Brief 0.1, rule 8** ("Preview deployments share the production database") is no longer true once this step merges. The brief's text should be updated with the next brief revision.
- **Seed script.** The brief asks for a seed script with the preview database; it existed but refused until the sample data was written; step 20 wrote it (see step 20).
- **Heritage fonts (brief 0.2).** Heritage loads Libre Caslon Display and Source Serif 4 instead of reusing Instrument Serif. Baloo 2 and Nunito, the Bright Scholastic fonts, move to WS11. WS01 ships the scholastic colour tokens and a fixture page only; `themeScholastic` stays off until WS11.
- **Structural work first.** The brief's WS01 says "Data model: none" and "API: none". K's October 8 structural decisions put steps 3 to 19 (the shared `domain/` folder, Drizzle, contracts, the sections and schedules tables, member pricing, and the folder moves) ahead of the design tokens, so WS01 adds migrations and changes endpoints.
- **Contracts.** K1d is met as a ratchet rather than a contract for all 88 endpoints at once (see Decisions).
- **Page routes (step 7).** The plan named two page routes outside `functions/api` and said `test/integration/preview.test.ts` covers them. There are three: `functions/news/[slug].ts` also serves the HTML shell with a link preview, so it is excluded with the same reason. `preview.test.ts` covers the JSON endpoint `functions/api/tournaments/[id].ts`, not the page routes; no test covers the page routes (see Follow-ups), so the exclusion's reason is that they serve HTML, not that another test checks them.
- **Client types (step 7).** The brief has the site's API types come from the contracts through `import type`. The contract types exist (`TournamentListItem` and the others in `domain/contracts`), and the boundary test allows only type imports, but `src/lib/api.ts` still declared `ApiTournamentListItem` by hand. Done in step 13: the tournament list, detail and section types come from the contracts; the other types in `api.ts` move as their endpoints get contracts.
- **Event-mode phases (step 5)** are a pure module at `domain/events/eventMode.ts` built in WS01, not `functions/utils/eventMode.ts` in WS02. The brief's 2.2 table (the "Per-user involvement endpoint" row) and its WS02 text (Event mode, the `GET /api/me/event-mode` API note and the Frontend "New" list) place the machine at `functions/utils/eventMode.ts`, as do `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase0.md` ("Window and phases" and the 5.5 API notes); K's October 8 decision to share it through `domain/` supersedes that path, so WS03's hero and WS13's event-day view use the same definition. WS02 still builds the endpoint, `src/lib/eventModeCopy.ts` and the strip, and imports this module instead of creating `functions/utils/eventMode.ts`. The brief's `pollSeconds` rule and the sooner-first sort stay with the endpoint, so their tests move to WS02 with it. Also built here: `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule`, `eventEndMs` and `eventModeWindow`, so the endpoint and the client's window gate read the schedule and the window the same way.
- **Event-mode phase times (step 5).** Two rules in `domain/events/eventMode.ts` go against the brief's text. `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase0.md` ("Window and phases") says `eventDay` runs from the first round time "until 11:59 PM", and that `after` runs "from `final` until `eventEnd + 48 hours`", with `eventEnd` at 11:59 PM on `end_date ?? date`. Instead:
  - `eventDay` runs until 6:00 AM the next morning, not 11:59 PM, so a round still being played after midnight keeps its day and the night between the days of a multi-day event has a phase. On the last day this runs past `eventEnd`, until 6:00 AM the next morning.
  - An event that is not final enters `after` at 6:00 AM the morning after its last day (not at 11:59 PM, and not only from `final`) and stays there until the window closes at `eventEnd + 48 hours`, so the people involved keep the strip for the brief's 48 hours while results are still coming in. A final event enters `after` at 11:59 PM on its last day, as the brief says. The wording in `src/lib/eventModeCopy.ts` (WS02) reads `status` and `lastRoundResultsComplete` to choose between the final-standings line and a results-still-coming-in line.
  WS02 and WS13 builders should read the module's doc comment, not the brief, for when `eventDay` ends; `pollSeconds` stays at 20 until 6:00 AM on an event night.
- **Shared folder and formats (step 3).** Brief 2.2 puts the format helpers in `src/lib/format.ts`. They live in `domain/format` instead, imported as `@domain/format` in the site and by relative path in the server functions and workers, and `src/lib/format.ts` re-exports them, so the brief's path still works. zod is allowed only in `domain/contracts` (step 7), so nothing the browser imports from `domain/` brings zod into the site bundle.
- **The daily-emails worker is type checked from step 3,** one step earlier than planned: `npm run typecheck:functions` now includes `workers/daily-emails/src`, so a broken import into the worker fails the checks. It had no type errors. Adding `domain/**` to the paths that trigger `deploy-worker.yml` stays with step 4, when the worker first imports from `domain/`.
- **One definition for shared rules (step 4)** landed as planned: section rules, Central time, pricing, regions and the family size moved into `domain/`, with one-line re-exports at the old paths, and `mirrors.test.ts` is replaced by `domain-shims.test.ts`. `deploy-worker.yml` now also runs on `domain/**`.
- **The zone constant goes the other way (step 4).** The step 3 follow-up planned for `date.ts` to import the moved time module. Instead the moved `centralTime.ts` imports `CENTRAL_TIME_ZONE` and `zonedParts` from `date.ts`, which already held the Central logic, and keeps `LCA_TIME_ZONE` as the same value under its old name. `date.ts` is unchanged.
- **The membership page reads the tier prices (step 4).** The plan allowed `src/pages/MembershipPage.tsx` to keep its own prices unless the change was one line. It is an import plus the four `price:` values, and it is the only way the prices have one definition, so the page now uses `MEMBERSHIP_TIER_PRICES`; its family line also takes "3" from the family size. The page renders the same text.
- **Where the minor name rule is written (step 4).** The plan cited `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase1.md` lines 210, 256, 352 and 394. Lines 210, 256 and 352 state the rule; line 394 is the 8.1 data-model list, whose last sentence repeats it for page 4 ("dependent profiles and members who ticked 'I'm under 18'") and drops `public_minor_names`. Line 36 (4.4) states it too. The file header cites lines 36, 210, 256, 352 and 394, and reads "dependent profile" in line 394 as a player with an active guardian link, which is how lines 210, 256 and 352 put it.
- **Minor display names (step 4)** follow `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase1`: a name is shortened when there is an active guardian link or the entry is marked under 18, not whenever the player is a household dependent.
- **`format-edges.test.ts` lists the new format file (step 4).** Its check that `domain/format` holds exactly the step 3 files now includes `centralTime.ts`.

- **Drizzle (step 6)** landed as planned: drizzle-kit writes into the staging folder `drizzle/` and `npm run db:generate` numbers each file into `migrations/`, so wrangler, `migrate-db.yml` and the test setup are unchanged. `migration-safety.test.ts` reads drizzle-kit's quoted names, `ON UPDATE` clauses and `ADD` without `COLUMN`, and the splitter tracks `BEGIN`, `CASE` and `END`.
- **Drizzle version (step 6).** The plan named 0.45.x; 0.45.4 was published on October 8, under two weeks old, so `drizzle-orm` is pinned at 0.45.3 (September 21).
- **Drizzle baseline (step 6).** `drizzle-kit pull` got several things wrong, so `functions/db/schema.ts` and the committed `drizzle/meta/0000_snapshot.json` were corrected by hand to match the database the migrations build (the drift test proves it):
  - It attached all 16 CHECK constraints it found to every table, each missing its closing bracket. CHECK constraints are left out of the schema and the snapshot; they stay in the migrations.
  - It wrote SQL defaults as quoted strings (``"sql`(datetime('now'))`"``), which would have made the text the default. They are ``sql`...` `` defaults again, `(lower(hex(randomblob(8))))` included, so expression defaults are modelled rather than left out as planned.
  - It dropped `DEFAULT 0` on `entry_fee`, `early_discount`, `late_fee` and `member_discount`, the `DESC` on five index columns and the `WHERE ended_at IS NULL` of `idx_seat_member_current`. All are restored.
  - Inline `UNIQUE` constraints (`lca_posts.slug` and three multi-column ones) are not modelled: SQLite backs them with unnamed indexes, and declaring them would make drizzle-kit believe in named indexes that do not exist.
  - Its snapshot marked lone `TEXT PRIMARY KEY` columns as nullable (true in SQLite unless they say `NOT NULL`), which Drizzle cannot express, so the first `generate` wanted to rebuild every table. The snapshot now marks them `NOT NULL`, as the schema does.
  - Relation names were made plain: `tournaments.creator`, `members.guardian` and `members.dependents`, `supportMessages.loggedByMember`, `emailCampaigns.creator`. 0/1 flags stay integers and dates stay text, as every query reads them.
- **drizzle-kit drops foreign key actions on added columns (step 6).** For `ALTER TABLE ... ADD ... REFERENCES`, drizzle-kit 0.31.11 writes no `ON DELETE` or `ON UPDATE`, while its snapshot records them. `db:generate` refuses such a file, which the brief did not ask for. The `alter-add-fk.sql` fixture is drizzle-kit's text with ` ON DELETE cascade` written back by hand, since drizzle-kit never writes that form; its header says so.
- **drizzle-kit exits 0 when it cannot ask about a rename (step 6).** When a column or table goes and another arrives, drizzle-kit 0.31.11 asks whether it was renamed. Without a terminal it prints "Error: Interactive prompts require a TTY terminal", writes nothing and still exits 0. `db:generate` treats any output line starting with `Error:` as a failure (it writes nothing, restores `drizzle/` and says to run it in a terminal), and from a terminal it hands drizzle-kit stdin and stdout so the question can be answered, capturing only stderr. Covered in `db-generate-guard.test.ts` (a stand-in that exits 0 with an `Error:` line) and `db-generate-real-kit.test.ts` (the real drizzle-kit on `clubs.longitude` renamed to `lng`); checked by hand under a pseudo-terminal, where the prompt appeared and the answered file was written.
- **`--custom` keeps the new schema (step 6).** drizzle-kit's own `--custom` copies the previous snapshot, so after a hand-written schema change the next run would offer the same change again. When the schema changed, `db:generate --custom` keeps drizzle-kit's snapshot of the new schema and puts its proposed SQL in the file as comments; with no schema change it writes an empty file for a data migration.
- **Wider checks than planned (step 6).** The drift test also compares indexes and foreign keys, and the splitter also skips over double-quoted and backtick identifiers. `BEGIN` opens a block only inside `CREATE TRIGGER`, so a stray `BEGIN TRANSACTION;` still ends at its `;`. `getDb` passes the relations with the tables so relational queries work. `npm run db:local-sqlite` is a new script.

- **Sections and schedules tables (step 8)** landed as planned: `tournament_sections` (with `fee_regular`, optional `fee_early` and `fee_late`, and a cap), `tournament_schedules`, `tournament_schedule_rounds`, and `section_id` and `schedule_id` on registrations replace `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase1` 8.1's schedules JSON column, `tournaments.merge_round` and the cap inside the JSON. Early and late prices are worked out when read, from the tournament's own deadline and fee columns, unless a section sets its own. One correction to the planned line: the JSON columns kept are `tournaments.sections` and `tournaments.round_schedule`; there is no `schedules` column. Both stay in today's exact shape and nothing is dropped. `tournament_games` keeps section names in WS01; a `section_id` there is left for WS07.
- **The sync trigger stays past checkpoint B (step 8)**, as planned: `tournaments_sections_sync_update` copies sections and rounds from the JSON columns into the tables and stays until the JSON columns are retired, because dropping it at a merge would reopen the window during a deploy when old code still writes only the JSON. It never touches caps, `fee_early`, `fee_late` or ids, and agrees with the single writer (which writes the table first and the JSON last).
- **One copy of the sync (step 8).** The brief lists the backfill and the tournament insert trigger as their own statements. Both run the update trigger instead: the backfill with `UPDATE tournaments SET sections = sections`, the insert trigger by setting `sections` to the inserted value. A first version kept the JSON reading in two views; `drizzle-kit pull` stops on a view written over several lines ("Could not process view"), so the views were dropped before anything was committed.
- **Archived rows come back (step 8).** When old code puts back a name it removed, the sync revives the latest archived row of that name rather than adding a new one, so entries left under that name stay with their section. The brief's list says only "insert missing live names".
- **No entry is left without a section (step 8).** The brief has the entry trigger fill `section_id` "by live name". When no live row has the name, the trigger uses the latest archived row of that name, and creates an archived row when there is none, as the backfill does for names the JSON lacks.
- **Rounds are replaced only when `round_schedule` changes (step 8).** The admin PATCH rewrites `round_schedule` on every save; the trigger replaces the primary rounds only when the value differs (and is null or a valid array), or when the primary schedule has none, so an edit that leaves the rounds alone never rewrites them.
- **A schedule's time control is null (step 8).** The backfill leaves `tournament_schedules.time_control` null, meaning the tournament's own, rather than copying `tournaments.time_control`, which the trigger does not follow.
- **What the backfill keeps (step 8).** A known key goes to its column only when its JSON type is the expected one (a number for fees and limits, text for `prizeFund`, true or false for the flags, an object or array for `prizes`); anything else stays in `extra_json`. An element without a text name, or with an empty one, is skipped. Names are not trimmed.
- **Extra indexes (step 8).** Beyond the two partial unique indexes the brief names, both new tables index `tournament_id` and registrations index `section_id` and `schedule_id`.
- **Files beyond the step's list (step 8).** `drizzle/meta/0002_snapshot.json` (written by `--custom`), `functions/db/relations.ts`, `migrations/README.md`, and the pinned counts in `schema-drift.test.ts`, `split-sql.test.ts`, `split-sql-edges.test.ts`, `drizzle-baseline.test.ts`, `db-generate-guard.test.ts` and `db-generate-real-kit.test.ts`.

Recorded in step 9:

- **Drizzle conversion rule (steps 6 to 19).** The brief has queries move to Drizzle; WS01 converts a query only when a step changes its SQL or the values it binds. A query whose rows are only handled differently afterwards stays as plain D1. When a converted statement shares a batch, the whole batch moves to Drizzle's `db.batch`. Step 9 is the first use: in the admin PATCH the tournament `SELECT` (its values now feed a typed Drizzle update) and the `UPDATE` (it no longer writes `sections`) became Drizzle and share one `db.batch` with the section writes; in the admin POST the `INSERT` (it no longer names `sections`, which takes the column's default `'[]'`) became Drizzle in one `db.batch` with the section writes. The unchanged lookups (the club check, the taken id, the `SELECT *` that answers) stay plain D1.
- **The section request schema is `sectionSchema`** (repo naming), not `SectionSchema` as the brief writes it. Its name limit is the literal 80, because contracts import only zod and their own files; `SECTION_NAME_MAX` in `domain/events/sections.ts` is the same 80 and `sections-domain.test.ts` holds them equal.
- **Archived rows come back by name in the writer too.** The brief's match is "by id, else by live name". A section sent without an id whose name has no live row takes the latest archived row of that name, as the 0053 trigger does, so entries left under that name are with their section again and the trigger never has to.
- **Games, as well as entries, stop a removal (step 9).** Decision 7 speaks of entries. A section that has games is refused too, because games are matched by name and an archived name would leave them pointing at nothing live.
- **Games and the report settings key follow a rename only from a live row.** They are keyed by name; a row that was archived does not own its name, so reviving and renaming it moves its entries (by `section_id`) but not games or report settings under that name.
- **The PATCH answers with the section rows too, priced.** The brief puts the new answer (sections from the table, ids included) on the POST. The PATCH answered with the JSON parsed; it now answers the same way as the POST, under the same contract. Each section takes the shape step 12 plans for every reader (today's field names plus `id`, `cap` and `fees { regular, early, late }` from `tierFees`, with `entryFee` the regular price), so step 12 reuses `savedSectionSchema` rather than changing these answers again. The site ignores both answers apart from the wizard's `id`.
- **The PATCH no longer rewrites `sections` when none are sent.** It used to write the JSON back on every save. Now the JSON column is written only by the writer, and only when `sections` is sent (`null` still means "keep").
- **Renaming from today's setup form.** The form sends no ids, so a rename there is a removal and an addition. A section with entries can no longer be renamed that way: the save is refused with the decision 7 message. The old behaviour left those entries under a name the event no longer had. Renaming keeps entries once the setup sends ids (step 13). (Step 13: the manage page sends each loaded section with its id; neither screen has a rename box yet, see the step 13 deviations.)
- **Request contracts reject what the form never sends.** Both handlers moved from `parseJsonBody` to `parseBody`, so a body that is not the right shape (for example `sections` that is not a list, or a section name over 80 characters) is now 400 with `{ error, fields }` instead of being stored. What the manage page sends back from a stored list is still taken: a bare name as a list element (very old events stored sections that way), and a known key holding a value of another type (`entryFee: "40"`, `prizeFund: 700`), which goes to `extra_json` as the 0053 trigger puts it. A known key holding a value of its column's type must be valid for it (a negative `entryFee`, or `prizes` that is a list, is refused), as must `id`, `cap` and `fees`, which are new. Fields the handlers treat as optional stay optional and accept null, and the handlers keep their own messages for a missing name, location, date or entry fee and for an unknown status.
- **No switch for the new refusals (step 9).** Brief 0.2 puts every visible change behind a switch. The server now refuses repeated section names and removing a section that has entries or games, and the manage page shows the message in its usual error line. These are rules on the data, enforced where the data is written, and a switch in `src/lib/features.ts` cannot reach the server, so they ship without one.
- **Files beyond the step's list.** `test/unit/schema-drift.test.ts` loses its "no runtime code imports `functions/db` yet" check (the step 6 follow-up said the first step to use Drizzle removes it); `test/unit/contract-coverage.test.ts` gives its synthetic cases only the tournaments contract, since the scratch folder holds only that handler; `test/integration/sections-triggers.test.ts` and `sections-triggers-edges.test.ts` read the POST's new answer and, where a section with an entry was removed, withdraw the entry first (decision 7); the header of `test/integration/drizzle-smoke.test.ts` no longer says no endpoint uses Drizzle.

Recorded in step 10:

- **`priceEntry` takes the section as read (step 10).** The brief has `priceEntry(section, tournament, now, opts)` take the table row (`fee_regular`, `fee_early`, `fee_late`). It takes a section as `loadSections` returns it (`feeRegular`, `feeEarly`, `feeLate`, the Drizzle names of those columns), and `now` is in milliseconds, as the `nowMs` of `entryPrice` was.
- **Malformed bodies answer in the `{ error, fields }` shape (step 10).** The three handlers moved from `parseJsonBody` to `parseBody`. A body that is not JSON answers "Invalid JSON body"; a body missing `tournamentId` or `section`, or with a value of the wrong type, answers "Some fields are missing or not valid." with a message per field ("Choose a tournament.", "Choose a section."), where the handlers said "tournamentId and section are required" (or "tournamentId and at least one entry are required" for a family entry). An empty list of players in a family entry now reads "Choose at least one player." The site always sends these fields, so a player does not normally see any of this.
- **An unknown payment status is refused before the role check (step 10).** A `paymentStatus` outside pending, paid and refunded is now 400 from the contract for anyone allowed to act on the entry; the player who owns the entry used to get 403 ("Only a tournament manager can change payment status") because the role check came first. A valid status from someone who is not a manager is still 403, and a stranger is still 403 before the body is read.
- **A section fee written as text charges the event fee (step 10).** The server took `entryFee` from the JSON as it was, so a fee saved as text (such as `"40"`) was added as text: $400 with no discount or fee lines, and not a number at all with one. Since step 8 the backfill keeps such a value in `extra_json` and leaves `fee_regular` null; the readers now use the row, so that section charges the tournament's `entry_fee`, and `entryPrice` on the pages reads only a numeric `entryFee`, so the page shows the same price. Step 8's production query 5 lists any such section.
- **Plain-language messages (step 10).** Two refusals said "USCF ID"; they now say "US Chess ID" ("A US Chess ID is required to enter a rated tournament.", and in a family entry the player's name followed by "needs a US Chess ID to enter a rated tournament."). The warning about a US Chess membership that runs out before the event writes its date with the weekday ("Tue, Sep 1, 2026") instead of "2026-09-01". These follow brief 0.1's plain-language rule and come with no switch, as the step 9 refusals did.
- **A move writes the entry and its payment together (step 10).** Repricing a pending payment on a section move was its own write, made before the entry's update; both are now in one batch, so a failed update leaves the payment as it was.
- **Files beyond the step's list (step 10).** `domain/contracts/index.ts` exports the new contract file. Besides the three test files the plan named, `test/unit/registration-readers.test.ts` (the code scan and the request bodies from `src/lib/api.ts`), `test/integration/registration-section-reads.test.ts`, `registration-plain-language.test.ts` and `registration-replays-edges.test.ts` were added.

Recorded in step 11:

- **Malformed bodies answer in the `{ error, fields }` shape (step 11).** Both handlers moved from `parseJsonBody` to `parseBody`, as step 10's did. A walk-in without a name (or with only spaces) or a section answers "Some fields are missing or not valid." with "Enter the player's name." or "Choose a section." where it said "fullName and section are required"; a rating that is not a number reads "Enter the rating as a number, or leave it blank." where it said "uscfRating must be a number"; a waitlist offer without `registrationId` reads "Choose a player on the waitlist." where it said "registrationId is required". A `uscfId` that is not text, or a `markPaid` that is not true or false, is now 400 too; before, the first failed with a server error and the second counted as paid. The manage page never sends any of these.
- **Plain-language messages (step 11).** The walk-in refusals now say "US Chess ID": "A US Chess ID is required for walk-ins to rated tournaments." and "A player with this US Chess ID is already registered." (409). Neither handler shows a date. These come with no switch, as in steps 9 and 10.
- **The waitlist reads a section's own early and late price (step 11).** `priceEntry` gets the whole row, so a section's own `fee_early` or `fee_late` applies to an offered spot, as it does to an online entry since step 10. The JSON never held those prices, so for every section that exists today the amount is the one `entryPrice` gave (36 combinations compared in the contract test). No row has them until WS08.
- **A waitlisted entry in a section archived since keeps that section's fee (step 11).** `entryPrice` charged the event fee for a section the JSON no longer listed. The handler now finds the entry's own row, archived or not, and charges its `fee_regular`. Decision 7 refuses to remove a section that has waitlisted entries, so this happens only when a row is archived by hand.
- **Files beyond the step's list (step 11).** `test/integration/registration-section-ids.test.ts` (two cases with the entry triggers dropped) and `test/unit/registration-readers.test.ts` (the code scan and the request bodies from `src/lib/api.ts`) were extended rather than copied into a new file, since the integration pool cannot read source files.

Recorded in step 12:

- **Shape changes no page reads (step 12).** `round_schedule` is a list (not text) in the list, the admin PATCH and POST and the registration PATCH answers. The club page's sections went from a JSON string to objects. A bare-name section is now an object at the event fee (the client already accepted both). `custom_details` stays text on the list because the wizard parses it, and is a list on the detail and manage answers. All sections gain `id`, `cap` and `fees`.
- **Registration settings PATCH answer and validation (step 12).** It answers the tournament through `toTournamentResponse` instead of the raw row with JSON strings; its only caller, `updateTournamentRegistration` in `src/lib/api.ts`, returns `Promise<void>`. A malformed body now answers 400 with `{ error, fields }`; an invalid status used to fail at the database check. Shipped without a flag.
- **US Chess wording and weekday dates (step 12).** The rating report says "US Chess" instead of "USCF", and the membership expiry date shows its weekday through the domain formatter ("Mon, Aug 31, 2026"; "Sun, Nov 1, 2026" around the clock change). Shipped without a flag, as in steps 10 and 11.
- **Files beyond the step's list (step 12).** `functions/api/admin/tournaments.ts` (POST uses `toTournamentResponse`), `domain/contracts/clubs.ts` (new), `domain/contracts/index.ts`, `domain/events/sections.ts` (`sectionFeeOverrides`), and `test/integration/contracts/tournaments-readers.test.ts` (tester's file). `parseTournamentSections` is removed rather than moved.
- **Flag comments.** No flag was added or changed, so `src/lib/features.ts` is untouched.

Recorded in step 13:

- **The section type is `savedSectionSchema` (step 13).** The plan said `tournamentSectionSchema` carries `id`, `cap` and `fees`. It does not: it is the strict shape of an element of the stored JSON. The answer shape with the id, cap and prices is `savedSectionSchema`, so `ApiTournamentSection` is its type (`SavedSection`). Its rule fields (`ratingMax`, `ratingMin`, `gradeMin`, `gradeMax`) are `number | null` and `rulesSet` a boolean, always present in an answer, where the old hand-written type had them optional; `prizeFund`, `unratedOk` and `prizes` stay optional, and the loose part of the schema (stored extra keys) gives the type an index signature. Pages read those fields as before; the editing screens use `ApiSectionDraft`, which keeps them optional.
- **No rename box (step 13).** The plan has a rename made in the wizard or the manage page keep its entries. Neither screen lets a director type a new name today (the name is plain text and rows are keyed by it), and the manage page already sent its loaded sections back with their ids. So the step makes the id part of the type the pages edit and send, and the rename test sends the body the manage page builds with one name changed. A rename box is left to the workstream that redesigns the setup screens.
- **The pages price through `priceShownSection`, not `priceEntry` directly (step 13).** The plan says the pages pass "the section object from the response" to `priceEntry`. The response's `fees.early` and `fees.late` mix the section's own prices with ones worked out from the tournament's columns, and `priceEntry` treats any tier price it is given as the section's own (it replaces the line's amount, where a worked-out early price is also clamped at $0). Passed straight through, a page would differ from checkout when the early discount is larger than the fee and the late fee is due; passing only `fees.regular` would differ whenever a section has its own tier price. `priceShownSection` tells the two apart by comparing each with what the tournament alone gives, and passes on only the section's own. An own early price of $0 where the discount is larger than the fee reads the same as a worked-out one and is taken as worked out (see Follow-ups).
- **The price is read for the time the page opened (fixed in checkpoint B review) (step 13).** `entryPrice` read the clock on each render, inside the library. A page that calls `priceEntry` itself must pass the time, and reading the clock during render is a lint error (`react-hooks/purity`), so the tournament page and the family panel keep the time in state from when they open. A page left open across the early deadline or the late date shows the earlier price until it is reloaded; checkout prices the entry again on the server, so the amount charged is unchanged. **Fixed in the checkpoint B review:** a small hook, `src/hooks/useNow.ts`, now keeps the time in state. It reads the clock only when it starts, refreshes every 30 seconds and whenever the tab comes back into view, the window regains focus or the page is restored from the back-forward cache, clears its timer and listeners when the page closes, and returns a refresh function. The tournament page refreshes the time when the event loads (which also covers a change of event in the address), when the section changes, and just before the confirmation dialog opens. The family panel refreshes it when the children load and on every change of a tick box, section, grade or bye. The prices shown therefore follow the early deadline and the late date as checkout does, and can lag by at most one timer period. Tests: 9 fake-clock cases in `test/unit/tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx` (past the early deadline, past the late date, a change of event, the timer alone, the family total, and a tab coming back into view or a restored page); with the hook swapped for a frozen-clock version all 8 timer cases fail, and without the listeners the ninth fails. One small gap remains and is not blocking: the family button and Confirm in an already open dialog act on the amount on screen, which in a visible tab can be up to 30 seconds old at the deadline minute. The amount charged is always the server's, and Stripe shows it before payment.
- **Check on the dev server with a local sign-in (step 13).** The dev server cannot reach Supabase from here, so sign-in went to a local stand-in that accepts the same tokens the integration harness uses, with the site built against it. Everything else (the Pages Functions, the local D1, the built pages) was the real thing.
- **Files beyond the step's list (step 13).** `src/components/tournaments/SectionRulesEditor.tsx` and `PrizesEditor.tsx` (they take and return the edited section, now `ApiSectionDraft`), `src/lib/scholastic.ts`, `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts`, `src/pages/ScholasticPage.tsx` and `src/pages/TournamentsPage.tsx` (bare-name branches removed, types from `api.ts`), `test/unit/check-bundle.test.ts`, `test/unit/client-sections-audit.test.ts`, `test/unit/api-section-bodies.test.ts`, `test/unit/tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx` and `test/integration/sections-client-readers.test.ts` (new), `test/integration/domain-shared-rules.test.ts` and `test/integration/contracts/walkins-waitlist.test.ts` (they called `entryPrice`), and a comment in `test/unit/sections-reader-audit.test.ts` (the site no longer calls `normalizeLegacySections`). `src/lib/pricing.ts` and `functions/utils/pricing.ts`, on the list, needed no edit.

Planned for later steps:

- **Member pricing ships without a switch (step 15).** This is an exception to brief 0.2's rule that every visible change ships behind a switch. The member-discount input on the tournament setup form, the "LCA members save" line and the member price line on the tournament page and the family panel are removed outright, because keeping them would advertise a price that is not charged. `tournaments.member_discount` stays in the database but is neither read nor written. Done in step 15, with the pre-merge production query still not run.
- **Folder moves (steps 16 to 19).** Files under `functions/api` do not move, because their paths are the URLs. Server helpers are grouped under `functions/utils/<area>/` and site code under `src/areas/<area>/`, using K's seven areas plus governance; shared platform files stay where they are. The scanner code (`functions/utils/scan`, `src/lib/scanner`) stays put so its own release checks are not set off.
- **API client (step 18).** `src/lib/api.ts` is split by area but keeps re-exporting every name, so no importer changes in WS01. The route audit is widened to every area's `api.ts`.
- **Big pages are not broken up in WS01 (step 19).** WS01 moves components and library code and adds lint rules for the area boundaries (imports across areas warn rather than fail). The large existing pages are broken up when WS04, WS05, WS06, WS08 and WS15 rebuild them (`TournamentsPage.tsx` is WS04's; see step 19 below). Done in step 19, where a page calling `fetch` warns as well.
- **Browser tests (step 20).** The accessibility suite runs against a local build on seeded local data, with switches turned on through `VITE_FLAGS` only in that mode; the live site ignores the override, and a test proves it. The colours and fonts of the build with every switch off are recorded once, and later steps must not change them. The family registration and round publishing end-to-end tests are written as pending specs with a preview workflow started by hand; they can run only once WS06 and WS07 exist and the preview database is migrated and seeded. Step 20 confirms this; the build-and-seed order and the other differences are under Deviations, step 20.
- **Mono figures and the focus ring (step 21).** Geist Mono applies only under the new look; with `newLook` off, monospace text keeps the device's own font, and the old focus token draws today's ring exactly. Done in step 21 (the Badge keeps its own ring; see Step 21 below).
- **Appearance control and the rook mark (steps 21 and 28).** Until WS02 rebuilds the footer, the Appearance control (System, Light, Dark) sits in the current footer behind `newLook`, and the script that applies the choice before the page paints is included only when `newLook` is on (done in step 21). The current header shows the rook mark under `newLook`. The full-colour logo goes on `/about`; the AC10 placements whose pages do not exist yet (the identity band, membership card, certificates, the print kit cover and the Champions page) wait for WS03, WS10 and WS12.
- **ScrollPanel (step 26).** Added from K's October 8 feedback: a scroll panel and a bounded table scroll area, so long screens such as tournament setup stay together. Each page adopts them when its own workstream rebuilds it.
- **White and Black markers (step 27)** in result cells are drawn as small images, because the Geist Mono file has no ○ or ● characters.
- **Homepage hero (step 29).** With `newLook` on, the homepage hero shows one still, responsive picture instead of the slideshow. WS03 replaces the hero entirely.
- **`hero.png` is left out of the image pipeline (step 29)**, because nothing uses it.
- **AC7's Lighthouse score is recorded, not enforced (step 29).** The mobile score of 90 or more is a lab measurement (`npm run perf:home`, plus K's run on the preview) written down here. The 200 KB limit on the largest image is enforced by a test.
- **Small caps (step 30)** use uppercase at 0.85em, because the Source Serif 4 and Libre Caslon Display files have no true small capitals. Source Serif 4 uses its weight-only file (51 KB) rather than the optical-size one (122 KB), to stay under the 110 KB font budget.
- **AC13 compares screenshots from the same run (step 31)**, base against heritage, rather than against stored reference images.
- **AC1's live and heritage runs (step 32)** set the look on each page's `<main>` inside the test, since few routes use those looks in WS01.
- **Role-safety tests (steps 9 to 15).** WS01 adds no new endpoints, so no new-endpoint role-safety tests are needed. (Step 9 added them for the admin create and edit: `sections-writer.test.ts` and `contracts/admin-tournaments.test.ts`. Step 10 added them for the single entry, the family entry and the entry edit: `contracts/registrations.test.ts` and `registration-replays-edges.test.ts`. Step 11 added them for walk-ins and the waitlist: `contracts/walkins-waitlist.test.ts`.) The endpoints it changes gain role-safety cases instead: the admin tournament create and edit (sections and `requires_lca_membership`, including the assigned director), walk-ins and the waitlist.
- **Schedules (step 14).**
  - Contract names follow the repo: `scheduleSchema`, `scheduleRoundSchema`, `scheduleInputSchema`.
  - A null date or time in a schedule row is answered as `''`, the form's blank, so `ApiRoundScheduleItem` stays `{ round, date: string, time: string }` and the pages compile unchanged. Times stay free text.
  - The JSON mirror is written from the normalised rows, not the raw body: extra keys are dropped, rounds are sorted, a round sent as `"2"` becomes 2, and a repeated or invalid round is dropped. `null` is still stored as the text `'null'`, as before.
  - `schedules` appears in every tournament answer, the list and the create included.
  - New refusals with no flag (only the API can reach them until WS08): both `roundSchedule` and `schedules` sent together; removing a schedule that has entries that are not withdrawn; lowering the round count below a second schedule's merge round (400 up front, 409 if it races).
  - Three handlers outside the step's file list changed because `toTournamentResponse` stopped parsing the column: `registration.ts`, `functions/api/admin/tournaments.ts` and `functions/api/tournaments.ts`. Two field-count tests outside the list now count `schedules`.
  - Review fix: an unsent round count is left out of a plain edit rather than guarding every edit, and the fit guard reads the round count inside the batch (see step 14).
  - A request that makes a new primary with no label while the old primary is demoted gets a duplicate-name 400, because both end up named "Main schedule". WS08's setup must send labels when it switches the primary.

- **Step 15.**
  - `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` changed although the step did not list it: "discounted tournament entry" now reads "entry to LCA-run tournaments across the state", without a switch, because the old words advertised a discount that is no longer charged. The reworded line also describes a rule that is not live yet, since a non-member can still enter an LCA-run event until WS06; it matches decision D12.

  - `PATCH` writes `requires_lca_membership` only when the body sends it or the club is taken off (forced to 1), unlike most columns it rewrites from the row it read.
  - Moving an LCA-run event to a club keeps the value 1 rather than resetting it to 0; a rep or an admin switches it off afterwards.

  - The spec calls the shared helper `entryPrice`; the code keeps `priceEntry` and `priceShownSection`, as the step's brief says.
  - `REDESIGN_SPEC.md` has decisions D11 and D12 as table rows (around lines 2024 and 2025), so `grep '^- \*\*D1[12]'` matches nothing; read them there.

- **Step 16.**
  - No `households/` folder. The plan put `family` there, but `functions/utils/family.ts` is not a one-line re-export: it holds the database helpers `listChildren`, `canActFor` and `syncFamilyCoverage` and re-exports one constant from `domain/households/family`. It stays at the root until a second household helper exists on the server (see Follow-ups). `membershipActivation` imports it as `../family`.

Three tests name a moved file in a string, which the mover does not touch, and were updated by hand: `sections-reader-audit.test.ts` (`events/tournament-manage.ts` and `events/prizes.ts` in the list of scanned files), `no-member-price-copy.test.ts` (`registration/registrationEmails.ts`, and its header comment) and `test-setup.test.ts` (the swiss include it pins). `verify-move` shows each as a moved-path line. `contract-coverage.test.ts` keeps `'../../utils/membershipActivation'` inside a line of sample text given to `exportedMethods`; the path is never resolved, so it was left alone.
