Before that branch Tailwind writes a fallback for browsers that cannot mix colours: Safari 15.4 to 16.1, which reads the `oklch()` colours of the plain `:root`, and older ones such as Chrome 109 on Windows 7 and 8.1. A brand colour falls back to its worked-out sRGB tint, exactly as the class it replaced did, which is why the navy tints stay `bg-brand-navy`. A token class falls back to its token at full strength, so in those browsers the two token forms with a modifier differ from before: `text-ink/80` (a post's date on `/news` and a child entry's "For" line on the dashboard) is full navy instead of 80 per cent, and `bg-surface/90` (the scanner's remove button over a photo) is solid white instead of 90 per cent. Both only raise the contrast, and `semantic-map.test.ts` allows no other token form with a modifier.

  - The plan's guard fails on `text-lca-gold`, the brand gold hex in a text class, a style colour of `#c8a94a` or `LCA.gold`, `#705718` and `#866a1e`. It also fails on brand gold as an icon's paint and as a token's variable in a style, and holds the badge and pill tokens to their kind of ground and a component told its ground by `on` to the ground it is drawn on, with `GOLD_BADGE_ALLOWLIST` in the fixture for the 12 badge and pill uses its reading cannot place; and `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` reads a colour given two hex digits of alpha as a tint, which places the admin's colour preview on its card.
  - Files beyond the step's list: `src/areas/clubs/clubColors.ts`, `scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.gold.map.json`, and the tests `test/unit/gold-ink.test.ts`, `test/unit/news-pinned.test.tsx`, `test/unit/news-pinned-off.test.tsx`, `test/unit/news-pinned-flag.test.tsx`, `test/unit/reminder-bell.test.tsx`, `test/unit/gold-icons-words.test.ts`, `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts`, `test/unit/raw-colours.test.ts`, `test/unit/dark-spot-later.test.ts`, `test/unit/redesign-status-step24.test.ts`, `test/unit/redesign-status-step25.test.ts` and `test/a11y/dark-spot.spec.ts`.

### Verify items resolved

- **wrangler.toml has no preview override.** Confirmed before the change: one `[[d1_databases]]` (`DB` to `lca-db`, `e891b93f-e7fe-48d0-9afc-8570d805ef08`), `[vars]` with ten keys (`SITE_URL`, `SUPABASE_URL`, `FROM_EMAIL`, `CONTACT_EMAIL`, `SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `REPLY_TO_EMAIL`, `VITE_SITE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `VITE_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`), `[[r2_buckets]]` `CLUB_LOGOS` to `lca-club-logos`, and no `[env.*]` block. No workflow deploys the Pages site (only the daily-emails worker has a deploy job), which fits Pages deploying through its Git integration with `wrangler.toml` as the binding source; that part cannot be confirmed from the repo.
- **Pages environment inheritance.** Per Cloudflare's Pages configuration docs, `vars`, `d1_databases` and `r2_buckets` are not inherited by an environment, so `[env.preview]` restates all of them. This is from the docs, not checkable in the repo; `test/unit/wrangler-config.test.ts` holds the restatement in place. wrangler 4.112 accepts the block: against a scratch copy of the config with `--local`, `--env preview` finds `lca-db-preview` and cannot find `lca-db` at all.
- **Does the preview database exist.** K created it on October 8 and confirmed the id. This container is not logged in to Cloudflare and cannot check it.
- **Who applies remote migrations.** `.github/workflows/migrate-db.yml` (push to `main` touching `migrations/**`, or manual run) lists and then applies migrations to `lca-db --remote`. Recorded as a deviation above. Nothing in any workflow targets `lca-db-preview`.
- **Workers.** `workers/daily-emails` binds production `lca-db` and deploys only from `main` (`.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml`). `workers/clearinghouse-sync` binds production `lca-db` and imports nothing from `functions/`. Neither is affected by the preview block.
- **Event-mode phase definitions (step 5).** Confirmed: the brief's WS02 section defines the Phase 0 predicates and the WS07 phases. Its Event mode paragraph names the nine phases, marks `roundPosted`, `roundInProgress` and `between` as WS07 and `eventDay` as Phase 0 only, and defines `eventEnd`; the `GET /api/me/event-mode` note in `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase0.md` (5.5) states the Phase 0 predicates and the key format (`${tournamentId}:${phase}:${round ?? 0}`), and its "Window and phases" section says the same. Brief 2.2 places the machine at `functions/utils/eventMode.ts`, which K's `domain/` decision supersedes (see Deviations). Neither `functions/utils/eventMode.ts` nor `functions/api/me/event-mode.ts` existed, and nothing in the code read a phase before this step. The data the endpoint will pass in exists: `tournaments.date`, `end_date` (nullable), `status` (`upcoming`, `active`, `completed`) and `round_schedule` (JSON items `{ round, date, time }` from date and time inputs, where `date` and `time` can be blank). `round_publications` and `live_state` do not exist yet (WS07).
- **Phase 0 switch list (step 2).** Brief 0.2 lists 15 switches to add, all off, and the names match the list in step 2 exactly. Before the change `src/lib/features.ts` held `clubTournaments`, `tournamentQuickFilters` and `externalTags`, all off, and only `src/pages/TournamentsPage.tsx` reads them.
- **Unit tests and the `@` alias (step 2).** Confirmed: `vitest.config.ts` had no alias and collected only `functions/utils/swiss/**/*.test.ts`, `src/lib/scanner/**/*.test.ts` and `test/unit/**/*.test.ts`, so no `.tsx` test could run, and the unit tests reached `src/` by relative paths (for example `mirrors.test.ts`). The alias and the `.tsx` pattern are added; existing tests keep their relative imports.
- **JSX in test files (step 2).** Confirmed: the root `tsconfig.json` holds only references, `tsconfig.app.json` includes only `src`, and `tsconfig.functions.json` includes only `functions`, so `test/` is in no tsconfig and nothing there sets the JSX runtime. In practice, with vite 8 and vitest 4.1.10, the smoke test also passes without the React plugin, because the default compiler already uses the automatic runtime. The plugin is added anyway, so tests compile JSX the same way the site build does rather than relying on a default.
- **Shared folder wiring (step 3).** Confirmed before the change: only `@/*` to `src/` existed (`tsconfig.app.json`, `vite.config.ts` and `vitest.config.ts`); `tsconfig.functions.json` included only `functions`, with no `paths` and no DOM library; `workers/daily-emails/src/index.ts` already imports `../../../functions/utils/*` by relative path, and its tsconfig included `src/**/*.ts` (the worker's whole source) plus `../../functions/utils/email.ts`. `eslint.config.js` was one block giving every TypeScript file the browser globals. No gate command checked the worker's types before; with it included, `npm run typecheck:functions` still passes.
- **ESLint and browser globals (step 3).** Confirmed: typescript-eslint's recommended config turns `no-undef` off for TypeScript, and flat-config globals merge rather than replace, so taking the browser globals away from `domain/` would never fail. `no-restricted-globals` is what fails on `window` and `document`, and the boundary test proves it.
- **`src/lib/format.ts` (step 3).** Confirmed it did not exist, and no `formatScore`, `formatTime` or `formatTimeControl` existed anywhere. The only `formatDate` was in `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts` (month, day and year, no weekday), used only by `src/pages/HomePage.tsx`; it is left alone (see Follow-ups).
- **Which helpers were copied (step 4).** Confirmed: `test/unit/mirrors.test.ts` compared, as text past each header, `functions/utils/sectionRules.ts` with `src/lib/sectionRules.ts`, `functions/utils/time.ts` with `src/lib/lcaTime.ts`, and `functions/utils/pricing.ts` with `src/lib/pricing.ts` (with `'./lcaTime'` read as `'./time'`), and compared the values of `REGIONS` and `FAMILY_MEMBERSHIP_CHILDREN`. Each pair differed only in its header comments (and the pricing import line). `src/lib/family.ts` was five lines holding only `FAMILY_MEMBERSHIP_CHILDREN = 3`.
- **Membership prices (step 4).** Confirmed: `functions/api/membership/checkout.ts` held `TIER_PRICES` (adult 15, scholastic 5, family 25, senior 10) as a `Record<string, number>`, and `src/pages/MembershipPage.tsx` wrote the same four numbers into its tier list. No other copy exists in `src`, `functions` or `workers`.
- **Minor display-name trigger (step 4).** Confirmed in `DESIGN_REPLAN_phase1.md` lines 210, 256 and 352: an active guardian link or the entry's `eligibility_json`, and full names in pairings, standings, results and winners. Neither input exists in the code yet: there is no `eligibility_json`, no under-18 mark and no `member_guardians` table (only `members.guardian_id`, from `migrations/0036_family_accounts.sql`), so `publicName` takes the two facts as plain true or false values for WS06 to supply.
- **Worker deploy paths (step 4).** Confirmed: `deploy-worker.yml` ran only on `workers/**` and `functions/utils/**`; `domain/**` is added. `workers/daily-emails/tsconfig.json` already included `../../domain` from step 3.
- **jsdom and Node (step 2).** This container runs Node 22.22.0 and CI asks for Node `'22'`. jsdom 30.0.0 to 30.1.2 require Node 22.22.2 or later; jsdom 29.1.1 accepts 22.13 and later, so 29.1.1 is the pin. Installing the three packages added no new `npm audit` findings.
- **Migration file set and runner (step 6).** Confirmed: 52 `.sql` files, `0001` to `0051` with two `0022` files (`0022_site_announcement.sql`, `0022_tournament_reminders_registration_notified.sql`), plus `README.md`; the next number is `0052`. `test/integration/setup.ts` globs `../../migrations/*.sql` without recursion and the splitter strips `--` comments, so drizzle-kit's `--> statement-breakpoint` lines are harmless (a test feeds it one). 0003, 0019, 0033, 0040 and 0046 rebuild tables; 0032 and 0038 also contain `DROP TABLE`. `wrangler.toml` has no `migrations_dir`, so wrangler and `migrate-db.yml` read `migrations/`.
- **Splitter trigger handling (step 6).** Confirmed: the old splitter kept a `CREATE TRIGGER` open until the text so far ended in `END`, so `CASE ... END;` inside a trigger body ended the trigger early (`split-sql.test.ts` keeps the old splitter as a reference and shows it). No existing migration hit it: the only `CASE` is in 0003, inside an `INSERT ... SELECT`, and the two triggers (0046, redefined in 0050) have none.
- **Introspection source (step 6).** Confirmed: no d1-http credentials here, so the schema was introspected from a local file built from the migrations, never from a remote database. Node is 22.22.0 with SQLite 3.50.4 (an ExperimentalWarning prints); Vitest loads `node:sqlite` in the unit suite, so the Miniflare fallback was not needed. Correction: in Node 22.22 `node:sqlite` turns foreign keys on by default (its `enableForeignKeyConstraints` option); SQLite itself does not, and the migrations' own `PRAGMA` lines are dropped by the splitter, so the helper switches them on itself. drizzle-kit read the file through `@libsql/client` (SQLite 3.45.1).
- **Safety test patterns (step 6).** Confirmed: the old patterns matched only bare names, `REFERENCES x(...)` directly followed by `ON DELETE`, and `ADD COLUMN`, so all three drizzle-kit fixtures went unseen. They now match.
- **Endpoint count and naming (step 7).** Confirmed: 88 handler files under `functions/api`, each exporting at least one `onRequest<Method>` as a `const`; no bare `onRequest` anywhere. The route audit already turned file paths into route segments (`[id]` kept as a parameter), and that is what the registry keys use. `functions/tournaments/[id].ts` and `functions/clubs/[id].ts` are page routes outside `functions/api` exporting only `onRequestGet`, and so is `functions/news/[slug].ts` (see Deviations).
- **Error response shape (step 7).** Confirmed: `functions/utils/response.ts` exports `jsonResponse`, `errorResponse` (`{ error: message }`), `handleOptions` and `parseJsonBody`. `isResponse` is not there; it is in `functions/utils/auth.ts`. `errorResponse` cannot carry extra keys, so `parseBody` builds its 400 with `jsonResponse`, keeping `{ error }` and adding `fields`. The site's API types are written by hand in `src/lib/api.ts` (`ApiTournamentListItem`, `ApiTournamentSection`).
- **Methods on the admin tournament endpoint (step 7).** Confirmed: `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id].ts` exports `onRequestOptions`, `onRequestPatch` and `onRequestDelete` only, and the manage page loads its tournament through GET `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts`. The coverage test lists PATCH and DELETE for it and nothing else.

- **Shape of `tournaments.sections` (step 8).** Confirmed: a JSON array of objects `{ name, entryFee, prizeFund?, ratingMax?, ratingMin?, unratedOk?, gradeMin?, gradeMax?, rulesSet?, prizes? }` (`tournamentSectionSchema`, `domain/contracts/events.ts`), with plain strings allowed for old events (`listedSectionSchema`; the parsers in `registrations.ts`, `registrations/batch.ts`, `admin/tournaments/[id]/walk-ins.ts` and `registrations/[id].ts` turn strings into names, and `sectionBaseFee` in `domain/registration/pricing.ts` gives a string the tournament's `entry_fee`). Every parser returns `[]` for malformed JSON. The column is `TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT '[]'` (0003, line 38). The pricing columns are `entry_fee`, `early_deadline`, `early_discount`, `late_after`, `late_fee` and `member_discount`; the only cap is `max_players`.
- **Old writes during the soak (step 8).** Confirmed: in `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id].ts` the PATCH builds `sections` at lines 88 to 90 and `round_schedule` at lines 109 to 111 (the plan said 106 to 108), and its one `UPDATE tournaments` (lines 125 to 170) rewrites both, and the fee columns, on every save. The only other writer of `sections` is the POST in `functions/api/admin/tournaments.ts` (the `INSERT`, `sections` built at lines 73 to 75); nothing else in `functions`, `workers`, `scripts` or `src` writes either column.
- **Who references registrations and tournaments (step 8).** Confirmed: no migration before 0052 references `registrations`. `tournament_directors`, `tournament_reminders`, `tournament_attendee_reminders` and `tournament_games` cascade from `tournaments`, and `state_champions` sets null. `registrations.tournament_id` has no delete action, and the admin DELETE removes entries, games, directors and both reminder tables before the tournament, so the new cascades never meet an entry (the new integration test deletes a tournament with an entry through it).
- **`round_schedule` (step 8).** Confirmed: added in 0007 (line 3) as nullable text holding `[{ round, date, time }]`; written only by the admin PATCH; read by `admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts` (line 23) and `tournaments/[id].ts` (line 36).
- **Names link entries and games to sections (step 8).** Confirmed: `registrations.section` and `tournament_games.section` are `TEXT NOT NULL` names, and pairing, results and rounds match them with `section = ?`.
- **Seed rows in the test databases (step 8).** The planning note said the 0002 seed rows exist in every integration test. They do not: 0003 rebuilds `tournaments` without them, so after all migrations a test database has no tournaments or entries until a test seeds them.

- **Writers of `tournaments.sections` (step 9).** Confirmed before the change: only `functions/api/admin/tournaments.ts` (the `INSERT`) and `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id].ts` (the PATCH `UPDATE ... sections = ?`) wrote it. `admin/tournaments/[id]/registration.ts` updates the registration status, opening time and reminder columns; `workers/daily-emails/src/index.ts` updates only `registration_status` (line 87 opens registration, line 105 closes it); `generate-pairings.ts` sets `status`; `admin/clubs/[id].ts` sets `club_id = NULL`; `workers/clearinghouse-sync` does not touch `tournaments`; `migrations/0002_seed.sql` seeds the JSON and the 0053 triggers write it on purpose. After the change only `sectionsRepo.ts` writes it, which the audit test holds.
- **Methods on the admin tournament endpoints (step 9).** Confirmed: `admin/tournaments/[id].ts` exports OPTIONS, PATCH and DELETE (no PUT, no GET); `admin/tournaments.ts` exports OPTIONS and POST and answered with the raw `SELECT *` row, `sections` as text; `adminCreateTournament` in `src/lib/api.ts` reads `data.tournament` as a loose record and the wizard reads only its `id`. DELETE keeps its pending line.
- **The tournament-edit assertion (step 9).** Confirmed: `test/integration/tournament-edit.test.ts` lines 108 to 122 send `[{ name: 'Open', entryFee: 40, prizeFund: '$700' }, { name: 'U1400', entryFee: 30 }]` and compare the parsed JSON with `toEqual` at line 122, so the JSON gains no ids or other keys; it passes unchanged.
- **Who may edit a tournament (step 9).** Confirmed: the PATCH uses `requireTournamentManager` (`canManageTournament`: an LCA admin, the rep of the owning club, or an assigned director; never a guest), and refuses an organizing-club change from anyone but an LCA admin before any write. Unchanged; covered again in `sections-writer.test.ts` and `contracts/admin-tournaments.test.ts`. Also found: the PATCH checked nothing about section names before, so repeated names went straight into the JSON.

- **Player registration readers (step 10).** Confirmed before the change: `functions/api/registrations.ts` parsed the JSON in `parseSections` and `parseSectionNames` (lines 25 to 40) and called them at lines 94 and 100; `registrations/batch.ts` had `parseSections` at lines 39 to 46, called it once per request at line 92, matched each entry at line 126 and priced at line 157 through `entryPrice`, which parsed the JSON again; `registrations/[id].ts` had `parseSectionList` and `sectionFee` at lines 28 to 40 and used them at lines 231 to 258 (the name check, then the old and new fees), with `sections` in its tournament select at line 113. `registrations/[id]/pay.ts` does not read sections: it charges the amount on the payment row (its comment at lines 47 and 48 says so) and uses the section name only in the Stripe product name.
- **Registration writers (step 10).** Confirmed: `INSERT INTO registrations` at lines 138 (waitlist), 180 (free) and 245 (paid) of `registrations.ts` and line 216 of `batch.ts`, and the section change in `registrations/[id].ts` (`section = ?` at line 253, run in the one `UPDATE registrations` at line 325). None set `section_id`; only the 0053 triggers filled it. No other file under `functions/api/registrations` writes `registrations`.
- **Price display (step 10).** Confirmed: `src/pages/TournamentDetailPage.tsx` renders `price.base` and each `price.lines` label and amount in `PriceLines` (lines 132 to 152; the plan's 136 to 149 are its body), used at line 115 and at line 963 (the plan said 961); `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` uses only `amount`. The old total was one expression, `Math.max(0, Math.round((base + the lines) * 100) / 100)`, which `priceEntry` keeps, so the shape and the labels are unchanged.
- **Tests that edit the pricing columns (step 10).** Confirmed: `test/integration/registration-rules.test.ts` sets `early_deadline`, `early_discount` and `member_discount` with a direct `UPDATE` at line 75 (expecting 17 for a member and 20 for a non-member) and `late_after` and `late_fee` at line 91 (expecting 35), after `seedTournament`. The handlers read those columns from the tournament row of the request, so the file passes unchanged.
- **Admin entry readers (step 11).** Confirmed before the change: `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/walk-ins.ts` parsed the JSON in its own `parseSectionList` (lines 19 to 26, turning plain strings into names and giving `[]` for malformed JSON), read `sections` in its tournament select (line 51) and matched the section with `s.name === section` at line 69, case-sensitive; its `INSERT INTO registrations` (lines 110 to 113) listed `id, tournament_id, member_id, section, payment_status, bye_rounds, rating_at_entry`, with no `section_id`, so only the 0053 trigger filled it. The fee was the JSON's `entryFee`, else `entry_fee`, with no tiers or member discount. `waitlist.ts` priced at line 48 with `entryPrice(t, reg.section, { isLcaMember: reg.membership_status === 'active' })` over a `SELECT *` typed with `sections: string` (lines 41 to 46), so the member discount applied through `priceEntry`. The brief's line numbers (walk-ins 19 to 68, waitlist 42 to 48) were right. Both export only POST and OPTIONS. `requireTournamentManager` runs before the body is read in both.

- **Reader list (step 12).** Re-run before the change: sections readers in `tournaments.ts`, `tournaments/[id].ts`, `clearinghouse.ts`, `clubs/[id].ts`, `admin/tournaments.ts` (step 9), `admin/tournaments/[id].ts` (PATCH answer), `[id]/registration.ts`, `manage.ts`, `rating-report.ts`, `waitlist.ts` and `walk-ins.ts` (step 11), `registrations.ts`, `batch.ts`, `registrations/[id].ts` (step 10), `utils/pricing.ts`, `utils/prizes.ts`, `utils/tournament-manage.ts` and `functions/types.ts`. Correction found by the builder: `registration.ts` never names the column; it returned `SELECT *` raw, so a plain grep misses it (the audit's `SELECT *` rule covers it). After the change the column is read only in `sectionsRepo.ts`; `pricing.ts`, `prizes.ts`, `waitlist.ts`, `walk-ins.ts` and the registration handlers do not read it. No worker mentions sections or `round_schedule`.
- **Registration PATCH client use (step 12).** Confirmed: `updateTournamentRegistration` in `src/lib/api.ts` sends `registration_status` and returns `Promise<void>`, so the new body shape breaks no caller.
- **Per-section rating system (step 12).** Confirmed in code: `report_settings.sections` is keyed by section name; `buildSaveSections` moves the key on a rename of a live row (step 9), and the rating report now takes its names from the table.
- **Other readers checked (step 12, from review).** `waitlist.ts` and `registrations/[id].ts` include archived rows but pick one with `.find()`, live rows first, so neither can list a name twice. Remaining `SELECT * FROM tournaments` uses either go through `toTournamentResponse` or never answer the row (waitlist, registrations, batch; the admin DELETE returns `{ success }`).

- **Client readers (step 13).** The plan counted 17 site files that reference sections. Read before the change, they split three ways. Readers of tournament sections: `TournamentWizard.tsx`, `TournamentManagePage.tsx`, `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx`, `GradeConfirm.tsx`, `TournamentDetailPage.tsx`, `TournamentPairingsPage.tsx` (names in order), `TournamentPrintPage.tsx` (names), `TournamentsPage.tsx` and `ScholasticPage.tsx` (through `UnifiedTournament` and `isScholasticTournament` in `scholastic.ts`), `clearinghouse.ts` (the `UnifiedTournament.sections` type), `pricing.ts` and `api.ts`, plus `SectionRulesEditor.tsx` and `PrizesEditor.tsx`, which the plan did not list. Not tournament sections at all: `ResultsEntry.tsx` (a list of names passed in), `UsChessUploadPanel.tsx` and `uschessUpload.ts` (the rating report's `{ name, players }` and upload settings keyed by name). `AdminPage.tsx` only uses "section" for its own sidebar sections. Every reader compiles against the contract types; only the ones changed above needed an edit.
- **Shims that re-export `entryPrice` (step 13).** Confirmed: `src/lib/pricing.ts` and `functions/utils/pricing.ts` are each `export * from` the domain file, so both lost `entryPrice` and `sectionBaseFee` with no edit of their own. No server file called either (a search of `functions/` found none), and no file imports `PricedTournament` now.
- **Contract shapes for the site (step 13).** Confirmed in `domain/contracts/events.ts`: `savedSectionSchema` is the section in every tournament answer (`tournamentListItemSchema.sections`, the detail and manage answers, the admin answers, the clearinghouse); `sectionSchema` and `sectionListItemSchema` are the request side, with optional `id`, `cap` and `fees`, and `fees` carrying `regular` is ignored on save. `@domain/*` resolves for the site in `tsconfig.app.json`, `vite.config.ts` and `vitest.config.ts`, and `tsconfig.app.json` includes `domain`. Before the change no site file imported `@domain/contracts` or zod, and `dist/assets` held no `ZodError`.
- **Bare-name sections in answers (step 13).** Confirmed: every endpoint answers a section stored as a bare name as an object with an id (step 12's `sectionResponse`), and the clearinghouse answers `[]` for partner events, so the string branches on the list and scholastic pages could not be reached. The pages still match sections by name (the chosen section, the price box, the family panel), which is unchanged; names are unique among an event's live sections.
- **Who writes and reads `round_schedule` (step 14).** Re-ran the grep. Writer confirmed: only the admin PATCH wrote it (sending `null` stored the text `'null'`). Readers: the plan's list was incomplete. Every caller of `toTournamentResponse` parsed the column through it, six in all: the list, the detail, the manage page, the registration PATCH, the admin PATCH and the admin POST. All six now read the table. The three pages read it through `src/lib/api.ts`, as expected. The manage endpoint is `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts`. Not confirmed: `tournament-edit.test.ts` holds no `round_schedule` comparison; the `toEqual` checks are in the `sections-triggers*`, `contracts/*` and `tournaments-readers` tests, and all pass unchanged.
- **Merge round meaning (step 14).** Confirmed: `merge_round` was in 0052 and `schema.ts` with no code using it. Two schedules that merge (for example a 3-day and a 2-day) show as one day-by-day list with a merge row. The second schedule plays its own rounds 1 to merge round minus 1, then the primary's from the merge round on. A player's choice is stored on `registrations.schedule_id`, which stays the primary until WS06; a test shows a new entry still gets it from the trigger.

- **Where the member discount lived (step 15).** Re-ran the grep before the change; slices 10 to 14 had moved some hits. `functions/utils/pricing.ts` and `src/lib/pricing.ts` are one-line re-exports and needed no edit. `registrations/[id].ts` and the POST in `admin/tournaments.ts` had no hits (the create body schema is loose, so `memberDiscount` was already dropped there). `src/lib/api.ts` had one hit (line 603). The detail page's "LCA members save" note was at lines 1083 to 1091. `TournamentManagePage.tsx` and `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` had the input and the per-player membership pricing. `HomePage.tsx` line 300 was a hit the plan missed ("discounted tournament entry"); fixed. `MembershipPage.tsx` has no discount wording, and no email template mentions a discount. After the change `member_discount`, `memberDiscount` and `isLcaMember` appear only in comments, the schema, `functions/types.ts` and the contract's tournament row and edit request; `no-member-price-surface.test.ts` pins that.
- **`requires_lca_membership` absent (step 15).** Confirmed before the change: no column, type, contract or page named it.
- **`canManageTournament` admits assigned directors (step 15).** Confirmed: `requireTournamentManager` uses `canManageTournament`, which passes an LCA admin, the event's club rep and an assigned director. The field rule therefore names the director case, and tests cover an assigned director, a plain member assigned as director, another club's rep (assigned and not), a rep assigned to an LCA-run event, a plain member and a signed-out caller.
- **Decisions D11 and D12 (step 15).** Found as table rows in `docs/redesign/REDESIGN_SPEC.md` (around lines 2024 and 2025): D11 "One entry price", D12 the per-tournament requirement with a planned site-wide override.
- **Other price callers (step 15, from review).** Every caller of `priceEntry` and `priceShownSection` (`registrations.ts`, `batch.ts`, `waitlist.ts`, `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx`, `TournamentDetailPage.tsx`) now passes three arguments; `walk-ins.ts` never used the member discount. `admin/tournaments.ts` is the only insert path for tournaments, so no other path creates an event whose requirement would default to 1 for a club.
- **Update triggers on `tournaments` (step 15, from review).** The only update trigger fires on `sections` and `round_schedule`, so the 0054 backfill fires none.

- **Pages routing of helper files (step 16).** Confirmed: nothing under `functions/utils` exports an `onRequest` handler, so folders there make no routes. The page routes outside `functions/api` are `functions/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/clubs/[id].ts` and `functions/news/[slug].ts` (three, as step 7 found), so no area folder can be `functions/<area>/`. `contract-coverage.test.ts` and `contracts-status.test.ts` skip anything under `utils`, at any depth.
- **Worker and deploy coupling (step 16).** Confirmed: `workers/daily-emails/src/index.ts` imported `email`, `campaigns`, `site`, `membershipExpiry`, `ratingSnapshots` and `time` from `functions/utils`; the mover rewrote the three that moved. `workers/daily-emails/tsconfig.json` names only `../../functions/utils/email.ts`, which stays, and `tsc` passes without a change. `tsconfig.functions.json` includes `functions`, `domain` and `workers/daily-emails/src`, unchanged. `deploy-worker.yml` triggers on `workers/**`, `functions/utils/**` and `domain/**`.
- **The swiss include (step 16).** Confirmed: `vitest.config.ts` collected `functions/utils/swiss/**/*.test.ts`, and `test-setup.test.ts` pins the same string; both now name `functions/utils/live/swiss/**/*.test.ts`, and the three swiss test files (20 tests) still run. No ESLint, Vite, bundle-check, database script or scanner setting names `functions/utils` or the swiss folder.
- **The re-exports (step 16).** Confirmed before the move: `pricing.ts`, `sectionRules.ts`, `regions.ts` and `time.ts` are each one `export * from` line of `domain/`; `family.ts` is not (see Deviations).
- **What the mover has to handle (step 16).** Confirmed: no `vi.mock`, `vi.doMock` or `?raw` import pointed into `functions/utils`. The two `?raw` uses in the repository are `import.meta.glob` calls in `test/integration/setup.ts` and `handler-exports.test.ts`, which take patterns rather than specifiers, so the mover does not treat them as imports. There were three dynamic imports of a moved file: two of `./permissions` in `auth.ts` and one in `functions/api/tournaments/[id].ts`. Nothing in `src/` imports `functions/utils`.
- **Site files that name server paths (step 16).** `src/vite-env.d.ts` and `src/lib/roles.ts` name `site.ts` and `auth.ts`, which stay; `src/lib/api.ts` named `functions/utils/prizes` in a comment, now `functions/utils/events/prizes`.
- **The audit tests (step 16).** `sections-writer-audit.test.ts`, `schedules-audit.test.ts` and `no-member-price-surface.test.ts` name only `events/sectionsRepo.ts` and `events/schedulesRepo.ts`, which did not move, and pass unchanged. `sections-reader-audit.test.ts` named `tournament-manage.ts` and `prizes.ts` at their old paths and was updated (see Deviations).
- **Wrangler without an account (step 16).** `npx wrangler pages functions build` and, from `workers/daily-emails`, `npx wrangler deploy --dry-run` both ran here without a Cloudflare login.
- **What `src/lib` held (step 17).** Confirmed before the move: `api`, `brand`, `champions`, `clearinghouse`, `clubColors`, `clubMapData`, `documentUpload`, `family`, `features`, `format`, `lcaTime`, `mapLocation`, `mfa`, `parseDocumentFile`, `posts`, `pricing`, `regions`, `resizeImage`, `roles`, `scholastic`, `sectionRules`, `supabase`, `tournamentStatus`, `uschessUpload`, `utils`, `viewOnly.tsx` and `scanner/`. `format.ts` was missing from the planned list; it is a one-line re-export of `domain/format` and stays. `pricing`, `sectionRules`, `lcaTime`, `regions` and `family` are each one `export * from '@domain/...'` line, and stay.
- **shadcn aliases (step 17).** Confirmed: `components.json` names `@/components`, `@/lib/utils`, `@/components/ui`, `@/lib` and `@/hooks`. `src/lib/utils.ts` stays, so a component added with the shadcn command still resolves, and the file needs no change.
- **What the mover has to handle on the site (step 17).** Confirmed: the script searches every `.ts` and `.tsx` file under `src` and `test` (`SCAN_DIRS` and `CODE_EXTENSIONS` in `scripts/move-files.ts`), which covers `src/App.tsx`, `src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx` and `src/lib/viewOnly.tsx` as well as pages and components. No `vi.mock`, `vi.doMock`, dynamic `import()` or `?raw` import points at a moved file; the only specifiers are static imports, three of them relative paths in tests. No test pins the path of a moved file in a string; the path-pinning tests name `src/lib/pricing.ts`, `src/lib/format.ts`, `src/lib/features.ts`, `src/lib/api.ts` and the shim paths, which stay.
- **Aliases and config (step 17).** Confirmed: `tsconfig.app.json` maps `@/*` to `./src/*` and includes `src` and `domain`, so `@/areas/...` resolves with no new alias; Vite and Vitest map `@` to `./src`. The Vitest include `src/lib/scanner/**/*.test.ts` is unchanged, and no ESLint rule names `src/lib`.
- **Cycles (step 17).** Confirmed: the moved files import only `@/lib/api` (types), `@/lib/brand` and `@/components/StatusBadge` (a type), and `StatusBadge.tsx` imports only `react` and `@/lib/utils`, so no import cycle comes in. Nothing imports an area's `index.ts` yet.

- **Route-audit couples to one file (step 18).** Confirmed: `test/unit/route-audit.test.ts` read `src/lib/api.ts` by a fixed path. It now scans the barrel, `http.ts` and every area `api.ts`, and compares the union with the paths from before the split. `test/unit/client-sections-audit.test.ts` read `src/lib/api.ts` by path too (see Deviations). `domain-boundaries.test.ts` uses the path only as an argument to its own helper, and the other mentions are comments.
- **Direct page fetches (step 18).** Confirmed: `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` line 162 calls `fetch('/api/clearinghouse?upcoming=true')` itself. So do `src/pages/ScholasticPage.tsx`, `src/pages/TournamentsPage.tsx`, `src/components/FacebookFeed.tsx` and `src/components/uscf/UscfSearchInput.tsx`. Route-audit has never covered them, and this step leaves them where they are; HomePage's call is WS03's.
- **Mocks of the client (step 18).** Confirmed: three tests `vi.mock('@/lib/api')` with a factory that spreads `importOriginal()`, which works with a barrel; none uses `import * as`, `vi.spyOn` on the module or `vi.importActual`. All pass unchanged.
- **What `src/lib/api.ts` held (step 18).** Confirmed before the split: three imports (`supabase`, the `RawScan` type, five contract types with `import type`), 182 exports and three private functions; no `export const`, default export, re-export or dynamic import. `supabase` was used only by `authHeaders`, `RawScan` only by `scanScoresheet`, and `exportQuery` only by the two member export calls. No statement had a comment after it on the same line, and none followed the last declaration.

- **Component folders before the move (step 19).** Confirmed: `src/components` held the folders `admin`, `auth`, `errors`, `family`, `governance`, `layout`, `maps`, `tournaments`, `ui` and `uscf`, and the files `AdminAnnouncementPanel`, `AnnouncementBanner`, `DonateButton`, `FacebookFeed`, `FilterDropdown`, `ImpersonationBanner`, `MultiSelectDropdown`, `PageHero`, `RegistrationReminderButton` and `StatusBadge`. `tournaments` held the eleven files the plan names (`AgendaList`, `Crosstable`, `GradeConfirm`, `MiniCalendar`, `PreviewBanner`, `PrizeWinners`, `PrizesEditor`, `ResultsEntry`, `SectionRulesEditor`, `StandingsTable`, `UsChessUploadPanel`), `admin` eleven, `governance` four, and `family`, `maps` and `uscf` two each: 34 files with the two top-level ones.
- **react-refresh lint (step 19).** The plan said `reactRefresh.configs.vite` warns on a module that mixes component and other exports. In the installed version (0.5.3) it sets `react-refresh/only-export-components` to an error, with `allowConstantExport`. Three moved files already broke it before the move (`LCAMap.tsx` 8, `GradeConfirm.tsx` 3, `UsChessMembership.tsx` 1) and keep the same errors; `MembersTable.tsx`, `TournamentWizard.tsx` and `UscfSearchInput.tsx` export a type beside their component, which the rule allows. Every other moved file exports components only, and no file mixes exports that did not before. The area `index.ts` files are re-exports only and did not change.
- **Lazy routes (step 19).** Confirmed: `src/App.tsx` has 33 `lazy()` calls, each `import('@/pages/X').then((m) => ({ default: m.X }))`. None names a component, and `App.tsx` imports only shared pieces, so the move left it alone. Before the move, 115 files were in `dist/assets`.
- **Mocks and pinned paths (step 19).** Confirmed: no `vi.mock` or `vi.doMock` under `test/unit` named `@/components/`, so there was nothing to rewrite by hand; the only test specifier that reached a moved file was the dynamic import in `tournament-pages-pricing.test.tsx`, which the script rewrote. Two tests pinned a moved path in a string (`client-sections-audit.test.ts` and `no-member-price-copy.test.ts`) and were fixed by hand.
- **Areas and pages (step 19).** Confirmed: nothing under `src/areas` imported `src/pages` before the move, and nothing does after it (rule 1 reports nothing over the folder).
- **Page fetch calls (step 19).** Confirmed: `HomePage.tsx` line 162, `ScholasticPage.tsx` line 96 and `TournamentsPage.tsx` lines 402 and 413, all to `/api/clearinghouse`. `FacebookFeed.tsx` and `UscfSearchInput.tsx` call `fetch` as well, but they are components, which rule 3 does not cover.
- **Barrels and components (step 19).** Confirmed: no area `index.ts` named a component, and every importer imported a component by its path, so leaving the `index.ts` files alone changed no importer.
- **Large pages (step 19).** Measured: `TournamentManagePage.tsx` 2379 lines, `TournamentDetailPage.tsx` 1110, `ScannerPage.tsx` 1031 and `TournamentsPage.tsx` 865. The brief's WS04 section lists `TournamentsPage.tsx` ("split into view components"), WS05 `TournamentDetailPage.tsx`, WS08 `TournamentManagePage.tsx` and WS15 `ScannerPage.tsx`.

- **Can `wrangler pages dev` serve a built folder with the functions and the local database here (step 20).** Yes, with wrangler 4.112.0. A build folder other than `pages_build_output_dir` is accepted, and two servers share one `--persist-to` when each has its own `--inspector-port`. Wrangler prints "Proxy environment variables detected", and the server's own outbound fetches go through the proxy. Both servers shut down cleanly after the run.
- **Do bindings override `wrangler.toml` `[vars]` and `.dev.vars` (step 20).** `-b NAME=value` does; the startup list showed `SUPABASE_URL ("(hidden)")`. `--env-file` does not: wrangler 4.112 `pages dev` ignores it and still reads `.dev.vars`. So the config turns each line of `.dev.vars.a11y` into a `-b` flag and binds the five service keys empty, and a planted `.dev.vars` with sentinel values did not reach the servers.
- **Does `vite build --mode a11y` read `.env.a11y` (step 20).** It would, but a value in the process environment wins over the file, empty strings included (`loadEnv` in `node_modules/vite/dist/node/chunks/node.js`, line 5697). The setup passes every `VITE_*` value on the command line and no dotfile was added.
- **Integration fixture conventions (step 20).** `test/integration/factories.ts` uses bound `INSERT` statements and `@test.lca` emails and creates sections through `saveSections`. The seed instead puts the JSON in `tournaments.sections` and lets the 0053 insert triggers build the section and schedule rows; the triggers never write `fee_early` or `fee_late`, so an `UPDATE` sets them. `test/unit/seed-fixtures.test.ts` proves it on a database built from 0001 to 0054 with `node:sqlite`. Its dry run of `db:seed:preview` rewrites the script, refuses to run unless the rewrite took, and runs it with a PATH that holds only a `wrangler` stub (exit 99) and a `node` wrapper that refuses any argument naming wrangler (exit 98), so a future form of the script can never reach the preview database from `npm test`.
- **Order of the web servers and `globalSetup` (step 20).** Confirmed: Playwright 1.63 starts the servers first (see Deviations, step 20).
- **Browser in this container (step 20).** Playwright 1.63.0 expects Chromium build 1243 and `/opt/pw-browsers` holds 1194; the suite ran with `PW_CHROMIUM_PATH=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` (see Deviations, step 20).
- **Sign-in with an unreachable Supabase (step 20).** Confirmed: signed-out pages make no Supabase call, and the smoke spec saw no request outside 127.0.0.1.
- **Package ages (step 20).** `@playwright/test` 1.63.0 was published Friday, September 4, 2026 and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.13.0 on Tuesday, August 11, 2026; both are over two weeks old at Tuesday, October 13, 2026.
- **The early stop's address check (step 20).** A first version of the Actions file's host check let `https://www.louisianachess.org./` and `https://someone:secret@louisianachess.org/` through (it printed that the preconditions were confirmed), and two tests in `e2e-preview-workflow.test.ts` failed. The check now reads the host with `new URL`; both addresses stop at the first job.
- **What `.dev.vars` did to the a11y servers (step 20).** A first version bound only the eight names in `.dev.vars.a11y`, so a developer's own `.dev.vars` still supplied the Stripe, Resend, Facebook and scanner-model keys. Fixed by the empty bindings (see the step).
- **Current token state (step 21).** Confirmed before the change: `src/index.css` had the stock oklch shadcn `:root` and a `.dark` block nothing toggled (no `dark` class set anywhere in `src`), the brand `@theme` with `lca-navy`, `lca-gold` and `lca-cream`, the dark variant `(&:is(.dark *))`, no `data-theme` rule, no semantic or gold-ink token, and only Geist Variable imported. `data-theme` was set only by step 20's fixture page, and `localStorage` was used nowhere in `src`.
- **Button focus (step 21).** Confirmed: `button.tsx` had `focus-visible:border-ring focus-visible:ring-3 focus-visible:ring-ring/50` with the stock `--ring` `oklch(0.708 0 0)`. Measured on the `a11y-off` build before the change, a keyboard-focused Button had the box shadow `oklab(0.708 0 0 / 0.5) 0px 0px 0px 3px`, the border `oklch(0.708 0 0)` and no outline, and `tokens.spec.ts` finds the same after it on the fixture, `/`, `/tournaments` and `/membership`. The destructive variant replaces the colour with `ring-destructive/20`, and `tailwind-merge` still drops the base colour for it with `ring-focus`. `badge.tsx` uses `focus:` and full-strength `ring-ring` (see Deviations, step 21). The other ring users (`input`, `textarea`, `select`, `tabs`, `toggle`, `dialog` and the contact page) are unchanged; under the new look the shared rule draws their ring.
- **Mono font (step 21).** Confirmed: no `--font-mono` was defined, so `font-mono` fell back to the system stack. There are 18 `font-mono` lines in 10 files (see Deviations, step 21). `@fontsource-variable/geist-mono` 5.3.0 exists; read with fontTools, its latin normal file (23,128 bytes) has ½ (U+00BD) and no ○ or ● (U+25CB, U+25CF), and its GSUB features are `ccmp`, `dnom`, `frac`, `locl` and `numr`: no `tnum` and no `liga`. Every `@font-face` in the package is `'Geist Mono Variable'`.
- **theme-color metas (step 21).** Confirmed: `index.html` already has `#1a2744` for light and `#0b1220` for dark, so it is unchanged.
- **Utilities need the token at `:root` (step 21).** Confirmed: an `@theme inline` utility writes `var(--token)` where it is used, so each token needs a value everywhere; `tokens.test.ts` checks every colour utility names a token the plain `:root` defines.
- **Live fonts (step 22).** Confirmed: `@fontsource/barlow-condensed` and `@fontsource/barlow` are both 5.3.0, published Sunday, July 19, 2026, so over two weeks old at Monday, October 19, 2026. Both are now installed and exact-pinned.
- **JetBrains Mono (step 22).** Decided: not required. See Decisions.
- **Heritage routes (step 22).** Confirmed in `src/App.tsx`: lazy routes for `/meeting`, `/about`, `/governance/board`, `/champions`, `/governance/bylaws` and `/governance/minutes`. The redirects `/governance`, `/state-champions` and `/governance/rules` lead into that set but redirect before anything draws, so `routeScope` returns base for them; the set stays exactly six.
- **Always-base routes (step 22).** Confirmed: `/dashboard`, `/account/security`, `/manage/club`, `/board/inbox`, `/workspace`, `/admin`, `/admin/:section`, `/admin/clubs/:id` and `/admin/tournaments/:id`. There is no `/manage/tournaments` route; the `/manage` prefix would cover one.
- **Token blocks (step 22).** Confirmed: every scope sits under `:root[data-look="new"]`. `--gold-ink` is `#705718` in heritage light and `#866a1e` in base light, but `#c8a94a` in both dark blocks, so `themeScopes.test.ts` reads the light blocks. With `newLook` off a scope switch changes no token, so AC8's colour change is asserted only on `a11y-on` (and the test asserts no change on `a11y-off`).
- **Fixture page (step 22).** Confirmed and changed as described in the step block: it was one container per URL with no scholastic tiles and an outdated header comment.
- **Flags (step 22).** Confirmed: `themeHeritage` and `themeScholastic` exist and stay unwired; nothing renders ThemeScope outside the fixture page, which `theme-scope-wiring.test.ts` checks.
- **Hardcoded colour counts (step 23).** Re-counted at `bb8c8a6` with ripgrep, counting class tokens with their variants and opacity modifiers. `text-lca-navy`: 381 in 72 TSX files (365 plain, 13 `hover:`, 2 `/80`, 1 `[&_a]:`), 7 of them in commented-out code, plus 1 in `src/lib/brand.ts` (`GOLD_BUTTON`); the plan's 382 counted that one. `bg-white`: 43 in 18 TSX files, 8 opaque (7 plain and 1 `hover:`) and 35 translucent (`/5` to `/90` and their hover forms). `bg-lca-navy`: 102 in 45 TSX files (105 across `src` with the 3 in `src/index.css`): 79 opaque, 4 `hover:bg-lca-navy/90` and 19 tints. `text-[#...]` 39 in 23 files, `bg-[#...]` 8 in 5, emerald text 62 in 30 and red text 19 in 11, all left to step 24. `text-muted-foreground` 671 in 85 files, `bg-muted` 123 in 51, `bg-card` 95 in 34, `bg-background` 63 in 35 and `border-border` 93 in 22 already follow the tokens under the new look. Also found: `border-lca-navy` 73 in 23 files and `ring-lca-navy` 1, which no map covers (see Follow-ups).
- **Dark usage today (step 23).** No `dark:` utility in a TSX file outside `src/components/ui`, where `button.tsx` has nine on four lines. `prefers-color-scheme` appears only in `src/lib/colorScheme.ts`, `src/hooks/useColorScheme.ts` (both step 21) and `src/assets/vite.svg`. `.dark` is set only when `newLook` is on (step 21), and the new look's dark tokens outrank the stock `.dark` block.
- **The mapped classes on the plain `:root` (step 23).** `text-lca-navy` compiles to `var(--color-lca-navy)`, `#1a2744` in the brand `@theme` block, and `text-ink` to `var(--ink)`, `#1a2744` on the plain `:root`. `bg-white` compiles to `var(--color-white)`, `#fff`, which Tailwind's own `theme.css` defines, and `bg-surface` to `var(--surface)`, `#fff`. With a modifier both forms give the same `color-mix(in oklab, ...)` (see Deviations, step 23, for the fallback). The greys are not identical: `border-gray-300` is `oklch(87.2% 0.01 258.338)` against `--hairline` `oklch(0.922 0 0)`, and `bg-gray-100` `oklch(96.7% 0.003 264.542)` against `--surface-2` `oklch(0.97 0 0)`. Tailwind orders same-property utilities by name, so a rename moves where a class lands in the stylesheet; every element that pairs a renamed class with a rival of the same property does so inside `cn()`, whose `tailwind-merge` (3.6.0, which reads the new names as colours) keeps the last one by string order, or in branches of a ternary, so no winner changed.
- **`bg-brand-navy` and the gold ring (step 23).** Confirmed absent before the change: no `--brand-navy` token or utility. `bg-lca-navy` already read a brand literal that no scope or dark block redefines, so navy grounds already stayed navy; the rename names them. The gold ring rule, `:root[data-look="new"] .bg-lca-navy :focus-visible`, was pinned by `tokens.test.ts` and measured on the footer by `tokens.spec.ts`; it now names `.bg-brand-navy` too, and both tests pass with the footer on `bg-brand-navy`. The rule also reaches a light menu drawn inside a band, as it did for `.bg-lca-navy`: the state filter in the `/tournaments` hero opens one, so a second rule gives a `bg-popover` panel's controls the look's own ring back (see Judgement calls under the step).
- **Navy text on gold (step 23).** 55 lines in 34 TSX files put `text-lca-navy` beside a `bg-lca-gold` ground: 40 opaque in 30 files, of which 31 strings in 24 files carry the full `GOLD_BUTTON` set (12 of them local copies: `goldButtonClass` in 11 pages and `goldBtn` in `TournamentDetailPage`), and 15 on tints. `GOLD_BUTTON` is imported by 18 files (the plan said 19). One more navy text sits on gold set on its parent: the home page's trophy.
- **Opaque white (step 23).** The 8 opaque `bg-white` are 5 white mats (`WorkspacePage` line 193, `ClubsPage` 64, `ClubDetailPage` 153, `AdminClubPage` 442 and the QR code on `AccountSecurityPage` 219, all kept), the manage page's tab bar (1113) and the club map's button (`LCAMap` 468), now `bg-surface`, and the scanner's remove button over a photo (448), now `bg-surface/90` with `hover:bg-surface`. The 35 translucent uses all sit on navy grounds or the dark hero photo, except the scanner's `bg-white/90`.
- **Navy grounds outside class strings (step 23).** `ClubDetailPage.tsx` and `AdminClubPage.tsx` draw their heroes with `style={{ backgroundColor: LCA.navy, borderBottomColor: color }}` (the border is the club's colour) and hold links and, on the admin page, tab buttons, so under the new look their ring was navy on navy; both now carry `data-ground="dark"`. `AdminEmailPage.tsx` draws an email preview with inline literals and stays literal. `src/lib/viewOnly.tsx` is a TSX file under `src/lib` and was rewritten with the rest.
- **The step 21 follow-ups this step meets (step 23).** "Dark grounds that are not a navy band": the homepage hero (its outer element holds both groups of controls) and the `urgent` and `info` announcement tones are marked; the gold tone is not. "Dark mode on pages not converted yet": this step converts the ink and grounds, step 24 the status colours, greys and raw hex; `newLook` stays off until both land.
- **Lint in touched files (step 23).** 18 of the 34 errors were in 10 TSX files this step rewrites (`LCAMap.tsx` 9; `ClubLocationPicker.tsx`, `PostsPanel.tsx`, `ResultsEntry.tsx`, `UsChessMembership.tsx`, `FacebookFeed.tsx`, `src/lib/viewOnly.tsx`, `ContactPage.tsx`, `ScannerPage.tsx` and `SupportPage.tsx` 1 each), and all 13 warnings are in touched files. The 18 errors are fixed (see the step); the 13 warnings are unchanged. `HomePage.tsx`'s `fetch` warning moved from line 162 to 165.
- **Baseline and the dark run (step 23).** `test/a11y/baseline.json` covers four pages and four elements each, so it cannot see a heading or a link colour; `semantic-map.test.ts` is the proof of identity, and the baseline comparison still matches. No spec ran axe in dark mode before this step; `dark-spot.spec.ts` now does on the three pages, and `dark-grounds.spec.ts` reads their computed colours.
- **Build size (step 23).** `npm run check:bundle` checks only that no zod reaches `dist/assets` and measures no size, so the sizes are recorded under the step: CSS 112,204 to 113,933 bytes, JavaScript 2,569,320 to 2,568,712 bytes.
- **New files (step 23).** `scripts/codemods`, the script, its map and `semantic-map.test.ts` did not exist. Scripts are linted (`eslint.config.js`) but sit in no tsconfig, so the type check does not cover them; the script is checked by lint and by its unit tests, which run it with Node. The step also adds eight more unit test files, `test/unit/helpers/jsxClasses.ts` and `test/a11y/dark-grounds.spec.ts` (see the step's Files).
- **Records (step 23).** No row of the acceptance table owned this step, so T1 is added, with its own letter so the 32 AC and K rows stay as they are. The WS01 header still said the branch was not pushed and had no PR; it now names the merged checkpoints and draft pull request 108. The step 22 deviation block sat under Verify items and is moved to Deviations.
- **Legitimate raw colours (step 24).** Confirmed at `4e4ad3a`: `AdminClubPage.tsx` line 395 gives the colour input `placeholder="#c8a94a"` and `AdminPage.tsx` line 532 sets `style={{ backgroundColor: club.color || '#c8a94a' }}`; `clubColors.ts` is `src/areas/clubs/clubColors.ts` since step 17. Neither is a class: club colours are inline styles throughout, and the 17 quoted hex literals in TSX files (9 files: `LCAMap.tsx` 6, its pin colours and the SVG of its markers, `AdminEmailPage.tsx` 4, one each in `WorkspacePage`, `TournamentsPage`, `ClubDetailPage`, `AdminPage`, `AdminClubPage`, `AgendaList` and `ClubLocationPicker`) are inline styles or data. So the allowlist holds no club colour, and `raw-colours.test.ts` holds the two lines above and that no class is built from a value or put together around a `${}`.
- **Raw hex counts and the gold exception (step 24).** Counted at `4e4ad3a` with the script's own parser: `text-[#...]` 39 in 23 files and `bg-[#...]` 8 in 5, as planned, plus `accent-[#1a2744]` 3 and `hover:border-[#1877F2]` 1: 51 hex classes in 30 files, all in code strings. `#7a5c00` is 28 uses in 16 files and `#f0d07a` 5 in 5, not two strings; decided: every gold hex text class waits for step 25 (see Deviations). `text-[#7a5c00]` is exactly `--status-soon` on the plain `:root`, but no token has `#f0d07a` or `#8a6d1f`. Of the other values, `#1a2744`, `#1668d8` and the Scheduled tag's `#1e4d7b` became tokens, and the banners' grounds, `#1877F2` and `#f2efe6` are kept with reasons.
- **Status classes with a token of the same colour (step 24).** On the plain `:root`, `text-emerald-800` equals `--ok` and `--status-open`, `bg-emerald-100` `--status-open-tint`, `text-red-700` and `bg-red-100` the full, waitlist and live pair, and `text-blue-800` and `bg-blue-50` the partner pair, to the letter. `text-red-600` is `--bad`'s colour written differently (`oklch(0.577 0.245 27.325)` against `oklch(57.7% 0.245 27.325)`), so `semantic-map.test.ts` compares the `oklch()` numbers and allows that one pair alone. Every other shade got a token whose plain value is that shade, each held in `tokens.test.ts` and checked under the new look in `contrast.test.ts`.
- **Status words on their own tint (step 24).** Confirmed: under the new look `--status-live` and `--status-lca` are `#fff` in base and `--status-lca` is `#0b1220` in dark mode, so a status word on a page or a card can vanish. The map writes `text-status-open` only on the two Open pills, beside `bg-status-open-tint`, and red or green words elsewhere take `text-bad`, `text-ok` or a shade token; `contrast.test.ts` fails on a `text-status` class without its own opaque tint.
- **Greys and slates (step 24).** Confirmed: `text-gray-600` 5 in 4 files, `bg-gray-100` 3, `border-gray-300` 2 (the print page), `bg-slate-400` 2 and `bg-slate-500` 1 (the dots on `/tournaments`), and no zinc, neutral or stone; none equals a token on the plain `:root`. Decided: gray-600 and gray-100 get `--quiet-600` and `--quiet-100`, whose plain values are those greys; the print page's row rules and the slate dots are kept with reasons (see Judgement calls).
- **Families the plan does not name (step 24).** Confirmed: amber 37 in 9 files, blue 43 in 7, green 11 in 2, yellow 9 in 3, purple 2 in 1, and step 23's records and `dark-spot.spec.ts` already gave them to this step. Decided: the map takes all of them, and the ratchet watches every Tailwind palette. The categories among them are recorded under Judgement calls.
- **Light tones on navy (step 24).** Confirmed: StatusBadge's navy surface (three tones), the tournament page's navy hero (three tags) and the manage page's (three tags) put light green, red or blue words on a 20 per cent tint over `bg-brand-navy`, which stays navy in every look. Kept, each keep entry naming its one string, so the same class anywhere else fails the ratchet.
- **StatusBadge (step 24).** Confirmed: `TONE_CLASSES` holds 13 classes in this step's families and `text-[#7a5c00]`, and StatusDot's open dot is `bg-emerald-500`. None is rewritten: step 26 maps the status prop to the status tokens and keeps the tone and on props for today's callers, so the file is left whole to it, and its gold tone to step 25.
- **Light islands (step 24).** Confirmed: the member strip (`MembershipPage.tsx` line 183) and the finished-event panel (`TournamentManagePage.tsx` line 2177) are `bg-emerald-50` under `text-ink`, as are ten success notes (`RegisterPage`, `ContactPage`, `AccountSecurityPage`, `DashboardPage`, `ForgotPasswordPage`, `TournamentDetailPage` twice, `SupportPage`, `FamilyRegistrationPanel` and `PostsPanel`). All twelve take `bg-ok-50`, emerald-50 with `newLook` off and the ok tint under the new look, so no island stays light in dark mode. Found in review: one ground does stay light, the club map's paper `bg-[#f2efe6]`, and the map's loading note on it followed the look; it now takes tokens that do not (see Judgement calls under the step).
- **Navy tints and lines (step 24).** Confirmed: the 17 faint tints and 73 `border-lca-navy` (33 opaque, 27 of them beside an opaque `bg-brand-navy`; 40 tinted, one of them the `/30` on the gold banner) and one `ring-lca-navy`. Done, with the block for browsers that cannot mix colours (see the step). `semantic-map.test.ts`'s step 23 pins now give `bg-brand-navy` the forms `''`, `10`, `55` and `90` and 9 cases.
- **Quoted strings in comment prose (step 24).** Fixed before the run, as step 23's follow-up asked; no TSX comment named a class of this step's families.
- **The map's grammar (step 24).** Confirmed: watch entries, rule `from` and `to`, and keep classes took plain utility names only, so no hex class could be watched, ruled or kept. Extended: a map names a hex class wherever it names a class to watch, rewrite or keep, but never in what a rule writes or in the classes a rule looks for beside it, with edge tests. The status map's check runs in `semantic-map.test.ts` and in `semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` on the real tree.
- **The ratchet and its allowlist (step 24).** Confirmed: a test of the planned kind would have failed on the tree (39 `bg-white` that step 23's map keeps, the gold hex and every class this step keeps), and `text-lca-navy` is in no TSX file (`GOLD_BUTTON` in `src/lib/brand.ts` is not a TSX file). Decided: the ratchet reads the keep entries of both maps that name their one string, as the codemod's check finds them, instead of repeating them, and the allowlist counts the rest with a reason: the hex colours, the 34 translucent veils step 23's map keeps in every file and the 13 classes the status map keeps for a whole file. A keep entry that reaches further would let any number of new uses through, a translucent white card under `text-ink` on a new page included, which review found. The planted tree builds its class names when the test runs, so Tailwind never sees them.
- **Contrast (step 24).** Confirmed: `contrast.test.ts` checked `--ok` and `--bad` on `--ground` and `--surface` and each status word on its own tint, and nothing on `--surface-2`, a tint, or a token this step adds. Extended as the T2 row says.
- **The dark spot run (step 24).** Its list of classes a later step owns is now gold text and StatusBadge's light tones, as planned; the `/tournaments` failure (`TournamentsPage.tsx` line 644, `text-[#7a5c00]` at 1.84:1) stays, as gold text, until step 25.

No acceptance row owned this step, so T2 is added after T1 in the table's own pattern, and the step 23 follow-ups it closes say "Done in step 24" without renaming them.
