- **About 135 uses of `text-lca-gold` (step 25).** Confirmed at `3d80a0e` with the script's own parser: 135 in 43 TSX files (118 plain, 16 `hover:` and one `text-lca-gold/90` in `Navbar.tsx` line 233), and one more in a comment of `src/index.css`. 131 sit in the className strings of elements, 2 in objects of strings (StatusBadge line 28, the gold tone of its navy surface, and the tournament page's line 38, the Upcoming tag in its navy hero) and 2 in commented-out code (`AnnualMeetingPage.tsx` line 226, `NewsPage.tsx` line 166). `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` places 51 on navy in 20 files (the header, the footer and `PageHero`, the navy heroes and bands, and the two club heroes drawn with an inline navy and marked `data-ground="dark"`), the 2 constants by the allowlist, and 80 on light grounds in 32 files, every one an icon beside words in another colour and none of them words. The 8 tokens whose navy is not in the element or its ancestors are the More menu's button (two), `NavLinkItem` and `DrawerLink` (two each), which the script follows to the navy header, and the two constants. No TSX file names `bg-lca-navy` since step 23, so the grounds read are `bg-brand-navy`, `PageHero`, `Navbar`, `Footer`, `data-ground="dark"` and an inline navy; `data-ground="dark"` places a use on navy only beside a navy of its own, as on the club heroes, since the one element marked with it and no navy, the home page hero, is a darkened photo.
- **Meaningful gold icons (step 25).** Confirmed: `NewsPage.tsx` line 140 was `<Pin className="size-3 text-lca-gold" aria-label="Pinned" />` in a `bg-card` link, so a screen reader heard the word and a sighted visitor saw only the icon; under `newLook` it now shows the word (see the step). `PostsPanel.tsx` line 155 is the same pin with the same label in the admin list of posts, whose editor says "Pin to the top of the News page" in words; decided: it keeps the icon and its label (see Follow-ups). `NewsPostPage.tsx` line 57 draws the pin beside the word Pinned on the navy hero and needs nothing. The reminder bell at `RegistrationReminderButton.tsx` line 83 is decoration beside the Button's words, hidden from screen readers by lucide-react 1.25.0 (`Icon.mjs` line 36), and the tournament page's Bell and BellOff (lines 1062 and 1064) carry no gold and the same words, so the reminder bell has nothing to fix and gets no label.
- **Other gold-ish text (step 25).** Confirmed: StatusBadge line 35 and PageHero line 75 are two of 34 gold hex text classes in 20 files, which the step 24 records give this step whole: `#7a5c00` 28 times in 16 files (once as a hover, the add-seat pill in `MemberSeatsCell.tsx`), `#f0d07a` 5 times in 5 files and `#8a6d1f` once, the club page's Upcoming badge at 4.38:1 on its tint in today's look. 24 of the 28 are dark gold words on a gold tint of 8 to 20 per cent and 4 are not on a tint (the results count in `ResultsEntry.tsx` twice, a label on a card on `/champions`, the add-seat pill's hover); the 5 light golds each sit on a gold tint of 15 or 20 per cent over a ground that stays dark. All 34 moved (see the step and Deviations).
- **Tokens for the gold hex colours (step 25).** Confirmed: the plain `:root` gives `--gold-ink` and `--gold-on-dark` brand gold (`tokens.test.ts` holds them to it), so neither can take a hex the pages spelled; `--status-soon` is `#7a5c00` there, but it is a status word, which sits only on its own tint, and the pages' tints are brand gold at 8 to 20 per cent; no token had `#f0d07a` or `#8a6d1f`. Decided: three tokens, inside the existing `@theme inline` block, each held to its plain value in `tokens.test.ts`, set in base, dark, live and scholastic, heritage passing them on, and checked in each scope by `themeScopes.test.ts`.
- **Club colours as words (step 25).** Found: `ClubDetailPage.tsx` (an officer's title, a news date, the contact link) and `ClubsPage.tsx` (the initials) draw words in `club.color || LCA_GOLD`, and `AdminClubPage.tsx` its preview in `form.color || LCA.gold`, and the database gives every club brand gold until it picks a colour (`DEFAULT '#c8a94a'` in migration 0009 and `functions/db/schema.ts`), so a literal scan never sees them and a fallback for no colour alone would miss most clubs. Decided: `clubTextColor` (see the step), and the guard reads every style colour.
- **The guard's subjects (step 25).** Confirmed: no brand gold hex text class in any TSX file, no style colour of `#c8a94a` (its two literals in TSX are the colour picker's placeholder, `AdminClubPage.tsx` line 395, and a swatch's fallback ground, `AdminPage.tsx` line 532), `LCA.gold` never a text colour (`AdminClubPage.tsx` lines 77, 112 and 252 are the club colour field's default), `#705718` only in a comment of the fixture page and `#866a1e` only in a comment of `src/lib/brand.ts`, `#fbfaf6` nowhere, and neither an About menu "You are here" label (the club map's "You are here" is a map marker's title) nor a phone tab bar (the two "Tab bar" comments are page tabs). No TSX file is a heritage file of its own: the heritage pages are the six routes of `HERITAGE_ROUTES` (`src/lib/themeScopes.ts` line 43), read from `src/App.tsx`. Found as well: `LCAMap.tsx` gives its info window's directions link `#8a6d1f` in a string of HTML, 4.86:1 on the window's white, which is Google's markup outside the theme; it is not brand gold and stays. Found for the guard's wider reading: no `color`, `fill` or `stroke` attribute in a TSX file is brand gold (the one `color` prop, `TournamentRow`'s in `ClubDetailPage.tsx` lines 258 and 274, takes the club's colour for a 4 per cent tint of its row, never words), no style names a gold token's variable, and the admin's colour preview (`AdminClubPage.tsx` line 400) sits on its card under a tint written as the club's colour and two hex digits of alpha, which the script now reads as a tint. Every place a component told its ground by an `on` prop is drawn gives the `on` its ground needs, and every element that reads a looked-up badge sits on a light ground.
- **Gold that is not text (step 25).** Counted at the end of the step: `bg-lca-gold` 155 in 54 files, `border-lca-gold` 78 in 36, `ring-lca-gold` 6 in 5, `border-l-lca-gold` 4 in 3, `divide-lca-gold/20` 1 and `decoration-lca-gold` 1 (`Navbar.tsx` line 44, on navy). None is text, so none moves (see Follow-ups).
- **The State champions link (step 25).** Confirmed: `TournamentsPage.tsx` line 531 is `text-lca-gold hover:underline` in `PageHero`'s subtitle: gold on navy (6.51:1), kept, but set apart from the words around it by colour alone until it is hovered, which axe reports as `link-in-text-block` (see Follow-ups).
- **`GOLD_BUTTON` (step 25).** Confirmed: `src/lib/brand.ts` line 36 named `text-lca-navy`, the same fixed navy as `text-brand-navy`, in no TSX file and so in no scan. Decided: it names `text-brand-navy` now, and `semantic-map-cascade.test.ts` and `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts`, which read the string through `goldButton()`, pass.
- **StatusBadge (step 25).** Confirmed: only its light gold tone's words change (line 35, `bg-lca-gold/20 text-gold-ink-badge`, which `tokens.test.ts` pins); its navy surface's gold (line 28) stays `text-lca-gold` by the allowlist, and its keep entries and allowlist lines for step 26 are untouched.
- **The maps and the ratchet (step 25).** Confirmed: neither earlier map watched a gold class, and the ratchet reads only the maps' keep entries and counts palette, navy, white and hex classes, so `text-lca-gold` on navy is the gold guard's, not the ratchet's. The 23 allowlist entries that named step 25 (34 uses) are gone, leaving 57 uses and 4 hex colours (10 classes in 7 files), and the allowlist's comment no longer speaks of gold. `redesign-status-step24.test.ts` pins the numbers step 24's records give rather than deriving them.
- **Lint, build and the browser runs (step 25).** Measured: lint 13 errors and 13 warnings before and after; the build sizes are under the step; the browser runs under Checks.

### Follow-ups

- **AC9 live check.** After the first push, K opens the branch deploy in the Pages dashboard and confirms its D1 binding shows `lca-db-preview`. Record the result here.
- **First preview migration.** K runs `npm run db:migrate:preview` once before reviewing the first preview; on the new, empty database it applies every migration from `0001`.
- **Preview secrets.** Before the first push, K checks the Preview environment's secrets in the Pages dashboard and removes any live Stripe or Resend key left from before this slice, then sets Stripe test keys before anyone pays on a preview.
- **Club logos.** Previews write to the production `lca-club-logos` bucket. A separate preview bucket would need creating and binding under `[env.preview]`.
- **Logins.** Previews use the production Supabase project, so a sign-in on a preview is a real account.
- **One preview database for every branch.** A migration applied from one branch is there for all of them; only the redesign branch migrates it today.
- **Section move loses the early, member and late adjustments (step 10, from review).** When an entry changes section, its pending payment is set to the new section's regular fee, as before. A player who entered Open at $25 with a $5 early discount (payment $20) and is moved to Reserve at $15 is charged $15, not $10. Kept as it was because acceptance criterion 4 asks for identical charges. Later, work out the change as `priceEntry` for the new section minus `priceEntry` for the old one, using the tiers that applied when the entry was made.
- **Bye rounds are not checked as whole numbers on a single entry (step 10, from review).** `registrations.ts` accepts `byeRounds: [1.5]`, and the waitlist branch saves bye rounds before the bye limits are checked; `batch.ts` does check whole numbers. Make `byeRoundsSchema` `z.array(z.number().int())` in `domain/contracts/registration.ts` and move the bye checks above the capacity check.
- **A section's own early price needs one rule (step 10, from review, for WS08).** `priceEntry` applies a section's `feeEarly` only while `early_deadline` is set and not passed; `tierFees` in `domain/events/sections.ts` reports it with no deadline check. No row has `fee_early` until WS08; decide the rule then and apply it in both. A `feeEarly` above the base would also give a positive line labelled "Early entry discount".
- **Two more "USCF ID" messages (step 10).** Walk-ins done in step 11: both refusals in `walk-ins.ts` say "US Chess ID". `functions/api/uscf/lookup.ts` ("A valid 8-digit USCF ID is required") still says USCF, and `src/pages/TournamentManagePage.tsx` shows a bare ISO date in a tooltip.
- **Ids that can collide (step 10).** Registration ids are `reg-<tournament>-<Date.now() base36>`, so two different players entering in the same millisecond would collide on the primary key. This predates step 10 and is untested.
- **Walk-ins and the waitlist (step 11).** Done in step 11: neither reads the JSON, a walk-in sets its `section_id`, and the waitlist prices with `priceEntry` over the row. `entryPrice` stays an adapter over the JSON for the tournament page and the family entry panel until step 13. `functions/api/admin/tournaments/[id]/manage.ts` still reads the JSON for the manage page's section list (out of this step's scope); the writer and the triggers keep it in step with the live rows.
- **Walk-in rating accepts fractions (step 11, from review).** `walkInRequestSchema` takes any number for `uscfRating`, but `walkInResponseSchema` needs `rating_at_entry` to be a whole number, so a rating of 1500.5 is saved and echoed back in a body that breaks its own contract. The old handler accepted it too. Add `.int()` with the message "Enter the rating as a whole number, or leave it blank." and one 400 case in `walkins-waitlist.test.ts`, and record it as a deviation.
- **Brief text.** Update brief 0.1 rule 8 and the 0.2 line "K applies remote migrations" in the next brief revision.
- **Member discount query.** K runs the production query under Decisions before checkpoint B merges; record the result here. Not run yet (step 15 lists it with the two other things the merge waits for).
- **Production before the merge (step 15). Done.** The member-discount query, the step 8 queries and the legacy-shape queries were run on production on Monday, October 12, 2026; results under Checkpoint B. 0052 and 0053 are applied; `migrate-db.yml` applies 0054 on the merge.
- **Enforcement at checkout (step 15).** WS06's household checkout calls `needsMembershipAtCheckout`, adds the right membership in the same payment, and only then may `membershipRequirementSwitch` be turned on. WS08 replaces `FORCE_LCA_MEMBERSHIP` with the admin-only site setting `membership_required_for_all`, and builds the full setup toggle.
- **New refusals of typed input (checkpoint B review).** A negative section fee or a section name over 80 characters, typed in the wizard or the setup page, is now refused, and the director sees only the generic "Some fields are missing or not valid." These are not on the decided list. Later, show which field is wrong.
- **Pre-submit expiry line (checkpoint B review).** The event page's line before entering still shows the US Chess expiry as "2026-09-01", while the warning after entering shows "Tue, Sep 1, 2026". Show the weekday form in both.
- **Leftover USCF wording (checkpoint B review).** `TournamentDetailPage.tsx` still says "This is a USCF-rated tournament. Add your USCF ID..." in the Register click message and "USCF ID" in the confirmation dialog, and `FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` says "needs a USCF ID". Change them to "US Chess" with matching test text.
- **Production reads before the merge (checkpoint B review).** The legacy-shape queries, the member-discount query and the step 8 queries are listed under Checkpoint B, Before the merge. Not run yet.
- **Stale detach race (step 15, from review). Closed in step 15.** When an admin took the club off an event, the write that forced `requires_lca_membership` to 1 depended on the value read at the start of the request, so a rep's concurrent switch-off could have left an event with no club and the column at 0. `admin/tournaments/[id].ts` now writes 1 whenever the event has no club after the save, whatever the earlier read said.
- **Concurrent edits lose fields (step 15, from testing; predates the step).** `PATCH /api/admin/tournaments/[id]` rewrites most columns from the row it read, so two saves at the same moment can lose the other's change to the name, the club and similar fields while both answer 200. Only `rounds` and `requires_lca_membership` are written when sent. Consider writing only the changed columns, or comparing `updated_at`.
- **Migration header (step 15, from review).** The generated header of `0054_requires_lca_membership.sql` still says to edit the schema and generate again rather than edit the file; the paragraph below it explains the hand-written update. Reword the first sentence when the file is next touched.
- **Clearinghouse dates.** `src/lib/clearinghouse.ts` keeps its own `formatDate` ("Oct 24, 2026", no weekday), used only by `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` for partner events. It should move to `formatDate` from `@domain/format` (with the year option) when the homepage is rebuilt behind `newHome` in WS03, and then be removed.
- **Reading stored timestamps (step 3).** Any later step that shows a `datetime('now')` column (`created_at`, `updated_at`, `sent_at`, `recorded_at` and the like) with `formatDate` or `formatTime` must pass `{ stored: 'utc' }`; the default reads wall-clock text and would show those times 5 to 6 hours early. Reviewers of those steps check for it.
- **One time-zone definition (step 3).** Done in step 4: `domain/format/centralTime.ts` takes `LCA_TIME_ZONE` from `CENTRAL_TIME_ZONE` in `date.ts`. `src/pages/AnnualMeetingPage.tsx` still writes `'America/Chicago'` in its own date formatter; it should use `formatDate` and `formatTime` when that page moves to the heritage look.
- **Minor names on the entrants list (step 4).** `publicName` is ready but unused. WS06 calls it from the public event endpoint once guardian links and the under-18 mark on an entry exist, with `'entrants'` for the pre-event list and `'results'` everywhere else.
- **Name suffixes (step 4, from review).** `publicName` skips Jr., Sr., II, III and IV but not a trailing "V" or "3rd", so "Leo Robichaux V" shows as "Leo V." on the entrants list. The result is shorter, not a leak. Add `v`, `2nd` and `3rd` to the suffix list with a test when `publicName` is wired in WS06.
- **Zero increment or delay (step 3).** `formatTimeControl("G/90+0")` reads "... + 0 sec per move". Minor; drop the clause when the value is 0 when a page first shows time controls.
- **Dynamic imports (step 3).** ESLint's `no-restricted-imports` does not see `import()` or `require()` inside `domain/`; `test/unit/domain-boundaries.test.ts` scans for both.
- **Event-mode phases in WS02 (step 5).** The endpoint computes `lastRoundResultsComplete` (a non-null result in every section's last scheduled round) and passes it with `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(round_schedule, date, end_date)`. It builds the hide key with `phaseKey(id, phase, round)`, passing the first round of the next playing day for `week` and `dayBefore` and the first round of that day for `checkin` and `eventDay`, so a strip hidden before day 1 comes back before round 2. Its `pollSeconds` rule (20 in `checkin`, `eventDay` and the round phases, 300 otherwise), the sooner-first sort and their tests are WS02's. When WS02 or WS07 changes the inputs, only `domain/events/eventMode.ts` and its test change. `eventPhase` can join the one-definition list in `test/unit/domain-shims.test.ts` once WS02 calls it, so a second copy fails the suite.
- **jsdom 30.** Moving to jsdom 30 needs Node 22.22.2 or later in development and in CI (`.github/workflows/ci.yml` asks for `'22'`). Not needed for WS01.
- **drizzle-kit and added foreign keys (step 6).** Until drizzle-kit writes `ON DELETE` for a column added with `ALTER TABLE`, add such columns with `npm run db:generate -- <name> --custom` and write the clause by hand. When drizzle-kit is upgraded, run `npx drizzle-kit generate` with no schema change first; it must still report no changes.
- **Development audit findings (step 6).** drizzle-kit brings older esbuild copies (0.18.20 through `@esbuild-kit`, and 0.25.12), which `npm audit` reports as moderate (the esbuild development server advisory). They run only on a developer's machine and are not in the built site or the functions.
- **`db:generate` arguments (step 6, from review).** Every argument except `--custom` is taken as the migration name, so `--name add_notes` gives `0052_name_add_notes.sql` and `--help` is read as a name. Reject other `--` arguments and print the usage line.
- **Bracketed identifiers (step 6, from testing).** `splitSql` does not treat `[name]` as quoted, so a `;` inside one would split. No migration uses them and drizzle-kit never writes them; the old splitter had the same gap.
- **The import check (step 6).** Done in step 9: the check in `test/unit/schema-drift.test.ts` that no runtime code imports `functions/db` is removed, now that the admin tournament endpoints use `getDb`.
- **Production and the migrations (step 6).** The schema was introspected from the migrations, not from the production database. Anything ever changed in production by hand, outside `migrations/`, is not in it.
- **Endpoints that do not answer with JSON (step 7).** `GET clubs/[id]/logo`, `GET officer-photos/[key]` and `GET post-images/[id]` serve images, `GET admin/members/export` serves CSV text, and `POST stripe/webhook` takes Stripe's event body. They are on the pending list, marked. When each is next changed, decide whether it gets a contract for its error answers only or moves to the excluded list with that reason.
- **Page route tests (step 7).** No test runs `functions/tournaments/[id].ts`, `functions/clubs/[id].ts` or `functions/news/[slug].ts`. A test that checks the link-preview tags for a visible event, a hidden draft and an unknown id belongs with the next change to `functions/utils/linkPreview.ts`.
- **Contracts as endpoints change (step 7).** Each step that changes an endpoint adds its contract to `domain/contracts`, a contract test in `test/integration/contracts/` that uses `contracts['<route>'].<METHOD>`, moves the handler to `parseBody` where it reads a body, takes the line out of `test/unit/contracts-pending.ts` and lowers `PENDING_COUNT`. Request schemas are written from the bodies `src/lib/api.ts` sends.
- **CORS methods (step 7).** `functions/utils/response.ts` allows `GET, POST, PATCH, OPTIONS`, while three handlers take PUT and eighteen take DELETE. Same-origin calls are unaffected; worth correcting when the file is next changed for another reason.
- **Review nits (step 7).** (1) The `expectContract` comment says a loose object is caught for extra fields; it is not, only `z.object` is, so response schemas must be `z.object` or `z.strictObject`. (2) `contract-coverage.test.ts` treats a bare `onRequest` as a catch-all handler; a `_middleware.ts` under `functions/api` would need skipping first. (3) `parseBody` fields use zod's default wording (for example "Invalid input: expected string, received undefined"); request schemas should carry their own plain messages, or `parseBody` should map issue codes, before any form shows them next to inputs.
- **Production queries for step 8.** K runs the read-only queries under step 8 before checkpoint A merges and the four after it, and again before checkpoint B merges; record the answers here.
- **Renames from the setup form (step 13).** Until the wizard and the manage page send each section's id back, a rename there is a removal plus an addition and is refused while the section has entries (decision 7). Done in step 13: the pages keep and send each loaded section's id, and `sections-rename-ui-path.test.ts` shows a rename in that body keeping its entries. Neither screen has a rename box yet (see Deviations, step 13).
- **Games by name.** Games still name their section; the writer moves them on a rename from a live row. A `section_id` on `tournament_games` (WS07) would let the rename skip that step.
- **Report settings past 8000 characters.** A rename that would push `report_settings` past the 8000 characters the PATCH keeps leaves the old key in place rather than cutting the JSON. Nothing that long exists today.
- **Two edits at once.** The writer plans from the rows it reads, then writes in one batch that opens with guards. If another save (or old code writing the JSON) changed the rows in between, or a section being removed gained an entry or a game, the guard or the live-name index fails the batch as a whole and the handler answers 409 with a plain message; the director reloads and saves again. The guard does not cover a change to `report_settings` alone between the read and the batch; the PATCH writes `report_settings` as it was sent or as it read it, as it did before this step.
- **Guard depends on D1's SQLite (step 9, from review).** The stale-plan guard relies on `group_concat` following the order of its ordered subquery, and `isSectionsConflict` recognises the guard by the text `bad JSON path:` (SQLite 3.45 and later wording). Both fail safe, nothing is written, but on a SQLite build that differs every section save would answer 409 (order) or 500 (error text) until the code changes. D1's version could not be read (`sqlite_version()` is blocked). Re-check when D1's engine changes; matching the older "JSON path error" wording is optional.
- **Site code may import `functions/db` (step 9, from review).** Removing the import check from `schema-drift.test.ts` also dropped it for `src/`. A narrowed check that walks `src/` only and expects no import of `functions/db` or `drizzle` should be restored, so the site bundle stays free of Drizzle. `domain/` is covered by `domain-boundaries.test.ts`.
- **Bare-name section pinned to the event fee (step 12, from review).** A section with no fee of its own is answered with `entryFee` set to the event fee; the manage page echoes it, so a later section save stores that fee as the section's own and the section stops following the event's `entry_fee`. Nothing edits the event entry fee after creation today, so no price is wrong yet. Smallest fix in `buildSaveSections`: when the input carries `fees.regular` (an echoed answer), the row's `fee_regular` is null and `entryFee === fees.regular`, keep `fee_regular` null. Step 13 left it (the writer was outside that step's files, and the pages' behaviour is unchanged); owner WS08, before directors can change the event fee.
- **Public responses expose personal and internal fields (step 12, from review; predates the step).** `GET /api/clubs/[id]` returns every officer's email address, and `GET /api/tournaments` returns `keep_apart` (member ids), `created_by` and `report_settings` for every visible event through `t.*`. The new strict contracts now treat these as promised shape. Trim them from the responses and their contracts, and add a role-safety contract case, in a separate task.
- **Admin PATCH heals the mirror from itself (step 12, from testing).** The admin PATCH writes `round_schedule` on every call, which fires the 0053 update trigger and re-reads the JSON mirror into the section rows. A mirror behind the table is therefore overwritten on the next admin PATCH; this matters only if something writes the rows without the mirror. Resolved when the triggers go with the JSON columns. Changed in step 14: an edit that sends neither sections nor round times no longer writes a JSON column, so it no longer fires the trigger or heals the mirror; the sections and schedule saves each write the mirror last.
- **Audit argument match (step 12, from testing).** The reader audit stops its argument match at the first `)`, so `parseJsonArray(await load(id).then((r) => r.sections))` is not flagged. Judged contrived; tighten if a reader like it appears.
- **Manage-page readers (step 11 follow-up).** Done in step 12: `manage.ts` reads the table.
- **Client readers (step 13).** Done in step 13: the pages read the contract-derived section type, and `entryPrice` and `sectionBaseFee` are gone.
- **Schedules in WS06 and WS08 (step 14).** WS06 adds the schedule picker and sets `registrations.schedule_id` (today the trigger sets the primary). WS08's setup builds the second-schedule screen and must send labels when it switches the primary. The default labels read awkwardly in messages ("the “Main schedule” schedule"); drop the trailing word when the label already ends in "schedule" when WS08 shows them to directors.
- **Retiring the JSON columns.** When `tournaments.sections` and `round_schedule` are dropped, the four 0053 triggers go in the same migration.
- **`tournament_games.section_id` (WS07).** Games keep their section names; the archived rows the backfill makes for game names are there for that link.
- **Member discount line (step 15).** Done in step 15: `priceEntry` no longer reads `member_discount`. The column stays; drop it, `memberDiscount` in the edit request and `member_discount` in the contract row in a later migration once production has run without it.
- **`entryPrice` on the pages (step 13).** The tournament page and the family entry panel still price from the legacy JSON through `entryPrice`. Done in step 13: both price with `priceShownSection`, which passes a section's own early or late price on to `priceEntry`.
- **An own early price of $0 under a larger discount (step 13).** An answer's `fees.early` of $0 is either the section's own price or a worked-out one clamped at $0 (the early discount larger than the fee), and `priceShownSection` takes it as worked out. The page then differs from checkout only when the late fee is also due (the early and late windows overlap). No section has its own tier price until WS08; if WS08 lets a director set one, have the answer say which tier prices are the section's own (for example `ownFees`) and drop the comparison. Owner WS08.
- **Prices on a page left open (step 13).** Done in the checkpoint B review: both screens refresh their time every 30 seconds and on the actions that matter (see Deviations, step 13). Possible later polish: also re-read the clock when a tab becomes visible again or a page is restored, and before the family button submits.
- **Admin link opened directly (step 13, seen on the local dev server).** Loading `/admin/tournaments` in a fresh tab as a signed-in admin went to `/dashboard`: `AuthContext` clears `loading` as soon as the stored session is read, and for one render the profile is neither loaded nor loading, so `RoleProtectedRoute` sees the default role. Moving to the same address inside the site worked. Seen with the local sign-in stand-in only; check it with a real sign-in before relying on it, and if it holds, start `memberLoading` true when a session is found.
- **A households folder (step 16).** When the server gains a second household helper, move `functions/utils/family.ts` with it into `functions/utils/households/` using `scripts/move-files.ts` and a new map.
- **Retire the four re-exports (step 16).** `functions/utils/pricing.ts`, `sectionRules.ts`, `regions.ts` and `time.ts` go when the files that import them are next changed for another reason; each importer then imports `domain/` directly.
- **`support.ts` lint error (step 16).** `functions/api/support.ts` line 75 still has `_number` unused (it is there to drop the ticket number from each row). Fix it the next time that file changes for another reason.
- **Lint problems in the map components (step 17).** `LCAMap.tsx` (its exported helpers belong in a module of their own, likely `src/areas/clubs/`), `ClubLocationPicker.tsx`, `viewOnly.tsx` and `HomePage.tsx` keep the lint problems listed under Deviations. Fix them when those files are next changed for another reason; WS09 and WS03 rebuild the club map and the home page. Errors done in step 23: the helpers are in `src/areas/clubs/clubMap.ts`, both refs are updated in effects and `useViewOnly` is in `src/hooks/useViewOnly.ts`. The `exhaustive-deps` warnings in `LCAMap.tsx` and `HomePage.tsx` remain.
- **Retire the site re-exports (step 17).** `src/lib/pricing.ts`, `sectionRules.ts`, `lcaTime.ts`, `regions.ts` and `family.ts` stay while the brief names them; once no page imports them, importers can use `@domain/...` or the area `index.ts` directly. `test/unit/domain-shims.test.ts` goes with them.
- **Importers onto area paths (step 18).** Pages, components and tests import the client from `@/lib/api`. When one is next changed for another reason, it imports from the area's `api.ts` instead; once nothing imports `@/lib/api`, the barrel and `test/unit/fixtures/api-exports.json` go, and `api-exports.test.ts` keeps only the one-module-per-name check.
- **Section headers in the area modules (step 18).** The three headers that moved with the declaration under them (`Contact` in `governance/api.ts`, `Admin API` in `membership/api.ts`, the `Append to src/lib/api.ts` note in `admin/api.ts`) can be removed or reworded the next time those files change.
- **Three fetches the route audit cannot see (step 18; predates the step).** `adminGetTickets` (`admin`), `getGovernanceDocuments` (`governance`) and `getBoardTickets` (`governance`) build their URL in a variable, so route-audit does not check them and the fixture does not list them. Write them as a literal `fetch` when those calls are next changed.
- **Imports across areas (step 19).** Five files import another area's files directly, and the cross-area rule warns on each. Fix one when its file is next changed for another reason, then take it off this list and off `CROSS_AREA_TODAY` in `test/unit/area-boundaries.test.ts`:
  - `src/areas/admin/components/ChampionsPanel.tsx` line 17 imports `parsePasted` from `@/areas/events/champions`. `@/areas/events` already re-exports it, so the fix is the import line.
  - `src/areas/admin/components/PostsPanel.tsx` line 12 imports `RichTextEditor` from `@/areas/governance/components/RichTextEditor`. The editor serves the governance pages and news posts, so it likely becomes a shared piece in `src/components` (WS10 or WS14).
  - `src/areas/admin/components/TournamentWizard.tsx` line 26 imports `SectionRulesEditor` from `@/areas/events/components/SectionRulesEditor`. The wizard creates events, so it likely moves to `src/areas/events/components` when WS08 rebuilds event setup.
  - `src/areas/households/components/FamilyCard.tsx` line 20 imports `USCHESS_RENEW_URL` from `@/areas/membership/components/UsChessMembership`. Since step 23 `usChessState` lives in `src/areas/membership/usChess.ts`, which `src/areas/membership/index.ts` re-exports, and `FamilyCard` takes it from `@/areas/membership` (that cleared the component's `react-refresh` error); moving the constant there too clears this warning (WS06 or WS12).
  - `src/areas/households/components/FamilyRegistrationPanel.tsx` line 17 imports the `GradeConfirm` component from `@/areas/events/components/GradeConfirm`. Since step 24 the three values that are not components (`confirmedRange`, `NO_TICKS` and `GradeTicks`) come from `@/areas/events`, which re-exports `src/areas/events/gradeConfirm.ts`; WS06, which rebuilds family entry, decides where the grade check itself lives, and with it this import.
- **Pages that call fetch (step 19).** Four `fetch` calls in three pages, all to `/api/clearinghouse` (the partner event list), and the page rule warns on each: `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` line 165 since step 23 (`?upcoming=true`, WS03), `src/pages/ScholasticPage.tsx` line 96 (`?upcoming=true`, WS11) and `src/pages/TournamentsPage.tsx` lines 402 and 413 (`?upcoming=true` and `?upcoming=false`, WS04). Each becomes a call in `src/areas/events/api.ts` when its page is rebuilt; then take it off this list and off `PAGE_FETCH_TODAY` in `test/unit/area-boundaries.test.ts`. When both lists are empty, the two rules become errors (see Decisions, "Area boundaries and the lint baseline").
- **Lint errors in the moved components (step 19).** Six errors stay in four components step 19 moved; the ten in `LCAMap.tsx` and `ClubLocationPicker.tsx` are under step 17's follow-up. Fix each when its file is next changed for another reason. Three were fixed in step 23, which rewrote those files' classes, and the last three in step 24:
  - `src/areas/events/components/GradeConfirm.tsx`: 3 `react-refresh/only-export-components` (`NO_TICKS`, `asksGrade` and `confirmedRange` exported beside the component, lines 18, 43 and 49). Moving them into an events module also fixes the `FamilyRegistrationPanel` import above (WS06). Done in step 24: they are in `src/areas/events/gradeConfirm.ts` with `GradeTicks`, which `src/areas/events/index.ts` re-exports, and the component file exports the component alone; the component's own import stays on the list above.
  - `src/areas/membership/components/UsChessMembership.tsx`: 1 `react-refresh/only-export-components` (`usChessState`, line 17). Done in step 23: `usChessState` is in `src/areas/membership/usChess.ts`.
  - `src/areas/admin/components/PostsPanel.tsx`: 1 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` (the effect at line 68 calls `refresh()`, which sets state). Done in step 23: the first load sets the list from the request's callback.
  - `src/areas/live/components/ResultsEntry.tsx`: 1 `react-hooks/use-memo` (line 45, a dependency list holding `withGames.join('|')`). Done in step 23: the joined key is worked out before `useMemo`.
- **Empty keys do not stop the request (step 20).** The a11y servers bind `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` and `RESEND_API_KEY` empty, which refuses the call but does not keep it local: `createCheckoutSession` (`functions/utils/stripe`, used by membership checkout, registrations, `registrations/[id]/pay`, `registrations/batch` and donations checkout), `sendEmail` (`functions/utils/email.ts`) and the bulk campaign send (`functions/utils/admin/campaigns.ts`) still send a request to `api.stripe.com` or `api.resend.com` with `Bearer ` and an empty key, and `page.route` cannot block it because it leaves the worker. A later step makes each return early on an empty key.
- **Hero carousel and the known-violations list (step 20).** The hero advances every 5 seconds, so which dot is current changes between runs; the "Slide 1" dot matched under `.w-5` on one run, so both its selector forms are in `test/a11y/known-violations.json`. The step that owns the hero (WS03, with AC4 in step 29) or the step 32 sweep removes the carousel's auto-advance and prunes the extra selector forms.
- **Muted `StatusBadge` contrast (step 20).** On the fixture page the muted `StatusBadge` ("Registration closed") is 4.34:1 (#737373 on #f5f5f5) and fails axe; it comes from the existing light muted tone. The smoke spec does not load the fixture page, so it is not in the known list. Fix it in step 26 or the step 32 sweep.
- **README wording (step 20; predates the step).** `README.md` has em dashes and "USCF" lines from before this step; the step left them as they were. Reword them when the README is next changed for another reason.
- **First CI run of the `a11y` job (step 20).** Watch the first pull request: CI installs a newer Chromium than this container's, and the baseline's tolerance of 2 per colour channel may or may not absorb a difference. If the baseline fails there with no site change, record it again from CI's browser.
- **Remove `test.fixme` (step 20).** When WS06 ships the household checkout and WS07 ships round publishing, the matching spec in `test/e2e` drops `test.fixme` and `.github/workflows/e2e-preview.yml` is started by hand against a branch preview, with the preview database migrated and seeded and Stripe test keys set.
- **Dark mode on pages not converted yet (step 21).** With `newLook` on, dark follows the device, while most pages still spell out their colours (`bg-white`, `text-lca-navy` and the like). Steps 23 and 24 move them to the tokens before checkpoint D merges; until then `newLook` stays off. Step 23 moved the navy text, white grounds and navy bands; step 24 has the status colours, the grey and slate classes and raw hex. Done in step 24, but for gold text (step 25) and StatusBadge's tones (step 26); `newLook` stays off until those land too. Done in step 25 for gold text: every gold text class is on a navy ground or a gold token, and the dark spot run finds no colour-contrast failure on its three pages; StatusBadge's tones are step 26's, and `newLook` stays off until they land.
- **The browser bar colour when the choice differs from the device (step 21).** The two `theme-color` metas follow the device setting, so a visitor who picks Light on a dark device keeps the dark bar colour. Set the meta from the choice when WS02 rebuilds the header.
- **Dark grounds that are not a navy band (step 21).** Under the new look the ring turns gold only inside `bg-lca-navy` or `data-ground="dark"`. Three places have a dark ground without either, so their controls get the navy ring at well under 3:1: the homepage hero in `src/pages/HomePage.tsx` ("Find a tournament", "Join LCA", Previous slide, Next slide and the Slide dots; the navy is a sibling tint over the photo, not an ancestor of the buttons), and the `urgent` (`#9b1c1c`) and `info` (`#1e4d7b`) tones of `src/components/AnnouncementBanner.tsx` (the link and the dismiss button). Whichever step first draws each of them under the new look (step 23 or 24 converting the page, or step 29 for the hero) puts `data-ground="dark"` on the element that holds the controls; `newLook` is not turned on until then. Done in step 23 for all three, and for the two club heroes drawn with an inline navy (`ClubDetailPage`, `AdminClubPage`); `tokens.spec.ts` measures the hero and a club hero. Step 29's still hero keeps the marker on the element that holds its controls.
- **A light card inside a navy band (step 21).** The ring is gold inside any `bg-lca-navy` element, so a light card placed inside one would get a gold ring on a light ground. None was found on the pages checked; WS02's header and footer replace the rule with their own tokens. Step 23 found one, drawn by another component: the menu the state filter opens in the `/tournaments` hero (`FilterDropdown`, drawn inside `PageHero`; the menu is `bg-popover`, white in the light look), whose options took the gold ring on white at 2.28:1 with `newLook` on, as they had with `.bg-lca-navy` since step 21. A rule after the gold one now gives the controls of a `bg-popover` panel inside a navy band or a `data-ground="dark"` element the look's own ring back, and `tokens.spec.ts` measures it in light and dark mode. `semantic-map-grounds.test.ts` follows each component to the places it is drawn and fails on any other light card that holds a control inside a navy band unless that rule names its ground. No other was found; the tournament hero's "Not yet published" note is a light box but holds no control.
- **Tokens with no reader yet (step 21).** `StatusBadge` moves to the status utilities in step 26; the scholastic hues and the live tokens are read by WS11 and WS07; `font-accent` waits for its first heading. Since step 24 the two Open pills read `text-status-open` and `bg-status-open-tint`.
- **Dark, live and heritage under axe (step 21).** The smoke spec runs axe on `/` in the light look only; the sweep of step 32 covers the other looks.
- **Dedup tests in the edges file cannot fail (step 22).** A mocked module is cached after its first import, so a second `import()` of `live.css` never runs the mock again. `theme-scope.test.tsx` now clears the module registry after the first live import and keeps its copy of ThemeScope, so its "a second live scope does not import again" check fails if the guard in `ThemeScope.tsx` is removed. The same claims in `theme-scope-edges.test.tsx` (a scope that turns live again, three live scopes at once, a third scope after a retry) still pass without the guard; give them the same reset or drop them. The guard is also redundant with the browser's module cache.
- **ThemeScope as a page's main (step 22).** `src/App.tsx` already wraps every route in `<main>`, so a scoped main would nest a second landmark and axe would report it. The header comment in `ThemeScope.tsx` now says to put the scope on a `div` or `section` inside App's main, and why. `as` still accepts `main`; drop it when step 30 builds `HeritageLayout`.
- **Base inside heritage in a browser (step 22).** The fixture page has no nested base-in-heritage container, so that nesting is tested only in jsdom. Add one when step 30 or 31 renders a heritage page.
- **Binding the live fonts (step 22).** `live.css` loads Barlow Condensed and Barlow but nothing sets text in them. WS07 binds them, and the AC8 and heritage layout-shift checks are repeated there on real pages.
- **AC11 and AC12 second halves (step 22).** The heritage font chunk, the layout shift on `/about` and `/governance/bylaws`, `HeritageLayout` and the editor and governance-form render test are steps 30 and 31.
- **K reviews the step 23 judgement calls (step 23).** The brief asks for a person to review every place where a mapped class sits on a coloured ground. The review recorded under step 23 was made from the TypeScript parser's lists and the script's dry run, and at first it missed the light menu in the `/tournaments` hero (see "A light card inside a navy band"). Before `newLook` is turned on, K reads the judgement calls under step 23 and checks the pages they name with the new look, in light and dark mode.
- **Navy borders and rings (step 23).** `border-lca-navy` (73 in 23 files) and `ring-lca-navy` (1, `AdminAnnouncementPanel`) are in no map, so in dark mode they draw navy lines on a dark ground. The 33 opaque ones sit with a selected navy fill (`border-lca-navy bg-brand-navy text-white`) and should follow it (`border-brand-navy`); the hover and tint borders (`/10` to `/50`) sit on light grounds and should become `border-ink` at the same opacity; the impersonation banner's Exit border sits on gold and stays navy. Each pair resolves to the same colour on the plain `:root`, so step 24's map can take them with a review of each, like this step's; a tinted border moved to the ink needs the same fallback care as the tints beside it (next item). Done in step 24: 27 opaque lines beside a navy fill are `border-brand-navy`, the other 45 `border-ink` at their opacity, the Exit button's line on gold `border-brand-navy/30`, and the ring `ring-ink`; the tinted lines have their fallback in the block for browsers that cannot mix colours.
- **Navy tints in dark mode (step 23).** The 17 faint navy tints (`bg-brand-navy/5` to `/10` and `/[0.03]` to `/[0.06]`: section chips, avatars and officer initials, selected rows and options, support replies, "Your registration" and the view-only note) stay the brand navy, so in dark mode they barely show on the dark ground; the text on them is `text-ink` and stays readable. The same tint of the ink would lighten the ground instead, but Tailwind gives a token class with an opacity modifier only its token at full strength as the fallback for browsers that cannot mix colours (Safari 15.4 to 16.1, Chrome 109), which with `newLook` off would turn each tint into a solid navy box behind navy text. Step 24 moves the tints and the tinted navy borders to the ink together, with a fallback of their own: an `@supports not (color: color-mix(in lab, red, red))` block that gives each tint in use the sRGB tint its brand class falls back to, or tint tokens whose plain `:root` value is that tint. Done in step 24: the 17 tints are `bg-ink` at their opacity, and the `@supports` block gives each the tint `bg-brand-navy` fell back to; the hero photo's veil, the navy buttons' hover and the Exit button's hover on gold stay navy.
- **Classes only docs and tests name reach the CSS (step 23).** Tailwind reads every file in the repository for class names (`src/index.css` sets no `@source`), so a class that only a document, a test or a script names is built into the site's CSS though no page uses it: 733 bytes of rules at step 22 and 1,629 after step 23, mostly the old class names that the codemod's fixtures and map must spell out, and classes these records name. The step 23 tests join the classes they only plant at run time. An `@source not` for `docs/`, `test/`, `scripts/` and the records at the root would end it, once a check shows that no page builds a class name only those files spell out; for step 32's sweep, or the next step that changes `src/index.css`. Step 24 changes it but leaves `@source not` to step 32, because the brief asks it to report the size and the check needs every rule the build would drop compared. By step 23's count (the site built without `docs/`, `test/` and `scripts/`) such rules were 1,673 bytes at `4e4ad3a` and are 5,333 after step 24, whose map and tests spell out the old status, grey, navy and hex classes; with the Markdown records at the root left out as well, 3,665 and 8,140. Step 25's tests and script put together the class names they plant when they run, so they add no rule; the gold map and step 24's records keep the old gold hex classes' rules in the CSS.
- **Selected navy controls in dark mode (step 23).** Filter pills, tabs and step buttons show their selected state as a navy fill with white text. In dark mode that fill (`#1a2744`) sits close to an unselected control on the dark surface (`#111a2c`, with light text), so the state is hard to see, though every control keeps its words and, where it has one, `aria-pressed`. The Looks-3 tile draws a selected chip as a gold tint with a dark gold border; `FilterChip` (step 26) and the workstreams that rebuild these controls take that style, and the step 32 sweep checks the rest.
- **Gold grounds in dark mode (step 23).** Inside an opaque gold box (the impersonation banner's Exit button, the gold announcement tone's link and dismiss button), the dark-mode focus ring is gold on gold. A rule like the navy band's (`.bg-lca-gold :focus-visible` with the brand navy) or a marker would give them a navy ring; the step that rebuilds the banners (WS02) or the step 32 sweep.
- **Light islands that stay light in dark mode (step 23).** Two fixed light tints hold text that is now `text-ink`: the member strip on `/membership` (`bg-emerald-50`, "View my profile") and the finished-event panel on the manage page (`border-emerald-200 bg-emerald-50`). Step 24 moves those tints to status tokens, which turn dark in dark mode; if it keeps either light, that text needs `text-brand-navy` instead. Until then they are light text on a light ground in dark mode, which nobody sees because `newLook` is off. Done in step 24: both tints are `bg-ok-50`, the ok tint, which turns dark in dark mode, so the `text-ink` on them stays readable.
- **Navy text on the tournament hero's gold tint (step 23; older than the step).** On the tournament page, "See pairings / results" is navy text on a 15 per cent gold tint over the navy hero, about 1.3:1 in today's look (no axe run covers that page). It is now `text-ink`, readable in dark mode and unchanged in light; the light look needs light text there (WS05's event page, or the step 32 sweep).
- **Club colours in dark mode (step 23).** Club colours are user data and stay inline styles. In dark mode a dark club colour used as text (the club initials on `/clubs`, a link on a club page) falls below 3:1 on its tinted dark ground. WS09, which rebuilds the club pages, decides how a club colour shows on a dark ground.
- **The a11y servers share one database (step 23).** Once, `seeded-data.spec.ts` "a write with no token is refused as well" got 500 from `POST /api/admin/members` while the local server logged `SQLITE_BUSY`; the two servers share `.wrangler/a11y`, and earlier runs' logs show the same lock. The next run passed. A retry on that test, or a database folder per server, would end it.
- **`GOLD_BUTTON` keeps `text-lca-navy` (step 23).** `src/lib/brand.ts` is not a TSX file, so its gold button string still names `text-lca-navy` (the same fixed navy as `text-brand-navy`). It moves with the gold work of step 25 or when the file is next changed. Done in step 25: `GOLD_BUTTON` names `text-brand-navy`, the same navy, and no `lca-navy` text class is left in `src`.
- **Quoted strings in comment prose (step 23).** The script searches a comment only for its quoted strings (commented-out code), but it takes whatever lies between two matching quote marks on one line as one. So in a comment line with two apostrophes, such as "We don't want text-lca-navy here, it's dark mode's job.", the class between them is rewritten, which the script's header says never happens. This tree has no such line: the seven comment rewrites are commented-out JSX in `NewsPage.tsx` and `AnnualMeetingPage.tsx`. Step 24 runs the same script with a second map, so before that run the quote match should open and close a string only where no letter or digit stands on the outer side of the quote mark, with a test in `test/unit/semantic-colours-edges.test.ts` that puts a watched class between "don't" and "it's" in a comment and expects no rewrite. Done in step 24, with that test.
- **The filter menu clips its options' ring (step 23).** The menu `FilterDropdown` opens scrolls (`max-h-72 overflow-y-auto`) and each option fills its width, so the menu's own edges hide part of an option's focus outline, on any ground. Its one use is the state filter on `/tournaments`; WS04, which rebuilds the tournament filters, or the step 32 sweep gives the options a ring drawn inside them or gives the menu room for it.
- **Post dates without a weekday (step 23; older than the step).** `formatPostDate` in `src/lib/posts.ts` writes "Oct 1, 2026", with no weekday, on the admin posts list, `/news` and the news post page. It should give way to `formatDate` from `@domain/format` with the year option ("Thu, Oct 1, 2026") when WS10 rebuilds news, and then be removed.


- **K reviews the step 24 judgement calls (step 24).** As with step 23's: before `newLook` is turned on, K reads the judgement calls under step 24 (categories drawn in a tone's colour, the light tones kept on navy, the scanner's marks, the check-in toggle's fill, the kept greys and dots, the kept hex colours) and checks the pages they name with the new look, in light and dark mode.
- **Gold hex text is step 25's, every use of it (step 24).** The allowlist leaves 34 gold text classes in 20 files to step 25: `text-[#7a5c00]` 28 times (once as a hover), `text-[#f0d07a]` 5 and `text-[#8a6d1f]` 1, each entry's reason naming step 25. Step 25's plan names only StatusBadge's and PageHero's strings; it takes every allowlist entry whose reason names step 25, and with them the one colour-contrast failure the dark spot run still finds, the LCA pill on `/tournaments` (`text-[#7a5c00]` on its gold tint, 1.84:1 in dark mode). `text-[#7a5c00]` is exactly `--status-soon` on the plain `:root`; no token has `#f0d07a` or `#8a6d1f` as its plain value yet. Done in step 25: all 34 are gold tokens (`text-gold-ink-badge`, `text-gold-on-dark-badge` and `text-gold-ink-badge-club`, each at its hex on the plain `:root`), moved by the third map, their 23 entries have left the allowlist, and the LCA pill reads 5.08:1 in dark mode.
- **StatusBadge's tones (step 24).** Its 13 tone classes and StatusDot's open dot are keep entries of the status map, and `dark-spot.spec.ts` credits its light tones to step 26. When step 26 maps the status prop to the status tokens, it takes the light tones too or keeps them with a reason, the keep entries for the file and its lines in `test/unit/fixtures/raw-colours-allowlist.json` go, and the ratchet checks the file like any other.
- **Heritage on a muted panel (step 24).** Heritage restyles grounds and gold-ink only, so its words are the base look's: `--ok` `#047857`, `--bad` `#c0362c` and the Facebook link `#1668d8` read 4.33:1, 4.36:1 and 4.14:1 on the inset `#ece4d0`, though they reach 4.5:1 on its paper and card. No file a heritage page loads puts them on a muted panel today, which `contrast.test.ts` checks. Settling it, with a darker ok and bad for heritage or a lighter inset, is K's call before step 31 turns the look on; once it is settled, the test that pins the gap fails on purpose, and its heritage rows can then check every file.
- **The check-in toggle with `newLook` off (step 24).** The checked toggle's white word Present on emerald-600 is 3.67:1 in today's look, below 4.5:1, and step 24 keeps it, since nothing may change with `newLook` off; under the new look `--ok-fill` is `#047857`, 5.48:1. WS08, which rebuilds check-in, fixes today's look too, unless a step does so sooner.
- **What printing leaves (step 24).** A printed page takes the light look. What is left: on the screen in dark mode the print page keeps the black rule under each heading (1.12:1 on `#0b1220`) and its light grey row rules, both drawn for paper; the `dark:` utilities (nine in `button.tsx`, and `dark:text-white` on the three support Buttons) still apply when a dark device prints, on controls no print page shows; and a printed live page takes the base light look until WS07's print kit decides how live pages print.
- **Categories drawn in a tone's colour (step 24).** The audit log's entry colours and the support ticket badges are categories, not statuses. They took the tone of their colour, so under the new look blue and purple both show the partner tag's navy, and every badge keeps its words. WS08 and WS13, which rebuild those screens, decide whether a category gets a look of its own.
- **The club map's loading note in today's look (step 24).** With `newLook` off the note keeps today's muted words, 4.12:1 on the map's paper and 4.18:1 under the clubs map's veil, below 4.5:1; nothing may change with `newLook` off, so it stays. WS09, which rebuilds the club map, fixes today's look too, unless a step does so sooner.
- **Two copies of the rule reader in the tests (step 24).** `test/unit/helpers/tailwindRules.ts` reads a compiled stylesheet as rules with their selectors and the at-rules around them, for `semantic-map.test.ts`, and `test/unit/semantic-map-cascade.test.ts` keeps its own copy of the same reader (`leafRules`, and `escapeClass` beside it) for the step 23 map. A fix to one, such as a new nesting form Tailwind writes, would leave the other reading rules differently. The cascade test should import the helper's, the next time either file changes.
- **K reviews the step 25 judgement calls (step 25).** As with steps 23 and 24: before `newLook` is turned on, K reads the judgement calls under step 25 (the gold icons in gold-ink, the badges' words in a token of their own, the pill's light gold kept, the club badge's own shade, a club's colour as words, the pin's word on `/news`) and checks the pages they name with the new look, in light and dark mode. K also reviews every line the two runs changed, which no person has reviewed yet: 116 lines in 42 TSX files, one class to a line (82 by `scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts` and 34 by the gold map; the News page's pin line was then changed by hand), and the hand changes in `NewsPage.tsx`, `ClubDetailPage.tsx`, `ClubsPage.tsx` and `AdminClubPage.tsx`. The review tables were written outside the repository, so they are made again from step 24's tree: `git archive 3d80a0e src | tar -x -C <folder>` copies it, `node --no-warnings --experimental-strip-types scripts/codemods/gold-ink.ts --dry-run --root <folder> --table <file>` lists every use of `text-lca-gold` with its ground, what decided it and what the run did, and `node --no-warnings --experimental-strip-types scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.ts --dry-run --map scripts/codemods/semantic-colours.gold.map.json --root <folder>` lists the 34 gold hex classes the map moved.
- **The State champions link (step 25; older than the step).** On `/tournaments` the gold "State champions" link in the hero's subtitle is set apart from the words around it by its colour alone until it is hovered, which axe reports as `link-in-text-block` in both looks. It stays gold on navy (6.51:1); it needs an underline or another mark that is not colour, from WS04's list or the step 32 sweep.
- **Gold that is not text (step 25).** Gold fills, lines and rings stay brand gold: `bg-lca-gold` 155 in 54 files, `border-lca-gold` 78 in 36, `ring-lca-gold` 6 in 5, `border-l-lca-gold` 4 in 3, `divide-lca-gold/20` 1 and `decoration-lca-gold` 1. Two of the rings are focus rings in brand gold on a light ground (the club card on `/clubs`, `ClubsPage.tsx` line 126, and the scanner's photo button, `ScannerPage.tsx` line 869), 2.28:1 in today's look, which the new look's shared outline replaces; `MiniCalendar.tsx` line 77 rings today's date in brand gold on a light ground, a mark beside the date's own number. WS04, WS09 and the step 32 sweep take them.
- **The club page's Upcoming badge in today's look (step 25).** With `newLook` off its words read 4.38:1 on their tint, below 4.5:1, and step 25 keeps that, since nothing may change with `newLook` off; under the new look they read 6.13:1. WS09, which rebuilds the club page, fixes today's look too, unless a step does so sooner.
- **The pin in the admin list of posts (step 25).** It keeps its icon and its label, with no word beside it; the post's editor says "Pin to the top of the News page" in words. WS10, which rebuilds news and its admin, decides whether the list shows the word.
- **A club's own colour as words (step 25).** `clubTextColor` gives brand gold the gold-ink token; a colour a club picks is drawn as given, and a light one (a yellow, a pale blue) reads below 4.5:1 on white, as a dark one does in dark mode (step 23's "Club colours in dark mode"). WS09 decides how a club's colour shows as words.
- **WS02's gold (step 25).** `WS02_GOLD` in `test/unit/fixtures/goldAllowlist.ts` names the About menu's "You are here" label (`#705718`), the phone sheet's label (`#866a1e` at 13px on its `#fbfaf6` row) and the tab bar's current-tab bar (`#866a1e`) by the files WS02's Frontend list gives, and `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` checks each against its context once its file exists. WS02 keeps each to its context, or draws it with a token and takes its entry out.
- **What the gold guard's reading leaves out (step 25).** `test/unit/goldText.test.ts` checks a badge in an object of strings through the code its `GOLD_BADGE_ALLOWLIST` entry names as its reader (`meta.className` and the like), so an element that reads the same string through other code is not placed; it takes the ground of a component's branch from a test of `on` against `'navy'` or `'light'`, so a component that branches on its ground another way is read as any other; and it does not follow a colour passed through spread props where a component is drawn, or one a function works out. StatusBadge's light gold entry names the badge's own span as its reader, which sits on a light ground because every caller passes `on="light"`; step 26, which reworks StatusBadge, keeps that entry true or takes it out. A later step that meets one of these teaches the reading, or adds an entry with its reason.

## WS02: Information architecture and navigation

**Status:** not started. **Branch:** none. **PR:** none.

- Read the brief with this path: the event-mode phase machine is `domain/events/eventMode.ts` (built in WS01, step 5), not `functions/utils/eventMode.ts`. `functions/api/me/event-mode.ts` imports `eventPhase`, `phaseKey` and `firstRoundTimesFromSchedule(schedule, date, endDate)` from it (`../../../domain/events/eventMode` from the server, `@domain/events/eventMode` in the site). The endpoint passes a round to `phaseKey` for `week` and `dayBefore` (the first round of the next playing day, 1 before day 1) and for `checkin` and `eventDay` (the first round of that day), because those phases come back on later days of a multi-day event and a key ending in `:0` would keep a hidden strip hidden. The endpoint, `src/lib/eventModeCopy.ts`, the strip, `pollSeconds` and the sooner-first sort are still WS02's. See the WS01 deviations "Event-mode phases (step 5)" and "Event-mode phase times (step 5)" (`eventDay` runs until 6:00 AM, not 11:59 PM) and the decision "Event-mode phase gaps (step 5)" (including days with no round).
